     1	/**
     2	 * Listings verification runner — W1 (composes engine + adapters + detectors).
     3	 *
     4	 * Adds the whole-copy COMPLETENESS SWEEP no single claim can express:
     5	 * - unresolved rows → deterministic entity resolution (exact expected-title
     6	 *   match, unique) decides ID-MISMATCH (identity) vs GHOST (existence);
     7	 * - SOR rows never claimed → MISSING (existence), expressed as the copy's
     8	 *   implicit claim-of-omission (a constructed `existence:false` claim, so even
     9	 *   absence carries C2 receipts).
    10	 *
    11	 * Plain: after checking every statement the copy makes, we also check what it
    12	 * DOESN'T say — items it invented, items it renamed, items it silently dropped.
    13	 */
    14	import type { Claim, Finding, MatchingMode, VerifierReport } from "../../verifier-core/index.ts";
    15	import { makeFinding } from "../../verifier-core/guard.ts";
    16	import { buildReport, verifyClaims } from "../../verifier-core/verify.ts";
    17	import { listingsDetectors } from "./detectors.ts";
    18	import { expectedTitle, indexCatalog, sorReference } from "./reference.ts";
    19	import type { SyntheticCatalog } from "./types.ts";
    20	import { UCP_PINNED_VERSION } from "./ucp.ts";
    21	
    22	/** The pinned spec/taxonomy line stamped into every W1 report header (C10). */
    23	export const LISTINGS_SPEC_VERSION = `taxonomy-v1+acp-extract-2026-07-02+ucp-pin-${UCP_PINNED_VERSION}`;
    24	
    25	function completenessSweep(
    26	  claims: readonly Claim[],
    27	  sor: SyntheticCatalog,
    28	): readonly Finding[] {
    29	  const { byVariationId, byExpectedTitle } = indexCatalog(sor);
    30	  const findings: Finding[] = [];
    31	  const source = claims[0]?.source ?? "acp-feed";
    32	
    33	  // Group claims by serving row (skip catalog-level meta claims).
    34	  const rows = new Map<string, Map<string, unknown>>();
    35	  for (const c of claims) {
    36	    const rowId = c.id.split("#")[0];
    37	    if (rowId === "catalog") continue;
    38	    const fields = rows.get(rowId) ?? new Map<string, unknown>();
    39	    fields.set(c.field, c.value);
    40	    rows.set(rowId, fields);
    41	  }
    42	
    43	  const resolvedTruthIds = new Set<string>();
    44	  for (const [rowId, fields] of rows) {
    45	    if (byVariationId.has(rowId)) {
    46	      resolvedTruthIds.add(rowId);
    47	      continue;
    48	    }
    49	    // Unresolved row: entity resolution by exact expected-title match.
    50	    const title = String(fields.get("title") ?? "");
    51	    const titleMatches = byExpectedTitle.get(title) ?? [];
    52	    const existenceClaim: Claim = {
    53	      id: `${rowId}#existence`,
    54	      source,
    55	      field: "existence",
    56	      value: true,
    57	    };
    58	    if (titleMatches.length === 1) {
    59	      const truth = titleMatches[0];
    60	      resolvedTruthIds.add(truth.variation.id);
    61	      findings.push(
    62	        makeFinding({
    63	          claim: existenceClaim,
    64	          referenceRowId: truth.variation.id,
    65	          ruleId: "LST-IDENT-ID-MISMATCH",
    66	          severity: "error",
    67	          category: "identity",
    68	          plainLine: `The copy keys this row as "${rowId}" but the catalog row is "${truth.variation.id}" — same item, mismatched identity (resolved by exact name).`,
    69	        }),
    70	      );
    71	    } else {
    72	      findings.push(
    73	        makeFinding({
    74	          claim: existenceClaim,
    75	          // A ghost has no truth row; the reference row cited is the catalog
    76	          // itself — the row set it was (absently) checked against.
    77	          referenceRowId: "catalog-meta",
    78	          ruleId: "LST-EXIST-GHOST",
    79	          severity: "error",
    80	          category: "existence",
    81	          plainLine: `The copy serves "${title || rowId}" but no such item exists in the merchant's catalog.`,
    82	        }),
    83	      );
    84	    }
    85	  }
    86	
    87	  // Missing: non-hidden SOR variations never claimed by the copy.
    88	  for (const item of sor.items) {
    89	    for (const variation of item.variations) {
    90	      if (variation.stock === "hidden") continue;
    91	      if (resolvedTruthIds.has(variation.id)) continue;
    92	      findings.push(
    93	        makeFinding({
    94	          claim: {
    95	            id: `${variation.id}#existence`,
    96	            source,
    97	            field: "existence",
    98	            value: false, // the copy's implicit claim: "this item is not listed"
    99	          },
   100	          referenceRowId: variation.id,
   101	          ruleId: "LST-EXIST-MISSING",
   102	          severity: "error",
   103	          category: "existence",
   104	          plainLine: `"${expectedTitle(item, variation)}" exists in the merchant's catalog but is missing from the feed.`,
   105	        }),
   106	      );
   107	    }
   108	  }
   109	
   110	  return findings;
   111	}
   112	
   113	/**
   114	 * Who the data belonged to on THIS run — the two C3/C10 honesty labels a
   115	 * caller may set, and nothing else.
   116	 *
   117	 * Both were hardcoded until 2026-07-27, which was true of every caller then in
   118	 * existence (all fed the committed synthetic catalog) and becomes false the
   119	 * moment a reader supplies their own records: stamping a reader's real catalog
   120	 * `simulated: true`, and asserting shared-synthetic-ID matching that never
   121	 * happened, is a claim broader than the thing backing it (RULES §4).
   122	 *
   123	 * `specVersion` is deliberately NOT here. A caller may describe WHOSE data it
   124	 * ran on; it may never restate WHICH RULES ran — that stays pinned to
   125	 * {@link LISTINGS_SPEC_VERSION} by the engine (C10), proven by the T3 tooth in
   126	 * evals/packs/listings-provenance-labels.test.ts.
   127	 */
   128	export interface ListingsRunProvenance {
   129	  /** C3: shared synthetic IDs, or real-world entity resolution. */
   130	  readonly matchingMode?: MatchingMode;
   131	  /** C10: true when any synthetic artifact is involved in this run. */
   132	  readonly simulated?: boolean;
   133	}
   134	
   135	/**
   136	 * Run the full listings verification for one surface's claims.
   137	 *
   138	 * `provenance` is optional and defaults to the committed-corpus values, so
   139	 * every pre-existing two-argument call site produces a byte-identical report
   140	 * (the golden + drift-lock suites are the proof, and they were not touched).
   141	 * The labels describe the run; they never steer it — identical inputs yield
   142	 * identical findings under either label (T4 tooth), because the engine reads
   143	 * only `sor.items` and `sor.asOf` from a catalog.
   144	 */
   145	export function runListingsVerification(
   146	  claims: readonly Claim[],
   147	  sor: SyntheticCatalog,
   148	  provenance: ListingsRunProvenance = {},
   149	): VerifierReport {
   150	  const reference = sorReference(sor);
   151	  const detected = verifyClaims(claims, reference, listingsDetectors);
   152	  const completeness = completenessSweep(claims, sor);
   153	  return buildReport([...detected, ...completeness], {
   154	    specVersion: LISTINGS_SPEC_VERSION,
   155	    matchingMode: provenance.matchingMode ?? "synthetic-controlled",
   156	    simulated: provenance.simulated ?? true,
   157	  });
   158	}
     1	/**
     2	 * Playground seam — the REAL verifier, browser-side (owner commission 2026-07-13).
     3	 *
     4	 * This module is the entire bridge between the /playground page and the shipped
     5	 * engine: it composes exactly the two calls the CLI's TRUTH leg composes
     6	 * (`acpFeedToClaims` → `runListingsVerification`, see lib/packs/listings/cli.ts
     7	 * runCheck) against the committed synthetic SOR catalog. Nothing is re-implemented
     8	 * and nothing is mocked: the golden-equality test in
     9	 * evals/packs/playground-golden.test.ts proves this seam, fed the committed
    10	 * drifted sample feed, reproduces fixtures/synthetic-restaurant/expected-report.acp.json
    11	 * byte-for-byte — the same equality the page claims on its face.
    12	 *
    13	 * Browser safety: every transitive import in this closure is pure TypeScript —
    14	 * no node:fs, no network. The same test walks the import graph and asserts it.
    15	 *
    16	 * Honesty by construction: the reference is an illustrative catalog, so every
    17	 * report this seam produces carries `matchingMode: "synthetic-controlled"` — a
    18	 * pasted feed is genuinely verified, live, but always against the committed
    19	 * illustrative merchant records.
    20	 *
    21	 * Client-bundle de-jargon (reconcile 2026-07-14): this CLIENT module imports the
    22	 * JARGON-STRIPPED browser PROJECTIONS of the catalog + sample feed (generated by
    23	 * scripts-ts/generate-browser-fixtures.mts from the canonical fixtures), so lab
    24	 * labels never ship in the JS chunks. The projections copy every engine-read /
    25	 * claim field verbatim, so the report stays byte-identical to the golden (proven
    26	 * by evals/packs/playground-golden.test.ts + browser-fixtures.test.ts).
    27	 */
    28	import type { AcpFeed } from "@/lib/packs/listings/acp-feed";
    29	import { acpFeedToClaims } from "@/lib/packs/listings/adapters";
    30	import { runListingsVerification } from "@/lib/packs/listings/run";
    31	import type { SorItem, SorVariation, SyntheticCatalog } from "@/lib/packs/listings/types";
    32	import type { VerifierReport } from "@/lib/verifier-core/report";
    33	import sorCatalogJson from "@/fixtures/synthetic-restaurant/browser/sor.catalog.web.json";
    34	import sampleFeedJson from "@/fixtures/synthetic-restaurant/browser/acp-feed.web.json";

    36	/**
    37	 * The browser projections drop the top-level provenance metadata (simulated /
    38	 * generator / merchantName) so no lab-label ships in the JS bundle — but the
    39	 * TYPES require those fields, and they are all still TRUE of this data. They are
    40	 * reconstructed here with honest values instead of casting through `unknown`
    41	 * (phase-F batch finding #10): the engine never reads them (the golden-equality
    42	 * suite proves the computed report stays byte-identical either way).

    44	
    45	/** The committed synthetic merchant records every playground run is checked against. */
    46	export const SOR_CATALOG: SyntheticCatalog = {
    47	  simulated: true,
    48	  generator: { name: "browser projection of the committed fixture", seed: 0, version: "web" },
    49	  merchantName: "Illustrative merchant",
    50	  ...(sorCatalogJson as Pick<SyntheticCatalog, "currency" | "asOf" | "items">),
    51	};

    53	/** The committed drifted sample feed (the demo's feed) — the "load sample" source. */

    56	  ...(sampleFeedJson as Pick<AcpFeed, "spec" | "items">),
    57	};

    64	/**
    65	 * Pretty-printed sample CATALOG text — the record side of the pair (owner

    67	 * of BOTH files: a reader who has never seen the catalog shape cannot author
    68	 * one, and guessing at it produces parse refusals that read as the tool being
    69	 * broken. Loading this beside the sample feed is also the fastest way to see a
    70	 * PASS, since the pair is internally consistent apart from the planted drift.

    72	export function catalogSampleText(): string {
    73	  return JSON.stringify(SOR_CATALOG, null, 2);

    75	
    76	/**
    77	 * Display-layer copy cleanup for rendered finding text (matches /report + /demo).
    78	 * The illustrative feed's ghost item carries a lab-label inside its own title —
    79	 * a genuine claim value the frozen golden quotes verbatim — so rendered receipts
    80	 * reword it to the site's plain example voice. Serialization (the golden test) is
    81	 * unaffected; this only touches what a reader sees.
    82	 */
    83	export function cleanFinding(s: string): string {
    84	  return s.replace(/\bsimulated\b/gi, "example").replace(/\bsynthetic\b/gi, "illustrative");
    85	}
    86	
    87	export type ParseResult =
    88	  | { readonly ok: true; readonly feed: AcpFeed }
    89	  | { readonly ok: false; readonly error: string };
    90	
    91	/**
    92	 * Parse pasted text as an ACP feed — honest, specific failures; never a fake
    93	 * verdict. Mirrors the CLI's shape check (cli.ts checks `Array.isArray(items)`
    94	 * on the SOR; the feed-side equivalent here).
    95	 */
    96	export function parseAcpFeedText(text: string): ParseResult {
    97	  if (!text.trim()) {
    98	    return { ok: false, error: "The paste box is empty — paste an ACP feed JSON document first." };
    99	  }
   100	  let raw: unknown;
   101	  try {
   102	    raw = JSON.parse(text);
   103	  } catch (e) {
   104	    return {
   105	      ok: false,
   106	      error: `Not valid JSON: ${e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e)}`,
   107	    };
   108	  }
   109	  if (typeof raw !== "object" || raw === null || Array.isArray(raw)) {
   110	    return { ok: false, error: "Expected a JSON object with an `items` array — the top-level value is not a feed object." };
   111	  }
   112	  const items = (raw as { items?: unknown }).items;
   113	  if (!Array.isArray(items)) {
   114	    return {
   115	      ok: false,
   116	      error:
   117	        "This does not look like an ACP feed: the top-level `items` array is missing. See the sample feed for the expected shape.",
   118	    };
   119	  }
   120	  // batch-F P2 fix: a null / non-object / id-less row would throw inside the
   121	  // engine instead of reaching the honest error path — validate row shape
   122	  // first. Wrong or missing FIELD VALUES stay verifiable (they surface as
   123	  // findings); only rows the adapter cannot even read are rejected here.
   124	  for (let i = 0; i < items.length; i++) {
   125	    const row = items[i];
   126	    if (typeof row !== "object" || row === null || Array.isArray(row)) {
   127	      return {
   128	        ok: false,
   129	        error: `items[${i}] is not an object — every feed row must be a JSON object. See the sample feed for the expected shape.`,
   130	      };
   131	    }
   132	    if (typeof (row as { item_id?: unknown }).item_id !== "string") {
   133	      return {
   134	        ok: false,
   135	        error: `items[${i}] has no string \`item_id\` — every feed row needs one so its findings can name the row they belong to.`,
   136	      };
   137	    }
   138	  }
   139	  return { ok: true, feed: raw as AcpFeed };
   140	}
   141	
   142	/**
   143	 * Run the real verification — the exact CLI composition, in the browser.
   144	 * Deterministic: same feed in, same report out.
   145	 *
   146	 * `catalog` defaults to the committed illustrative records, so every existing
   147	 * caller (and the golden-equality suite) is unchanged. A reader-supplied
   148	 * catalog arrives via {@link parseCatalogText} and is passed explicitly, with
   149	 * the provenance that describes it (slice 1: the report labels the RUN).
   150	 */
   151	export function verifyAcpFeed(
   152	  feed: AcpFeed,
   153	  catalog: SyntheticCatalog = SOR_CATALOG,
   154	): VerifierReport {
   155	  return runListingsVerification(acpFeedToClaims(feed), catalog, {
   156	    // The truth side decides the honesty label. Checked against OUR committed
   157	    // records, the run genuinely IS the committed corpus and IS matched by ids
   158	    // we minted. Checked against the reader's own catalog, neither claim is
   159	    // ours to make. Both labels are C3/C10 surfaces; see run.ts.
   160	    matchingMode: isCommittedCorpus(catalog) ? "synthetic-controlled" : "real-world",
   161	    simulated: isCommittedCorpus(catalog),
   162	  });
   163	}
   164	
   165	/**
   166	 * Is this catalog the committed corpus — by CONTENT, not by object identity?
   167	 *
   168	 * Reference identity (`catalog !== SOR_CATALOG`) was the first implementation
   169	 * and it was WRONG, caught by the adversarial pass in
   170	 * evals/packs/provenance-attack.test.ts: the "Use the sample catalog" button
   171	 * fills the slot with `catalogSampleText()` — the committed corpus verbatim —
   172	 * which comes back through `parseCatalogText` as a NEW OBJECT. Under reference
   173	 * identity that run was labelled `real-world` / not-simulated: our own
   174	 * synthetic corpus reported as the reader's real records, matched by
   175	 * entity resolution that never ran. A claim broader than the thing backing it,
   176	 * introduced by the fix meant to prevent exactly that — so the check compares
   177	 * what the engine actually READS.
   178	 *
   179	 * Only `items` is compared. The engine reads `items` and `asOf`
   180	 * (reference.ts:38,64), but `asOf` rides on the same document and cannot
   181	 * differ without `items` differing for any path that produces this corpus; the
   182	 * records are what make the data OURS. Erring toward "reader's own" on any
   183	 * mismatch is the safe direction: it declines to claim a run as our simulation
   184	 * rather than claiming someone's real data is.
   185	 */
   186	function isCommittedCorpus(catalog: SyntheticCatalog): boolean {
   187	  return (
   188	    catalog === SOR_CATALOG ||
   189	    JSON.stringify(catalog.items) === JSON.stringify(SOR_CATALOG.items)
   190	  );
   191	}
   192	
   193	export type CatalogParseResult =
   194	  | { readonly ok: true; readonly catalog: SyntheticCatalog }
   195	  | { readonly ok: false; readonly error: string };
   196	
   197	const STOCK_STATES = ["in_stock", "soldout_86", "hidden"] as const;
   198	
   199	/**
   200	 * Parse pasted/uploaded text as a merchant catalog — the truth side of an
   201	 * upload (owner commission 2026-07-27).
   202	 *
   203	 * Required fields are exactly the ones the ENGINE READS: `asOf`, and per item
   204	 * `id`, `name`, `variations[].id`, `.name`, `.priceCents`, `.stock`
   205	 * (reference.ts:27-46, detectors.ts:38,94,222). `description`, `category` and
   206	 * `modifierLists` ride on the TYPE but are never read by a rule, so requiring
   207	 * them would refuse a catalog this engine verifies perfectly — a false refusal
   208	 * is as dishonest as a false verdict. They are preserved when present.
   209	 *
   210	 * Wrong VALUES are deliberately not parse errors: a price that disagrees with
   211	 * the feed is the product's entire purpose and must reach the engine as a
   212	 * FINDING. Only rows the engine cannot index are refused, each naming its own
   213	 * index so the reader can find it.
   214	 */
   215	export function parseCatalogText(text: string): CatalogParseResult {
   216	  if (!text.trim()) {
   217	    return { ok: false, error: "The catalog is empty — upload or paste a merchant catalog first." };
   218	  }
   219	  let raw: unknown;
   220	  try {
   221	    raw = JSON.parse(text);
   222	  } catch (e) {
   223	    return { ok: false, error: `Not valid JSON: ${e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e)}` };
   224	  }
   225	  if (typeof raw !== "object" || raw === null || Array.isArray(raw)) {
   226	    return {
   227	      ok: false,
   228	      error: "Expected a JSON object with an `items` array — the top-level value is not a catalog object.",
   229	    };
   230	  }
   231	  const doc = raw as Record<string, unknown>;
   232	  if (typeof doc.asOf !== "string" || !doc.asOf.trim()) {
   233	    return {
   234	      ok: false,
   235	      error:
   236	        "This catalog has no string `asOf`. The staleness rules read it as data — reading a clock instead would make the same catalog produce different results on different days.",
   237	    };
   238	  }
   239	  if (!Array.isArray(doc.items)) {
   240	    return {
   241	      ok: false,
   242	      error:
   243	        "This does not look like a merchant catalog: the top-level `items` array is missing. Load the sample catalog for the expected shape.",
   244	    };
   245	  }
   246	  if (doc.items.length === 0) {
   247	    // Accepted, an empty catalog yields a report where EVERY feed row is a
   248	    // ghost — a confident wall of errors whose real cause is that no records
   249	    // were supplied. Naming that beats producing the verdict (adversarial pass,
   250	    // evals/packs/catalog-parse-attack.test.ts).
   251	    return {
   252	      ok: false,
   253	      error:
   254	        "This catalog has no items. An audit against zero records would report every feed row as an item that does not exist, which says nothing about the feed.",
   255	    };
   256	  }
   257	
   258	  const items: SorItem[] = [];
   259	  /** Variation ids must be unique ACROSS the whole catalog — see below. */
   260	  const seenVariationIds = new Set<string>();
   261	  for (let i = 0; i < doc.items.length; i++) {
   262	    const row = doc.items[i] as Record<string, unknown>;
   263	    const at = `items[${i}]`;
   264	    if (typeof row !== "object" || row === null || Array.isArray(row)) {
   265	      return { ok: false, error: `${at} is not an object — every catalog item must be a JSON object.` };
   266	    }
   267	    if (typeof row.id !== "string" || !row.id) {
   268	      return { ok: false, error: `${at} has no string \`id\` — every item needs one so its findings can name it.` };
   269	    }
   270	    if (typeof row.name !== "string" || !row.name) {
   271	      return { ok: false, error: `${at} has no string \`name\` — the name is how a feed row is matched when its id does not resolve.` };
   272	    }
   273	    if (!Array.isArray(row.variations) || row.variations.length === 0) {
   274	      return {
   275	        ok: false,
   276	        error: `${at} has no non-empty \`variations\` array — an item with no sellable variation has nothing a feed could claim.`,
   277	      };
   278	    }
   279	
   280	    const variations: SorVariation[] = [];
   281	    for (let v = 0; v < row.variations.length; v++) {
   282	      const vr = row.variations[v] as Record<string, unknown>;
   283	      const vat = `${at}.variations[${v}]`;
   284	      if (typeof vr !== "object" || vr === null || Array.isArray(vr)) {
   285	        return { ok: false, error: `${vat} is not an object.` };
   286	      }
   287	      if (typeof vr.id !== "string" || !vr.id) {
   288	        return { ok: false, error: `${vat} has no string \`id\` — the id is what a feed row is matched against.` };
   289	      }
   290	      if (typeof vr.name !== "string") {
   291	        return { ok: false, error: `${vat} has no string \`name\`.` };
   292	      }
   293	      if (typeof vr.priceCents !== "number" || !Number.isInteger(vr.priceCents)) {
   294	        return {
   295	          ok: false,
   296	          error: `${vat} has a non-integer \`priceCents\` — prices are integer cents, so 21.50 dollars is 2150. Fractional cents cannot be compared exactly.`,
   297	        };
   298	      }
   299	      if (typeof vr.stock !== "string" || !STOCK_STATES.includes(vr.stock as SorVariation["stock"])) {
   300	        return {
   301	          ok: false,
   302	          error: `${vat} has an unknown \`stock\` state — expected one of ${STOCK_STATES.join(", ")}.`,
   303	        };
   304	      }
   305	      // UNIQUENESS IS LOAD-BEARING, not tidiness. `indexCatalog` keys a Map by
   306	      // variation id (reference.ts:38-44), so a duplicate SILENTLY OVERWRITES
   307	      // the earlier record: a feed row would then be checked against whichever
   308	      // copy happened to be last, and the report could not say which — two
   309	      // prices, one verdict, no way to tell them apart. That is a wrong verdict
   310	      // wearing a clean face, which is the exact failure this product exists to
   311	      // catch. Refuse and name the id.
   312	      if (seenVariationIds.has(vr.id)) {
   313	        return {
   314	          ok: false,
   315	          error: `${vat} repeats the variation id "${vr.id}", which already appears earlier in this catalog. Ids identify the record a claim is checked against, so a duplicate makes the verdict ambiguous — no report could say which of the two rows it used.`,
   316	        };
   317	      }
   318	      seenVariationIds.add(vr.id);
   319	
   320	      variations.push({
   321	        id: vr.id,
   322	        name: vr.name,
   323	        priceCents: vr.priceCents,
   324	        stock: vr.stock as SorVariation["stock"],
   325	      });
   326	    }
   327	
   328	    items.push({
   329	      id: row.id,
   330	      name: row.name,
   331	      description: typeof row.description === "string" ? row.description : "",
   332	      category: typeof row.category === "string" ? row.category : "",
   333	      variations,
   334	      modifierLists: Array.isArray(row.modifierLists)
   335	        ? (row.modifierLists as SorItem["modifierLists"])
   336	        : [],
   337	    });
   338	  }
   339	
   340	  return {
   341	    ok: true,
   342	    catalog: {
   343	      // NORMALIZED, never inherited. `simulated` on the CATALOG type is a
   344	      // literal `true` describing this corpus family; what a REPORT says about
   345	      // a run is decided by the caller's ListingsRunProvenance (slice 1), so an
   346	      // uploaded document cannot assert its own honesty label.
   347	      simulated: true,
   348	      generator: { name: "reader-supplied catalog", seed: 0, version: "upload" },
   349	      merchantName: typeof doc.merchantName === "string" ? doc.merchantName : "Uploaded merchant",
   350	      currency: "USD",
   351	      asOf: doc.asOf,
   352	      items,
   353	    },
   354	  };
   355	}
     1	"use client";
     2	
     3	/**
     4	 * The two-file audit workbench — the working tool (owner commission
     5	 * 2026-07-27: "I WANT WORKING WEBSITE WHERE I WILL UPLOAD TEST FILES").
     6	 *
     7	 * WHAT CHANGED AND WHY. The previous surface took ONE file (a feed) and
     8	 * checked it against a catalog compiled into the page. That is a demonstration
     9	 * of a rule, not an audit of your data: upload your own feed and nearly every
    10	 * row comes back "unknown or missing", because your items were never in our
    11	 * five illustrative records. A real audit takes BOTH sides — the claims and
    12	 * the records they are checked against — which is exactly what the CLI has
    13	 * always done (`runCheck(feedPath, catalogPath, surface)`, cli.ts:37).
    14	 *
    15	 * HONESTY OF THE REPORT HEADER. When the record side is the reader's own, the
    16	 * run is no longer ours and no longer matched by ids we minted, so the report
    17	 * must not say it was. That is slice 1: `verifyAcpFeed` passes the provenance
    18	 * describing the actual run (verify-in-browser.ts). Without it this component
    19	 * would stamp a reader's real records with the committed corpus's own labels —
    20	 * a claim broader than the thing backing it, the exact defect class this repo
    21	 * has caught six times across sessions 34-35.
    22	 *
    23	 * (Lab vocabulary is deliberately absent from this file, comments included:
    24	 * every byte here ships in the client bundle, and the C10 gate scans the
    25	 * source, not just the rendered text.)
    26	 *
    27	 * ZERO NETWORK, STILL. Every byte is read with FileReader and verified in this
    28	 * tab. Nothing is uploaded anywhere — "upload" here means "into the page", and
    29	 * the import-graph guard proves the closure cannot reach a network at all.
    30	 */
    31	import { useState } from "react";
    32	import type { VerifierReport } from "@/lib/verifier-core/report";
    33	import type { SyntheticCatalog } from "@/lib/packs/listings/types";
    34	import { FileDrop, type SlotStatus } from "./FileDrop";
    35	import {
    36	  SOR_CATALOG,
    37	  catalogSampleText,
    38	  cleanFinding,
    39	  parseAcpFeedText,
    40	  parseCatalogText,
    41	  sampleFeedText,
    42	  verifyAcpFeed,
    43	} from "./verify-in-browser";
    44	
    45	interface SlotState {
    46	  readonly text: string;
    47	  readonly fileName: string | null;
    48	  readonly status: SlotStatus | null;
    49	}
    50	
    51	const EMPTY: SlotState = { text: "", fileName: null, status: null };
    52	
    53	interface RunState {
    54	  readonly report: VerifierReport;
    55	  /** True when the record side was the reader's own upload. */
    56	  readonly readerRecords: boolean;
    57	  readonly feedRows: number;
    58	  readonly recordRows: number;
    59	}
    60	
    61	function severityCounts(report: VerifierReport) {
    62	  const counts = { error: 0, warn: 0, info: 0 };
    63	  for (const f of report.findings) counts[f.severity] += 1;
    64	  return counts;
    65	}
    66	
    67	export function AuditWorkbench() {
    68	  const [feed, setFeed] = useState<SlotState>(EMPTY);
    69	  const [record, setRecord] = useState<SlotState>(EMPTY);
    70	  const [run, setRun] = useState<RunState | null>(null);
    71	  const [runError, setRunError] = useState<string | null>(null);
    72	
    73	  /** Any new input invalidates the verdict on screen — a stale verdict beside
    74	   *  changed inputs is a lie the reader has no way to detect. */
    75	  function invalidate() {
    76	    setRun(null);
    77	    setRunError(null);
    78	  }
    79	
    80	  function feedText(text: string, fileName: string | null) {
    81	    invalidate();
    82	    const parsed = parseAcpFeedText(text);
    83	    setFeed({
    84	      text,
    85	      fileName,
    86	      status: !text.trim()
    87	        ? null
    88	        : parsed.ok
    89	          ? { kind: "ok", summary: `${parsed.feed.items.length} rows read` }
    90	          : { kind: "error", message: parsed.error },
    91	    });
    92	  }
    93	
    94	  function recordText(text: string, fileName: string | null) {
    95	    invalidate();
    96	    const parsed = parseCatalogText(text);
    97	    setRecord({
    98	      text,
    99	      fileName,
   100	      status: !text.trim()
   101	        ? null
   102	        : parsed.ok
   103	          ? { kind: "ok", summary: `${parsed.catalog.items.length} items read` }
   104	          : { kind: "error", message: parsed.error },
   105	    });
   106	  }
   107	
   108	  function runAudit() {
   109	    invalidate();
   110	    const parsedFeed = parseAcpFeedText(feed.text);
   111	    if (!parsedFeed.ok) {
   112	      setRunError(`The feed could not be read. ${parsedFeed.error}`);
   113	      return;
   114	    }
   115	
   116	    // An empty record slot is not an error — it means "check against the
   117	    // records we ship", which is the honest default and stays labelled as such.
   118	    let catalog: SyntheticCatalog = SOR_CATALOG;
   119	    let readerRecords = false;
   120	    if (record.text.trim()) {
   121	      const parsedCatalog = parseCatalogText(record.text);
   122	      if (!parsedCatalog.ok) {
   123	        setRunError(`The record could not be read. ${parsedCatalog.error}`);
   124	        return;
   125	      }
   126	      catalog = parsedCatalog.catalog;
   127	      readerRecords = true;
   128	    }
   129	
   130	    try {
   131	      setRun({
   132	        report: verifyAcpFeed(parsedFeed.feed, catalog),
   133	        readerRecords,
   134	        feedRows: parsedFeed.feed.items.length,
   135	        recordRows: catalog.items.length,
   136	      });
   137	    } catch (e) {
   138	      setRunError(
   139	        `The verifier could not process this pair: ${e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e)}`,
   140	      );
   141	    }
   142	  }
   143	
   144	  function downloadReport() {
   145	    if (run === null) return;
   146	    const blob = new Blob([`${JSON.stringify(run.report, null, 2)}\n`], {
   147	      type: "application/json",
   148	    });
   149	    const url = URL.createObjectURL(blob);
   150	    const a = document.createElement("a");
   151	    a.href = url;
   152	    a.download = "curbside-commons-report.json";
   153	    a.click();
   154	    URL.revokeObjectURL(url);
   155	  }
   156	
   157	  const counts = run ? severityCounts(run.report) : null;
   158	  const canRun = feed.text.trim().length > 0;
   159	
   160	  return (
   161	    <div className="wb">
   162	      <div className="wb-slots">
   163	        <FileDrop
   164	          title="The feed"
   165	          hint="What is served to the agent — the claims being made."
   166	          textareaLabel="Feed JSON"
   167	          value={feed.text}
   168	          onText={feedText}
   169	          onReadError={(m) => {
   170	            invalidate();
   171	            setFeed({ text: "", fileName: null, status: { kind: "error", message: m } });
   172	          }}
   173	          fileName={feed.fileName}
   174	          status={feed.status}
   175	          onLoadSample={() => feedText(sampleFeedText(), "sample-feed.json")}
   176	          sampleLabel="Use the sample feed"
   177	        />
   178	        <FileDrop
   179	          title="The record"
   180	          hint="The merchant's own catalog — what the claims are checked against."
   181	          textareaLabel="Catalog JSON"
   182	          value={record.text}
   183	          onText={recordText}
   184	          onReadError={(m) => {
   185	            invalidate();
   186	            setRecord({ text: "", fileName: null, status: { kind: "error", message: m } });
   187	          }}
   188	          fileName={record.fileName}
   189	          status={record.status}
   190	          onLoadSample={() => recordText(catalogSampleText(), "sample-catalog.json")}
   191	          sampleLabel="Use the sample catalog"
   192	        />
   193	      </div>
   194	
   195	      <div className="wb-run">
   196	        <button type="button" className="lp-btn primary" onClick={runAudit} disabled={!canRun}>
   197	          Run the audit
   198	        </button>
   199	        {/* Three states, three different sentences. A note that reads the same
   200	            whether or not the button works tells the reader nothing about why
   201	            it is waiting (screenshot review, 2026-07-27). */}
   202	        <p className="wb-run-note">
   203	          {!canRun
   204	            ? "Add a feed to run the audit — the record side is optional."
   205	            : record.text.trim()
   206	              ? "Your feed will be checked against your own records."
   207	              : "No record supplied, so your feed is checked against the sample catalog shipped with this site."}
   208	        </p>
   209	      </div>
   210	
   211	      {/* No-JS: the whole workbench runs in the reader's browser. Without
   212	          scripting there is nothing to run, so dead controls are hidden and the
   213	          requirement is stated plainly (the v9 defect class, session 26). */}
   214	      <noscript>
   215	        <style dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: ".wb-slots,.wb-run{display:none}" }} />
   216	        <p className="wb-hint">
   217	          The audit runs entirely in your browser and needs scripting turned on. Nothing runs on a
   218	          server either way.
   219	        </p>
   220	      </noscript>
   221	
   222	      {runError !== null && (
   223	        <div className="wb-error" role="alert">
   224	          <strong>No verdict.</strong> {runError}
   225	        </div>
   226	      )}
   227	
   228	      {run !== null && counts !== null && (
   229	        <section className="wb-result" aria-label="Audit result">
   230	          <header className="wb-verdict-head">
   231	            <p className={run.report.ok ? "wb-verdict ok" : "wb-verdict fail"}>
   232	              {run.report.ok ? "PASS" : "FAIL"}
   233	            </p>
   234	            <p className="wb-tally">
   235	              {run.report.findings.length} finding{run.report.findings.length === 1 ? "" : "s"} —{" "}
   236	              {counts.error} error · {counts.warn} warn · {counts.info} info
   237	            </p>
   238	            <button type="button" className="lp-btn ghost wb-dl" onClick={downloadReport}>
   239	              Download the report
   240	            </button>
   241	          </header>
   242	
   243	          <p className="wb-prov">
   244	            {run.feedRows} feed rows checked against {run.recordRows}{" "}
   245	            {run.readerRecords ? "of your own records" : "sample records"}, computed in your browser
   246	            just now — no AI calls, no network requests, nothing left this page.
   247	            {!run.readerRecords &&
   248	              " Items outside the sample records honestly read as unknown or missing."}
   249	          </p>
   250	
   251	          <dl className="wb-meta">
   252	            <div>
   253	              <dt>spec version</dt>
   254	              <dd className="wb-mono">{run.report.specVersion}</dd>
   255	            </div>
   256	            <div>
   257	              <dt>matching</dt>
   258	              <dd className="wb-mono">{run.report.matchingMode}</dd>
   259	            </div>
   260	            <div>
   261	              <dt>record side</dt>
   262	              <dd className="wb-mono">{run.readerRecords ? "your upload" : "sample catalog"}</dd>
   263	            </div>
   264	          </dl>
   265	
   266	          {run.report.findings.length > 0 ? (
   267	            <ol className="wb-findings">
   268	              {run.report.findings.map((f) => (
   269	                <li key={`${f.claim.id}:${f.ruleId}:${f.claim.field}`} className="wb-finding">
   270	                  <p className="wb-plain">
   271	                    <span className={`wb-sev ${f.severity}`}>{f.severity}</span>{" "}
   272	                    {cleanFinding(f.plainLine ?? "")}
   273	                  </p>
   274	                  <dl className="wb-receipts">
   275	                    <div>
   276	                      <dt>claim</dt>
   277	                      <dd className="wb-mono">
   278	                        {f.claim.id} · {f.claim.field}
   279	                      </dd>
   280	                    </div>
   281	                    <div>
   282	                      <dt>asserted</dt>
   283	                      <dd className="wb-mono">{JSON.stringify(f.claim.value)}</dd>
   284	                    </div>
   285	                    <div>
   286	                      <dt>checked against</dt>
   287	                      <dd className="wb-mono">{f.referenceRowId}</dd>
   288	                    </div>
   289	                    <div>
   290	                      <dt>rule</dt>
   291	                      <dd className="wb-mono">{f.ruleId}</dd>
   292	                    </div>
   293	                  </dl>
   294	                </li>
   295	              ))}
   296	            </ol>
   297	          ) : (
   298	            <p className="wb-clean">
   299	              No drift detected — every claim in this feed agrees with the records it was checked
   300	              against.
   301	            </p>
   302	          )}
   303	        </section>
   304	      )}
   305	    </div>
   306	  );
   307	}
     1	"use client";
     2	
     3	/**
     4	 * One upload slot — drag-drop, click-to-browse, or paste (owner commission
     5	 * 2026-07-27, "a working website where I will upload test files").
     6	 *
     7	 * THE PROMISE THIS COMPONENT MUST NOT BREAK. /playground states on its face:
     8	 * "No network requests — nothing pasted leaves the page." A file input keeps
     9	 * that promise only if the file is read LOCALLY. `FileReader` is a browser API
    10	 * that never touches the network, and this module imports nothing that could:
    11	 * evals/packs/import-walk-guard.test.ts walks the import graph of the
    12	 * playground closure and asserts no node:fs and no fetch reaches it. The
    13	 * upload path is therefore zero-network BY CONSTRUCTION, not by promise.
    14	 *
    15	 * ACCESSIBILITY. A drop zone that is only a drop zone excludes every reader
    16	 * who cannot drag. The real control here is a native <input type="file">,
    17	 * kept in the accessibility tree and driven by its own <label> — so keyboard,
    18	 * screen reader, and pointer all reach the same function. The drag surface is
    19	 * an ENHANCEMENT layered on top; dragging is never the only way in. The paste
    20	 * textarea is the third route, and the one that needs no file at all.
    21	 *
    22	 * TWO CHANNELS, NEVER ONE. Content and failure travel separately: `onText`
    23	 * carries what the reader supplied, `onReadError` carries why nothing arrived.
    24	 * Encoding a failure as content (a sentinel string, a leading space) is the
    25	 * shape this product exists to catch — a value that means something other than
    26	 * what it says.
    27	 *
    28	 * WHY THE FILE NAME IS SHOWN. A two-slot tool whose slots look alike is a tool
    29	 * you can load backwards. Echoing the file name and its parsed row count is
    30	 * how a reader catches "I dropped the feed into the record slot" before the
    31	 * verdict confuses them.
    32	 */
    33	import { useId, useState } from "react";
    34	
    35	export type SlotStatus =
    36	  | { readonly kind: "ok"; readonly summary: string }
    37	  | { readonly kind: "error"; readonly message: string };
    38	
    39	export interface FileDropProps {
    40	  /** Slot heading — "The feed" / "The record". */
    41	  readonly title: string;
    42	  /** One line saying what this side IS, in the site's plain voice. */
    43	  readonly hint: string;
    44	  /** Accessible name for the paste textarea. */
    45	  readonly textareaLabel: string;
    46	  /** Current text held by the slot (lifted state: the parent owns the run). */
    47	  readonly value: string;
    48	  /** Reader supplied content — from a file, a paste, or the sample. */
    49	  readonly onText: (text: string, fileName: string | null) => void;
    50	  /** The file could not be read at all; no content is arriving. */
    51	  readonly onReadError: (message: string) => void;
    52	  /** Name of the file that filled this slot, if it came from one. */
    53	  readonly fileName: string | null;
    54	  /** Parse/read status, rendered inline so a failure sits beside its cause. */
    55	  readonly status: SlotStatus | null;
    56	  readonly onLoadSample: () => void;
    57	  readonly sampleLabel: string;
    58	}
    59	
    60	/** Refuse implausibly large files before reading them into memory. */
    61	const MAX_BYTES = 5 * 1024 * 1024;
    62	
    63	export function FileDrop({
    64	  title,
    65	  hint,
    66	  textareaLabel,
    67	  value,
    68	  onText,
    69	  onReadError,
    70	  fileName,
    71	  status,
    72	  onLoadSample,
    73	  sampleLabel,
    74	}: FileDropProps) {
    75	  const [dragging, setDragging] = useState(false);
    76	  const inputId = useId();
    77	  const areaId = useId();
    78	
    79	  function readFile(file: File) {
    80	    if (file.size > MAX_BYTES) {
    81	      onReadError(
    82	        `${file.name} is ${(file.size / 1024 / 1024).toFixed(1)} MB — larger than the 5 MB this tab will read.`,
    83	      );
    84	      return;
    85	    }
    86	    const reader = new FileReader();
    87	    reader.onload = () => onText(String(reader.result ?? ""), file.name);
    88	    reader.onerror = () => onReadError(`Could not read ${file.name}.`);
    89	    reader.readAsText(file);
    90	  }
    91	
    92	  return (
    93	    <div className="fd-slot">
    94	      <div className="fd-head">
    95	        <p className="fd-title">{title}</p>
    96	        <p className="fd-hint">{hint}</p>
    97	      </div>
    98	
    99	      {/* The drag surface decorates a real control; the <label> reaches the
   100	          native input, so a keyboard user takes the same path a mouse does. */}
   101	      <div
   102	        className={`fd-zone${dragging ? " is-drag" : ""}${fileName !== null ? " is-filled" : ""}`}
   103	        onDragOver={(e) => {
   104	          e.preventDefault();
   105	          setDragging(true);
   106	        }}
   107	        onDragLeave={() => setDragging(false)}
   108	        onDrop={(e) => {
   109	          e.preventDefault();
   110	          setDragging(false);
   111	          const file = e.dataTransfer.files?.[0];
   112	          if (file) readFile(file);
   113	        }}
   114	      >
   115	        <input
   116	          id={inputId}
   117	          className="fd-input"
   118	          type="file"
   119	          accept="application/json,.json,text/plain,.txt"
   120	          onChange={(e) => {
   121	            const file = e.target.files?.[0];
   122	            if (file) readFile(file);
   123	            // Reset so re-choosing the SAME file fires change again.
   124	            e.target.value = "";
   125	          }}
   126	        />
   127	        <label className="fd-label" htmlFor={inputId}>
   128	          <span className="fd-cta">Choose a file</span>
   129	          <span className="fd-or">or drag it here</span>
   130	        </label>
   131	        <button type="button" className="fd-sample" onClick={onLoadSample}>
   132	          {sampleLabel}
   133	        </button>
   134	      </div>
   135	
   136	      {fileName !== null && <p className="fd-file">{fileName}</p>}
   137	
   138	      {status !== null && (
   139	        <p
   140	          className={`fd-status ${status.kind}`}
   141	          role={status.kind === "error" ? "alert" : undefined}
   142	        >
   143	          {status.kind === "ok" ? status.summary : status.message}
   144	        </p>
   145	      )}
   146	
   147	      <details className="fd-paste">
   148	        <summary>or paste it as text</summary>
   149	        <label className="fd-paste-label" htmlFor={areaId}>
   150	          {textareaLabel}
   151	        </label>
   152	        <textarea
   153	          id={areaId}
   154	          className="fd-area"
   155	          spellCheck={false}
   156	          value={value}
   157	          onChange={(e) => onText(e.target.value, null)}
   158	        />
   159	      </details>
   160	    </div>
   161	  );
   162	}

+/**
+ * Pretty-printed sample CATALOG text — the record side of the pair (owner
+ * commission 2026-07-27). A two-file tool is unusable without a worked example
+ * of BOTH files: a reader who has never seen the catalog shape cannot author
+ * one, and guessing at it produces parse refusals that read as the tool being
+ * broken. Loading this beside the sample feed is also the fastest way to see a
+ * PASS, since the pair is internally consistent apart from the planted drift.
+ */
+export function catalogSampleText(): string {
+  return JSON.stringify(SOR_CATALOG, null, 2);
+}

 /**
  * Display-layer copy cleanup for rendered finding text (matches /report + /demo).
  * The illustrative feed's ghost item carries a lab-label inside its own title —
@@ -130,7 +142,214 @@ export function parseAcpFeedText(text: string): ParseResult {
 /**
  * Run the real verification — the exact CLI composition, in the browser.
  * Deterministic: same feed in, same report out.
+ *
+ * `catalog` defaults to the committed illustrative records, so every existing
+ * caller (and the golden-equality suite) is unchanged. A reader-supplied
+ * catalog arrives via {@link parseCatalogText} and is passed explicitly, with
+ * the provenance that describes it (slice 1: the report labels the RUN).
+ */
+export function verifyAcpFeed(
+  feed: AcpFeed,
+  catalog: SyntheticCatalog = SOR_CATALOG,
+): VerifierReport {
+  return runListingsVerification(acpFeedToClaims(feed), catalog, {
+    // The truth side decides the honesty label. Checked against OUR committed
+    // records, the run genuinely IS the committed corpus and IS matched by ids
+    // we minted. Checked against the reader's own catalog, neither claim is
+    // ours to make. Both labels are C3/C10 surfaces; see run.ts.
+    matchingMode: isCommittedCorpus(catalog) ? "synthetic-controlled" : "real-world",
+    simulated: isCommittedCorpus(catalog),
+  });
+}
+
+/**
+ * Is this catalog the committed corpus — by CONTENT, not by object identity?
+ *
+ * Reference identity (`catalog !== SOR_CATALOG`) was the first implementation
+ * and it was WRONG, caught by the adversarial pass in
+ * evals/packs/provenance-attack.test.ts: the "Use the sample catalog" button
+ * fills the slot with `catalogSampleText()` — the committed corpus verbatim —
+ * which comes back through `parseCatalogText` as a NEW OBJECT. Under reference
+ * identity that run was labelled `real-world` / not-simulated: our own
+ * synthetic corpus reported as the reader's real records, matched by
+ * entity resolution that never ran. A claim broader than the thing backing it,
+ * introduced by the fix meant to prevent exactly that — so the check compares
+ * what the engine actually READS.
+ *
+ * Only `items` is compared. The engine reads `items` and `asOf`
+ * (reference.ts:38,64), but `asOf` rides on the same document and cannot
+ * differ without `items` differing for any path that produces this corpus; the
+ * records are what make the data OURS. Erring toward "reader's own" on any
+ * mismatch is the safe direction: it declines to claim a run as our simulation
+ * rather than claiming someone's real data is.
  */
-export function verifyAcpFeed(feed: AcpFeed): VerifierReport {
-  return runListingsVerification(acpFeedToClaims(feed), SOR_CATALOG);
+function isCommittedCorpus(catalog: SyntheticCatalog): boolean {
+  return (
+    catalog === SOR_CATALOG ||
+    JSON.stringify(catalog.items) === JSON.stringify(SOR_CATALOG.items)
+  );
+}
+
+export type CatalogParseResult =
+  | { readonly ok: true; readonly catalog: SyntheticCatalog }
+  | { readonly ok: false; readonly error: string };
+
+const STOCK_STATES = ["in_stock", "soldout_86", "hidden"] as const;
+
+/**
+ * Parse pasted/uploaded text as a merchant catalog — the truth side of an
+ * upload (owner commission 2026-07-27).
+ *
+ * Required fields are exactly the ones the ENGINE READS: `asOf`, and per item
+ * `id`, `name`, `variations[].id`, `.name`, `.priceCents`, `.stock`
+ * (reference.ts:27-46, detectors.ts:38,94,222). `description`, `category` and
+ * `modifierLists` ride on the TYPE but are never read by a rule, so requiring
+ * them would refuse a catalog this engine verifies perfectly — a false refusal
+ * is as dishonest as a false verdict. They are preserved when present.
+ *
+ * Wrong VALUES are deliberately not parse errors: a price that disagrees with
+ * the feed is the product's entire purpose and must reach the engine as a
+ * FINDING. Only rows the engine cannot index are refused, each naming its own
+ * index so the reader can find it.
+ */
+export function parseCatalogText(text: string): CatalogParseResult {
+  if (!text.trim()) {
+    return { ok: false, error: "The catalog is empty — upload or paste a merchant catalog first." };
+  }
+  let raw: unknown;
+  try {
+    raw = JSON.parse(text);
+  } catch (e) {
+    return { ok: false, error: `Not valid JSON: ${e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e)}` };
+  }
+  if (typeof raw !== "object" || raw === null || Array.isArray(raw)) {
+    return {
+      ok: false,
+      error: "Expected a JSON object with an `items` array — the top-level value is not a catalog object.",
+    };
+  }
+  const doc = raw as Record<string, unknown>;
+  if (typeof doc.asOf !== "string" || !doc.asOf.trim()) {
+    return {
+      ok: false,
+      error:
+        "This catalog has no string `asOf`. The staleness rules read it as data — reading a clock instead would make the same catalog produce different results on different days.",
+    };
+  }
+  if (!Array.isArray(doc.items)) {
+    return {
+      ok: false,
+      error:
+        "This does not look like a merchant catalog: the top-level `items` array is missing. Load the sample catalog for the expected shape.",
+    };
+  }
+  if (doc.items.length === 0) {
+    // Accepted, an empty catalog yields a report where EVERY feed row is a
+    // ghost — a confident wall of errors whose real cause is that no records
+    // were supplied. Naming that beats producing the verdict (adversarial pass,
+    // evals/packs/catalog-parse-attack.test.ts).
+    return {
+      ok: false,
+      error:
+        "This catalog has no items. An audit against zero records would report every feed row as an item that does not exist, which says nothing about the feed.",
+    };
+  }
+
+  const items: SorItem[] = [];
+  /** Variation ids must be unique ACROSS the whole catalog — see below. */
+  const seenVariationIds = new Set<string>();
+  for (let i = 0; i < doc.items.length; i++) {
+    const row = doc.items[i] as Record<string, unknown>;
+    const at = `items[${i}]`;
+    if (typeof row !== "object" || row === null || Array.isArray(row)) {
+      return { ok: false, error: `${at} is not an object — every catalog item must be a JSON object.` };
+    }
+    if (typeof row.id !== "string" || !row.id) {
+      return { ok: false, error: `${at} has no string \`id\` — every item needs one so its findings can name it.` };
+    }
+    if (typeof row.name !== "string" || !row.name) {
+      return { ok: false, error: `${at} has no string \`name\` — the name is how a feed row is matched when its id does not resolve.` };
+    }
+    if (!Array.isArray(row.variations) || row.variations.length === 0) {
+      return {
+        ok: false,
+        error: `${at} has no non-empty \`variations\` array — an item with no sellable variation has nothing a feed could claim.`,
+      };
+    }
+
+    const variations: SorVariation[] = [];
+    for (let v = 0; v < row.variations.length; v++) {
+      const vr = row.variations[v] as Record<string, unknown>;
+      const vat = `${at}.variations[${v}]`;
+      if (typeof vr !== "object" || vr === null || Array.isArray(vr)) {
+        return { ok: false, error: `${vat} is not an object.` };
+      }
+      if (typeof vr.id !== "string" || !vr.id) {
+        return { ok: false, error: `${vat} has no string \`id\` — the id is what a feed row is matched against.` };
+      }
+      if (typeof vr.name !== "string") {
+        return { ok: false, error: `${vat} has no string \`name\`.` };
+      }
+      if (typeof vr.priceCents !== "number" || !Number.isInteger(vr.priceCents)) {
+        return {
+          ok: false,
+          error: `${vat} has a non-integer \`priceCents\` — prices are integer cents, so 21.50 dollars is 2150. Fractional cents cannot be compared exactly.`,
+        };
+      }
+      if (typeof vr.stock !== "string" || !STOCK_STATES.includes(vr.stock as SorVariation["stock"])) {
+        return {
+          ok: false,
+          error: `${vat} has an unknown \`stock\` state — expected one of ${STOCK_STATES.join(", ")}.`,
+        };
+      }
+      // UNIQUENESS IS LOAD-BEARING, not tidiness. `indexCatalog` keys a Map by
+      // variation id (reference.ts:38-44), so a duplicate SILENTLY OVERWRITES
+      // the earlier record: a feed row would then be checked against whichever
+      // copy happened to be last, and the report could not say which — two
+      // prices, one verdict, no way to tell them apart. That is a wrong verdict
+      // wearing a clean face, which is the exact failure this product exists to
+      // catch. Refuse and name the id.
+      if (seenVariationIds.has(vr.id)) {
+        return {
+          ok: false,
+          error: `${vat} repeats the variation id "${vr.id}", which already appears earlier in this catalog. Ids identify the record a claim is checked against, so a duplicate makes the verdict ambiguous — no report could say which of the two rows it used.`,
+        };
+      }
+      seenVariationIds.add(vr.id);
+
+      variations.push({
+        id: vr.id,
+        name: vr.name,
+        priceCents: vr.priceCents,
+        stock: vr.stock as SorVariation["stock"],
+      });
+    }
+
+    items.push({
+      id: row.id,
+      name: row.name,
+      description: typeof row.description === "string" ? row.description : "",
+      category: typeof row.category === "string" ? row.category : "",
+      variations,
+      modifierLists: Array.isArray(row.modifierLists)
+        ? (row.modifierLists as SorItem["modifierLists"])
+        : [],
+    });
+  }
+
+  return {
+    ok: true,
+    catalog: {
+      // NORMALIZED, never inherited. `simulated` on the CATALOG type is a
+      // literal `true` describing this corpus family; what a REPORT says about
+      // a run is decided by the caller's ListingsRunProvenance (slice 1), so an
+      // uploaded document cannot assert its own honesty label.
+      simulated: true,
+      generator: { name: "reader-supplied catalog", seed: 0, version: "upload" },
+      merchantName: typeof doc.merchantName === "string" ? doc.merchantName : "Uploaded merchant",
+      currency: "USD",
+      asOf: doc.asOf,
+      items,
+    },
+  };
 }

+ * Open a slot's paste box and fill it — the keyboard/no-drag route.
+ * Idempotent: clicking the <summary> TOGGLES, so a second call on the same

+async function fillSlot(page: import("@playwright/test").Page, label: string, text: string) {
+  const slot = page.locator(".fd-slot", { hasText: label });
+  const details = slot.locator("details.fd-paste");
+  if (!(await details.evaluate((d: HTMLDetailsElement) => d.open))) {
+    await slot.getByText("or paste it as text").click();
+  }
+  await slot.locator("textarea").fill(text);

-/** Run the full listings verification for one surface's claims. */
+/**
+ * Who the data belonged to on THIS run — the two C3/C10 honesty labels a
+ * caller may set, and nothing else.
+ *
+ * Both were hardcoded until 2026-07-27, which was true of every caller then in
+ * existence (all fed the committed synthetic catalog) and becomes false the
+ * moment a reader supplies their own records: stamping a reader's real catalog
+ * `simulated: true`, and asserting shared-synthetic-ID matching that never
+ * happened, is a claim broader than the thing backing it (RULES §4).
+ *
+ * `specVersion` is deliberately NOT here. A caller may describe WHOSE data it
+ * ran on; it may never restate WHICH RULES ran — that stays pinned to
+ * {@link LISTINGS_SPEC_VERSION} by the engine (C10), proven by the T3 tooth in
+ * evals/packs/listings-provenance-labels.test.ts.
+ */
+export interface ListingsRunProvenance {
+  /** C3: shared synthetic IDs, or real-world entity resolution. */
+  readonly matchingMode?: MatchingMode;
+  /** C10: true when any synthetic artifact is involved in this run. */
+  readonly simulated?: boolean;
+}
+
+/**
+ * Run the full listings verification for one surface's claims.
+ *
+ * `provenance` is optional and defaults to the committed-corpus values, so
+ * every pre-existing two-argument call site produces a byte-identical report
+ * (the golden + drift-lock suites are the proof, and they were not touched).
+ * The labels describe the run; they never steer it — identical inputs yield
+ * identical findings under either label (T4 tooth), because the engine reads
+ * only `sor.items` and `sor.asOf` from a catalog.
+ */
 export function runListingsVerification(
   claims: readonly Claim[],
   sor: SyntheticCatalog,
+  provenance: ListingsRunProvenance = {},
 ): VerifierReport {
   const reference = sorReference(sor);
   const detected = verifyClaims(claims, reference, listingsDetectors);
   const completeness = completenessSweep(claims, sor);
   return buildReport([...detected, ...completeness], {
     specVersion: LISTINGS_SPEC_VERSION,
-    matchingMode: "synthetic-controlled",
-    simulated: true,
+    matchingMode: provenance.matchingMode ?? "synthetic-controlled",
+    simulated: provenance.simulated ?? true,
   });
 }

    95	/**
    96	 * Browser-safety closure walk — FAIL-CLOSED (batch-F P2 fix: the first cut's
    97	 * single regex missed dynamic import(), require(), and import-equals, and
    98	 * silently skipped unresolved specifiers — a fail-open "proof". This version:
    99	 * matches every import form, checks the COMPLETE Node builtin list, fails on
   100	 * any unresolved or non-allowlisted specifier, and proves its own matcher
   101	 * bites on planted hidden forms.)
   102	 */
   103	import { builtinModules } from "node:module";
   104	
   105	const IMPORT_FORMS: readonly RegExp[] = [
   106	  // import ... from "x" / export ... from "x"
   107	  /(?:import|export)\s[^"'`]*?from\s*["']([^"']+)["']/g,
   108	  // side-effect import "x"
   109	  /import\s*["']([^"']+)["']/g,
   110	  // dynamic import("x")
   111	  /import\s*\(\s*["']([^"']+)["']\s*\)/g,
   112	  // require("x") — incl. TS import-equals `import x = require("x")`
   113	  /require\s*\(\s*["']([^"']+)["']\s*\)/g,
   114	];
   115	
   116	function extractSpecifiers(src: string): string[] {
   117	  const specs: string[] = [];
   118	  for (const re of IMPORT_FORMS) {
   119	    for (const m of src.matchAll(re)) specs.push(m[1]);
   120	  }
   121	  return specs;
   122	}
   123	
   124	/** Bare specifiers the browser bundle legitimately provides. Everything else fails. */
   125	// "@number-flow/react": vetted ADOPT-WITH-CONDITIONS 2026-07-16 (MIT, exact-pinned
   126	// 0.6.1; zero network/hooks verified in the repo AND the published tarballs incl.
   127	// the esm-env transitive; reduced-motion/ARIA floors verified) — first real use is
   128	// the /fees paste-leg tally. The covering batch re-probes the whole condition set.
   129	// "react-dom": the shipped FeesView.tsx imports { flushSync } from it (a client-only
   130	// React DOM API for the SSR-then-page tab flush) — browser-provided, same bundle as the
   131	// already-trusted "react-dom/" prefix; no node builtin is reachable through it.
   132	const BARE_ALLOWLIST = new Set(["react", "react-dom", "@number-flow/react"]);
   133	const BARE_PREFIX_ALLOWLIST = ["react/", "next/", "react-dom/"];
   134	const NODE_BUILTINS = new Set([...builtinModules, ...builtinModules.map((m) => `node:${m}`)]);
   135	
   136	describe("playground browser safety (fail-closed import-graph walk)", () => {
   137	  function resolveSpec(fromFile: string, spec: string): string | null {
   138	    let base: string;
   139	    if (spec.startsWith("@/")) base = join(root, spec.slice(2));
   140	    else if (spec.startsWith(".")) base = resolve(dirname(fromFile), spec);
   141	    else return null;
   142	    // FILE candidates only (fee-surface extension 2026-07-16): a bare `base`
   143	    // hit can be a DIRECTORY (a barrel import like "@/lib/packs/fees"), which
   144	    // the old order returned and then failed to read — resolve barrels to
   145	    // their index.ts and never return a directory.
   146	    for (const cand of [`${base}.ts`, `${base}.tsx`, `${base}.json`, join(base, "index.ts"), base]) {
   147	      if (existsSync(cand) && statSync(cand).isFile()) return cand;
   148	    }
   149	    return null;
   150	  }
   151	
   152	  function walkClosure(entries: string[]): { seen: Set<string>; offenders: string[] } {
   153	    const seen = new Set<string>();
   154	    const offenders: string[] = [];
   155	    function walk(file: string) {
   156	      if (seen.has(file)) return;
   157	      seen.add(file);
   158	      if (file.endsWith(".json")) return;
   159	      const src = readFileSync(file, "utf8");
   160	      // Batch P2 fix (2026-07-16): a dynamic import()/require() whose specifier
   161	      // is NOT a single quoted literal (template literal, variable, or a
   162	      // quoted-prefix CONCATENATION like require("node:" + x)) would slip the
   163	      // extraction regexes — count call sites vs FULL-literal calls and fail
   164	      // closed on any gap.
   165	      const dynCalls = (src.match(/\bimport\s*\(/g) ?? []).length;
   166	      const dynLiterals = (src.match(/\bimport\s*\(\s*["'][^"']*["']\s*\)/g) ?? []).length;
   167	      if (dynCalls > dynLiterals) {
   168	        offenders.push(`${file} contains a dynamic import with a non-literal specifier — fail closed`);
   169	      }
   170	      const reqCalls = (src.match(/\brequire\s*\(/g) ?? []).length;
   171	      const reqLiterals = (src.match(/\brequire\s*\(\s*["'][^"']*["']\s*\)/g) ?? []).length;
   172	      if (reqCalls > reqLiterals) {
   173	        offenders.push(`${file} contains a require() with a non-literal specifier — fail closed`);
   174	      }
   175	      for (const spec of extractSpecifiers(src)) {
   176	        if (NODE_BUILTINS.has(spec)) {
   177	          offenders.push(`${file} imports the Node builtin "${spec}"`);
   178	          continue;
   179	        }
   180	        if (spec.startsWith("@/") || spec.startsWith(".")) {
   181	          const resolved = resolveSpec(file, spec);
   182	          if (resolved === null) {
   183	            offenders.push(`${file} imports "${spec}" which did not resolve — fail closed`);
   184	            continue;
   185	          }
   186	          walk(resolved);
   187	          continue;
   188	        }
   189	        // bare specifier: allowlisted or a failure — never silently skipped
   190	        if (BARE_ALLOWLIST.has(spec) || BARE_PREFIX_ALLOWLIST.some((p) => spec.startsWith(p))) {
   191	          continue;
   192	        }
   193	        offenders.push(`${file} imports non-allowlisted bare specifier "${spec}" — fail closed`);
   194	      }
   195	    }
   196	    for (const e of entries) walk(e);
   197	    return { seen, offenders };
   198	  }
   199	
   200	  it("the seam's transitive closure reaches no Node builtin and nothing unresolved", () => {
   201	    const { seen, offenders } = walkClosure([
   202	      join(root, "components", "playground", "verify-in-browser.ts"),
   203	      // The upload surface (2026-07-27) joins the same closure: a file input
   204	      // is only zero-network if what reads it is. FileReader never touches a
   205	      // network, and this walk proves nothing in the workbench's import graph
   206	      // can either — the page's "nothing leaves this page" promise is
   207	      // structural, not a courtesy.
   208	      join(root, "components", "playground", "AuditWorkbench.tsx"),
   209	      join(root, "components", "playground", "FileDrop.tsx"),
   210	      // Fee surface (NYC showcase N1+N2, 2026-07-16): the fee seam + clients
   211	      // run in the same browser closure — same fail-closed proof.
   212	      join(root, "components", "fees", "audit-in-browser.ts"),
   213	      join(root, "components", "fees", "FeePlaygroundClient.tsx"),
   214	      join(root, "components", "fees", "FeesView.tsx"),
   215	      join(root, "components", "fees", "fee-report-data.ts"),
   216	    ]);
   217	    expect(offenders, offenders.join("\n")).toEqual([]);
   218	    // Sanity: the walk actually traversed the engine, not just the seam.
   219	    expect(seen.size).toBeGreaterThan(8);
   220	  });
   221	
   222	  it("the scanner itself bites: every hidden import form is detected", () => {
   223	    const hidden = [
   224	      'void import("node:fs")',
   225	      'const fs = require("node:fs")',
   226	      'import fs = require("node:fs")',
   227	      'export { x } from "node:crypto"',
   228	      'import "fs"',
   229	      'import { join } from "path"',
   230	    ];
   231	    for (const sample of hidden) {
   232	      const specs = extractSpecifiers(sample);
   233	      const caught = specs.some((s) => NODE_BUILTINS.has(s));
   234	      expect(caught, `scanner missed: ${sample} (extracted: ${JSON.stringify(specs)})`).toBe(true);
   235	    }
   236	    // and a non-allowlisted bare package would fail rather than be skipped
   237	    const bare = extractSpecifiers('import x from "left-pad"');
   238	    expect(bare).toContain("left-pad");
   239	    expect(BARE_ALLOWLIST.has("left-pad")).toBe(false);
   240	  });
   241	
   242	  it("the non-literal-specifier catch bites (template-literal / variable imports)", () => {
   243	    // Mirrors the walker's FULL decision: a planted non-literal form is caught
   244	    // either by the call-site counting gap, or by its extracted quoted PREFIX
   245	    // failing the bare-specifier allowlist (fail-closed both ways).
   246	    const caughtByWalkRules = (planted: string): boolean => {
   247	      const dynCalls = (planted.match(/\bimport\s*\(/g) ?? []).length;
   248	      const dynLiterals = (planted.match(/\bimport\s*\(\s*["'][^"']*["']\s*\)/g) ?? []).length;
   249	      const reqCalls = (planted.match(/\brequire\s*\(/g) ?? []).length;
   250	      const reqLiterals = (planted.match(/\brequire\s*\(\s*["'][^"']*["']\s*\)/g) ?? []).length;
   251	      if (dynCalls > dynLiterals || reqCalls > reqLiterals) return true;
   252	      return extractSpecifiers(planted).some(
   253	        (s) =>
   254	          !s.startsWith("@/") &&
   255	          !s.startsWith(".") &&
   256	          !NODE_BUILTINS.has(s) &&
   257	          !BARE_ALLOWLIST.has(s) &&
   258	          !BARE_PREFIX_ALLOWLIST.some((p) => s.startsWith(p)),
   259	      );
   260	    };
   261	    for (const planted of [
   262	      "void import(`./mods/${name}`)",
   263	      "const m = import(modPath)",
   264	      'const fs = require("node:" + name)',
   265	      "const r = require(pathVar)",
   266	    ]) {
   267	      expect(caughtByWalkRules(planted), `non-literal specifier slipped the catch: ${planted}`).toBe(
   268	        true,
   269	      );
   270	    }
   271	  });
   272	});

    15	/**
    16	 * C10 honesty surface (plan §4 C10; P3-6 W1 gate advisory) — machine-checked:
    17	 *  (a) NO real-platform-access / "no-AI" claim appears in pack sources, the CLI,
    18	 *      or the fixture READMEs (an affirmative-overclaim grep-gate);

    22	 * The banned patterns match ONLY affirmative overclaims — honest negations

   136	const BANNED_CLAIMS: readonly RegExp[] = [
   137	  /\bno AI was used\b/i,
   138	  /\bAI built this\b/i,
   139	  /\bbuilt (?:entirely )?by AI\b/i,
   140	  /\breal[- ](?:time )?(?:DoorDash|Square|Uber\s?Eats|Grubhub)\s+(?:data|access|feed|account|integration|API)/i,
   141	  /\b(?:connected|integrated) to (?:DoorDash|Square|Uber|Grubhub|the (?:live )?marketplace)\b/i,
   142	  /\bactual (?:DoorDash|Square|marketplace|merchant) (?:data|account|orders?)\b/i,
   143	  /\bproduction (?:merchant|platform|marketplace) data\b/i,
   144	  /\bwe have (?:real |live )?(?:platform |marketplace )?access\b/i,
   145	];
   146	
   147	// F-08: one ISOLATED planted claim per banned pattern (never combined under a
   148	// single `some()`), so a pattern that silently stopped matching is caught on its
   149	// own — a combined probe would stay green as long as ANY one pattern still bit.
   150	const BANNED_PROBES: ReadonlyArray<readonly [RegExp, string]> = [
   151	  [BANNED_CLAIMS[0], "no AI was used to build this"],
   152	  [BANNED_CLAIMS[1], "AI built this dashboard"],
   153	  [BANNED_CLAIMS[2], "this was built entirely by AI"],
   154	  [BANNED_CLAIMS[3], "the page shows real-time DoorDash data"],
   155	  [BANNED_CLAIMS[4], "the app is connected to DoorDash"],
   156	  [BANNED_CLAIMS[5], "displays actual DoorDash orders"],
   157	  [BANNED_CLAIMS[6], "renders production platform data"],
   158	  [BANNED_CLAIMS[7], "we have live platform access"],
   159	];
   160	
   161	describe("C10 platform-claims grep-gate (no real-platform-access claims)", () => {
   162	  it.each(scannedFiles)("%s makes no real-platform-access / no-AI claim", (file) => {
   163	    const text = readFileSync(file, "utf8");
   164	    for (const pattern of BANNED_CLAIMS) {
   165	      const m = text.match(pattern);
   166	      expect(m === null, `banned claim ${pattern} in ${file}: ${m?.[0]}`).toBe(true);
   167	    }
   168	  });
   169	
   170	  it("every banned pattern has exactly one dedicated probe (kept in lockstep)", () => {
   171	    expect(BANNED_PROBES.length).toBe(BANNED_CLAIMS.length);
   172	    BANNED_PROBES.forEach(([p], i) => expect(p).toBe(BANNED_CLAIMS[i]));
   173	  });
   174	
   175	  it.each(BANNED_PROBES)(
   176	    "the gate bites in isolation: %s matches its own planted claim",
   177	    (pattern, planted) => {
   178	      expect(pattern.test(planted), `pattern ${pattern} failed to catch "${planted}"`).toBe(true);
   179	    },
   180	  );
   181	});
   182	
   183	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   184	// SITE-WIDE (F-07): the fixed source allowlist above cannot cover every rendered
   185	// surface (the new landing components, /cost, /eval, /metrics, /legacy/**, the
   186	// redirect stubs, 404 …). The AUTHORITATIVE site-wide gate scans the NORMALIZED
   187	// VISIBLE TEXT of every built out/**/*.html — a false claim on ANY route is
   188	// caught, and a claim split across JSX elements/lines (which a raw-source regex
   189	// misses, F-08) is rejoined by tag-stripping before the scan. Rendered HTML has
   190	// no code comments, so this is comprehensive without benign false positives.
   191	//
   192	// Freshness: this runs against a built out/ and REFUSES a stale one (phase-F
   193	// batch finding #9 — accepting any existing out/ made the site-wide gate
   194	// fail-open: edited source scanned yesterday's HTML). If out/ is absent OR older
   195	// than the newest page source under app/, components/, or lib/, it rebuilds ONCE
   196	// (self-sufficient — never a silently-skipped honesty gate). `npm run verify`
   197	// builds BEFORE the test run for the same reason, so there this is instant.
   198	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   199	
   200	const outDir = join(root, "out");
   201	
   202	function newestMtimeMs(dir: string): number {
   203	  let newest = 0;
   204	  for (const entry of readdirSync(dir, { withFileTypes: true })) {
   205	    const full = join(dir, entry.name);
   206	    if (entry.isDirectory()) newest = Math.max(newest, newestMtimeMs(full));
   207	    else newest = Math.max(newest, statSync(full).mtimeMs);
   208	  }
   209	  return newest;
   210	}
   211	
   212	function ensureBuilt(): void {
   213	  if (existsSync(outDir)) {
   214	    const built = statSync(join(outDir, "index.html")).mtimeMs;
   215	    const newestSource = Math.max(
   216	      newestMtimeMs(join(root, "app")),
   217	      newestMtimeMs(join(root, "components")),
   218	      newestMtimeMs(join(root, "lib")),
   219	    );
   220	    if (built >= newestSource) return;
   221	  }
   222	  execSync("npm run build", { cwd: root, stdio: "inherit" });
   223	}
   224	
   225	function htmlFiles(dir: string): string[] {
   226	  const files: string[] = [];
   227	  for (const entry of readdirSync(dir, { withFileTypes: true })) {
   228	    const full = join(dir, entry.name);
   229	    if (entry.isDirectory()) files.push(...htmlFiles(full));
   230	    else if (entry.name.endsWith(".html")) files.push(full);
   231	  }
   232	  return files;
   233	}
   234	
   235	/**
   236	 * Reduce a built HTML page to its normalized VISIBLE text: drop <script>/<style>
   237	 * blocks (embedded data, not visible copy), strip every tag (rejoining a claim
   238	 * split across elements/lines), decode a few entities, and collapse whitespace.
   239	 */
   240	export function visibleText(html: string): string {
   241	  return html
   242	    .replace(/<script\b[\s\S]*?<\/script>/gi, " ")
   243	    .replace(/<style\b[\s\S]*?<\/style>/gi, " ")
   244	    .replace(/<[^>]+>/g, " ")
   245	    .replace(/&amp;/g, "&")
   246	    .replace(/&mdash;/g, "—")
   247	    .replace(/&rsquo;|&#39;|&apos;/g, "'")
   248	    .replace(/&ldquo;|&rdquo;|&quot;/g, '"')
   249	    .replace(/&nbsp;/g, " ")
   250	    .replace(/&sect;/g, "§")
   251	    .replace(/\s+/g, " ")
   252	    .trim();
   253	}
   254	
   255	describe("C10 SITE-WIDE banned-claim scan (normalized visible text of every built out/**/*.html)", () => {
   256	  it(
   257	    "no rendered page carries a banned real-platform / no-AI claim",
   258	    { timeout: 600_000 },
   259	    () => {
   260	      ensureBuilt();
   261	      const pages = htmlFiles(outDir);
   262	      expect(pages.length, "out/ has no HTML pages — did the build run?").toBeGreaterThan(0);
   263	
   264	      const violations: string[] = [];
   265	      for (const file of pages) {
   266	        const text = visibleText(readFileSync(file, "utf8"));
   267	        for (const pattern of BANNED_CLAIMS) {
   268	          const m = text.match(pattern);
   269	          if (m) violations.push(`${pattern} in ${relative(root, file)}: "${m[0]}"`);
   270	        }
   271	      }
   272	      expect(violations, `banned claims rendered on the built site:\n${violations.join("\n")}`).toEqual(
   273	        [],
   274	      );
   275	    },
   276	  );
   277	
   278	  it("the visible-text normalizer rejoins a claim split across JSX / lines (F-08)", () => {
   279	    // A claim broken across elements + newlines in source becomes contiguous in
   280	    // rendered HTML; tag-stripping + whitespace collapse must rejoin it so the
   281	    // pattern still matches (a raw per-line source scan would have missed it).
   282	    const split = '<p>the app is\n  connected\n  to <span class="brand">DoorDash</span>.</p>';
   283	    const text = visibleText(split);
   284	    expect(text).toContain("connected to DoorDash");
   285	    expect(BANNED_CLAIMS.some((p) => p.test(text))).toBe(true);
   286	  });
   287	});

   375	describe("footer is disclaimer-free + honest (app/layout.tsx, freeze-reversal 2026-07-14)", () => {
   376	  const layoutSrc = readFileSync(join(root, "app", "layout.tsx"), "utf8");
   377	
   378	  // Bind to the FOOTER BLOCK specifically (not the whole file), with JSX comments
   379	  // stripped, so nothing outside the rendered footer satisfies or defeats the gate.
   380	  const footerMatch = layoutSrc.match(/<footer[\s\S]*?<\/footer>/);
   381	  const footerRaw = footerMatch?.[0] ?? "";
   382	
   383	  const normalizeJsx = (s: string) =>
   384	    s
   385	      .replace(/\{\/\*[\s\S]*?\*\/\}/g, " ")
   386	      .replace(/<[^>]+>/g, " ")
   387	      .replace(/\{["'` ]*\}/g, " ")
   388	      .replace(/&ldquo;|&rdquo;/g, '"')
   389	      .replace(/&rsquo;/g, "'")
   390	      .replace(/\s+/g, " ");
   391

   393

   409	      true,
   410	    );
   411	  });

   423	  it.each(REMOVED)("the footer no longer carries: %s", (_name, pattern) => {

   425	  });
