    21	/** Deterministic mojibake transform (UTF-8 bytes read as Latin-1) — the
    22	 * canonical double-encoding corruption the encoding detector recognizes. */
    23	export function mojibake(s: string): string {
    24	  return Array.from(new TextEncoder().encode(s), (b) => String.fromCharCode(b)).join("");
    25	}
    26	
    27	function isSorTruth(v: unknown): v is SorTruth {
    28	  return typeof v === "object" && v !== null && "variation" in v && "item" in v;
    29	}
    30	
    31	/** All per-claim rules. Dispatch is by claim.field; unmatched claims are the
    32	 * completeness sweep's job, so every rule here requires a resolved match. */
    33	export const listingsDetectors: readonly Detector[] = [
    34	  // availability/hidden — any served row whose truth row is hidden (fires once,
    35	  // on the row's existence claim).
    36	  (claim, match) => {
    37	    if (claim.field !== "existence" || !match || !isSorTruth(match.value)) return [];
    38	    if (match.value.variation.stock !== "hidden") return [];
    39	    return [
    40	      {
    41	        claim,
    42	        referenceRowId: match.referenceRowId,
    43	        ruleId: "LST-AVAIL-HIDDEN-SHOWN",
    44	        severity: "error",
    45	        category: "availability",
    46	        plainLine: "This item is hidden in the merchant's own catalog but is being served to customers.",
    47	      } satisfies FindingInput,
    48	    ];
    49	  },
    50	
    51	  // title — encoding drifts first (mojibake, truncation), then generic identity.
    52	  (claim, match) => {
    53	    if (claim.field !== "title" || !match || !isSorTruth(match.value)) return [];
    54	    const expected = expectedTitle(match.value.item, match.value.variation);
    55	    const actual = String(claim.value);
    56	    if (actual === expected) return [];
    57	    const base = { claim, referenceRowId: match.referenceRowId } as const;
    58	    if (actual === mojibake(expected)) {
    59	      return [
    60	        {
    61	          ...base,
    62	          ruleId: "LST-ENC-UTF8",
    63	          severity: "warn",
    64	          category: "encoding",
    65	          plainLine: `The name is garbled by a text-encoding error: served "${actual}" vs the real "${expected}".`,
    66	        },
    67	      ];
    68	    }
    69	    if (actual.endsWith("…") && expected.startsWith(actual.slice(0, -1))) {
    70	      return [
    71	        {
    72	          ...base,
    73	          ruleId: "LST-ENC-TRUNC",
    74	          severity: "warn",
    75	          category: "encoding",
    76	          plainLine: `The name is cut off in the feed: "${actual}" vs the real "${expected}".`,
    77	        },
    78	      ];
    79	    }
    80	    return [
    81	      {
    82	        ...base,
    83	        ruleId: "LST-IDENT-NAME",
    84	        severity: "error",
    85	        category: "identity",
    86	        plainLine: `The served name "${actual}" does not match the catalog name "${expected}".`,
    87	      },
    88	    ];
    89	  },
    90	
    91	  // price.amount — cents-vs-decimal recognized specifically, else value drift.
    92	  (claim, match) => {
    93	    if (claim.field !== "price.amount" || !match || !isSorTruth(match.value)) return [];
    94	    const cents = match.value.variation.priceCents;
    95	    const expected = centsToDecimal(cents);
    96	    const actual = String(claim.value);
    97	    if (actual === expected) return [];
    98	    const base = { claim, referenceRowId: match.referenceRowId } as const;
    99	    if (actual === String(cents)) {
   100	      return [
   101	        {
   102	          ...base,
   103	          ruleId: "LST-PRICE-CENTS-AS-DECIMAL",
   104	          severity: "error",
   105	          category: "price",
   106	          plainLine: `The price is serialized in cents (${actual}) where dollars are expected (${expected}) — a 100× overstatement.`,
   107	        },
   108	      ];
   109	    }
   110	    return [
   111	      {
   112	        ...base,
   113	        ruleId: "LST-PRICE-VALUE",
   114	        severity: "error",
   115	        category: "price",
   116	        plainLine: `The served price ${actual} does not match the catalog price ${expected}.`,
   117	      },
   118	    ];
   119	  },
   120	
   121	  // price.currency — exact ISO-4217 match required; case-mangled recognized.
   122	  (claim, match) => {
   123	    if (claim.field !== "price.currency" || !match || !isSorTruth(match.value)) return [];
   124	    const expected = match.value.catalog.currency;
   125	    const actual = String(claim.value);
   126	    if (actual === expected) return [];
   127	    const base = { claim, referenceRowId: match.referenceRowId } as const;
   128	    if (actual.toUpperCase() === expected) {
   129	      return [
   130	        {
   131	          ...base,
   132	          ruleId: "LST-PRICE-CURRENCY-FORM",
   133	          severity: "warn",
   134	          category: "price",
   135	          plainLine: `The currency code is malformed: "${actual}" instead of ISO-4217 "${expected}".`,
   136	        },
   137	      ];
   138	    }
   139	    return [
   140	      {
   141	        ...base,
   142	        ruleId: "LST-PRICE-CURRENCY",
   143	        severity: "error",
   144	        category: "price",
   145	        plainLine: `The served currency "${actual}" does not match the catalog currency "${expected}".`,
   146	      },
   147	    ];
   148	  },
   149	
   150	  // availability — served state must map from the SOR stock state.
   151	  (claim, match) => {
   152	    if (claim.field !== "availability" || !match || !isSorTruth(match.value)) return [];
   153	    const stock = match.value.variation.stock;
   154	    if (stock === "hidden") return []; // LST-AVAIL-HIDDEN-SHOWN owns hidden rows.
   155	    const expected = stock === "soldout_86" ? "out_of_stock" : "in_stock";
   156	    const actual = String(claim.value);
   157	    if (actual === expected) return [];
   158	    return [
   159	      {
   160	        claim,
   161	        referenceRowId: match.referenceRowId,
   162	        ruleId: "LST-AVAIL-STATE",
   163	        severity: "error",
   164	        category: "availability",
   165	        plainLine: `The copy says "${actual}" but the catalog state is ${stock === "soldout_86" ? "86'd/sold out" : "in stock"} (expected "${expected}").`,
   166	      },
   167	    ];
   168	  },
   169	
   170	  // staleness — expired expiration_date still being served.
   171	  (claim, match) => {
   172	    if (claim.field !== "expiration_date" || !match || !isSorTruth(match.value)) return [];
   173	    const asOf = match.value.catalog.asOf;
   174	    const actual = String(claim.value);
   175	    if (actual >= asOf) return []; // ISO-8601 strings compare lexicographically.
   176	    return [
   177	      {
   178	        claim,
   179	        referenceRowId: match.referenceRowId,
   180	        ruleId: "LST-STALE-EXPIRED",
   181	        severity: "warn",
   182	        category: "staleness",
   183	        plainLine: `This row expired ${actual} but is still served (catalog as-of ${asOf}).`,
   184	      },
   185	    ];
   186	  },
   187	
   188	  // staleness + conditional — pre_order rows must carry a future availability_date.
   189	  (claim, match) => {
   190	    if (claim.field !== "availability_date" || !match || !isSorTruth(match.value)) return [];
   191	    const asOf = match.value.catalog.asOf;
   192	    const base = { claim, referenceRowId: match.referenceRowId } as const;
   193	    if (claim.value === null) {
   194	      return [
   195	        {
   196	          ...base,
   197	          ruleId: "LST-XF-PREORDER-DATE-MISSING",
   198	          severity: "error",
   199	          category: "cross-field-invariant",
   200	          plainLine: "The row is pre_order but carries no availability_date (required-if conditional violated).",
   201	        },
   202	      ];
   203	    }
   204	    const actual = String(claim.value);
   205	    if (actual >= asOf) return [];
   206	    return [
   207	      {
   208	        ...base,
   209	        ruleId: "LST-STALE-AVAILDATE",
   210	        severity: "warn",
   211	        category: "staleness",
   212	        plainLine: `The pre-order availability date ${actual} already passed (catalog as-of ${asOf}) — the promise is stale.`,
   213	      },
   214	    ];
   215	  },
   216	
   217	  // identity — the variant label must identify THIS variation.
   218	  (claim, match) => {
   219	    if (claim.field !== "variant_dict" || !match || !isSorTruth(match.value)) return [];
   220	    const dictValue = claim.value as { readonly dict?: Readonly<Record<string, string>> };
   221	    const label = dictValue.dict?.["variation"];
   222	    const expected = match.value.variation.name;
   223	    if (label === expected) return [];
   224	    return [
   225	      {
   226	        claim,
   227	        referenceRowId: match.referenceRowId,
   228	        ruleId: "LST-IDENT-MODIFIER-AMBIG",
   229	        severity: "error",
   230	        category: "identity",
   231	        plainLine: `The variant label says "${label ?? "(none)"}" but this row is the "${expected}" variation — variants of this item can no longer be told apart.`,
   232	      },
   233	    ];
   234	  },
   235	
   236	  // cross-field — extracted ACP invariants (sale_price ≤ price is a PRICE-class drift per §7).
   237	  (claim, match) => {
   238	    if (claim.field !== "invariants" || !match) return [];
   239	    const v = claim.value as AcpInvariantsValue;
   240	    const out: FindingInput[] = [];
   241	    const base = { claim, referenceRowId: match.referenceRowId } as const;
   242	    if (v.sale_price !== undefined && Number(v.sale_price) > Number(v.price)) {
   243	      out.push({
   244	        ...base,
   245	        ruleId: "LST-PRICE-SALE-GT",
   246	        severity: "error",
   247	        category: "price",
   248	        plainLine: `The sale price ${v.sale_price} is higher than the regular price ${v.price} — a "sale" that costs more.`,
   249	      });
   250	    }
   251	    if (v.is_eligible_checkout && !v.is_eligible_search) {
   252	      out.push({
   253	        ...base,
   254	        ruleId: "LST-XF-CHECKOUT-SEARCH",
   255	        severity: "error",
   256	        category: "cross-field-invariant",
   257	        plainLine: "The row is checkout-eligible but not search-eligible — the extracted invariant requires checkout ⇒ search.",
   258	      });
   259	    }
   260	    return out;
   261	  },
   262	
   263	  // spec-version skew — the responder's supported versions must include the pin.
   264	  (claim, match) => {
   265	    if (claim.field !== "spec.supported_versions" || !match) return [];
   266	    const pinned = (match.value as { readonly pinnedSpecVersion: string }).pinnedSpecVersion;
   267	    const versions = claim.value as readonly string[];
   268	    if (versions.includes(pinned)) return [];
   269	    return [
   270	      {
   271	        claim,
   272	        referenceRowId: match.referenceRowId,
   273	        ruleId: "LST-SPECVER-MISMATCH",
   274	        severity: "warn",
   275	        category: "spec-version-skew",
   276	        plainLine: `The serving surface speaks spec version(s) [${versions.join(", ")}] but the verifier pins ${pinned} — field semantics may have skewed.`,
   277	      },
   278	    ];
   279	  },
   280	];
     1	/**
     2	 * Surface adapters — W1 (C3: "one comparator, two adapters").
     3	 *
     4	 * Each adapter normalizes a serving surface into the SAME claim language
     5	 * (field paths + value forms), so the comparator and detectors are truly
     6	 * surface-agnostic: the ACP static feed and the constructed UCP catalog
     7	 * response produce comparable claims, and the C3 differential test can assert
     8	 * the same drift is caught from both.
     9	 *
    10	 * Claim id convention: `<servingRowId>#<fieldPath>`. Row-level context needed by
    11	 * cross-field rules travels on a dedicated `invariants` claim; group-level
    12	 * variant ambiguity travels on `variant_dict` claims.
    13	 *
    14	 * Plain: two translators turn "what the feed says" and "what the live answer
    15	 * says" into the same list of checkable statements, so one referee can judge
    16	 * both.
    17	 */
    18	import type { Claim } from "../../verifier-core/index.ts";
    19	import type { AcpFeed, AcpFeedItem } from "./acp-feed.ts";
    20	import type { UcpCatalogResponseFixture } from "./ucp.ts";
    21	
    22	/** Cross-field tuple carried by the per-row `invariants` claim (ACP surface). */
    23	export interface AcpInvariantsValue {
    24	  readonly price: string;
    25	  readonly sale_price?: string;
    26	  readonly is_eligible_search: boolean;
    27	  readonly is_eligible_checkout: boolean;
    28	}
    29	
    30	function rowClaims(
    31	  source: Claim["source"],
    32	  rowId: string,
    33	  fields: readonly (readonly [string, unknown])[],
    34	): Claim[] {
    35	  return fields.map(([field, value]) => ({
    36	    id: `${rowId}#${field}`,
    37	    source,
    38	    field,
    39	    value,
    40	  }));
    41	}
    42	
    43	/** Normalize an ACP feed into claims. */
    44	export function acpFeedToClaims(feed: AcpFeed): readonly Claim[] {
    45	  return feed.items.flatMap((r: AcpFeedItem) => {
    46	    const invariants: AcpInvariantsValue = {
    47	      price: r.price,
    48	      ...(r.sale_price !== undefined ? { sale_price: r.sale_price } : {}),
    49	      is_eligible_search: r.is_eligible_search,
    50	      is_eligible_checkout: r.is_eligible_checkout,
    51	    };
    52	    return rowClaims("acp-feed", r.item_id, [
    53	      ["existence", true],
    54	      ["title", r.title],
    55	      ["price.amount", r.price],
    56	      ["price.currency", r.currency],
    57	      ["availability", r.availability],
    58	      ["variant_dict", { group: r.group_id, dict: r.variant_dict }],
    59	      ...(r.expiration_date !== undefined
    60	        ? ([["expiration_date", r.expiration_date]] as const)
    61	        : []),
    62	      ...(r.availability === "pre_order"
    63	        ? ([["availability_date", r.availability_date ?? null]] as const)
    64	        : []),
    65	      ["invariants", invariants],
    66	    ]);
    67	  });
    68	}
    69	
    70	/**
    71	 * Normalize a (constructed) UCP catalog response into claims. Availability
    72	 * vocabulary is normalized to the shared enum (interpretation, documented:
    73	 * available → in_stock, unavailable → out_of_stock). The response-level
    74	 * `supported_versions` becomes a catalog-meta claim (§7 spec-version skew).
    75	 */
    76	export function ucpResponseToClaims(resp: UcpCatalogResponseFixture): readonly Claim[] {
    77	  const meta: Claim = {
    78	    id: "catalog#spec.supported_versions",
    79	    source: "ucp-catalog",
    80	    field: "spec.supported_versions",
    81	    value: resp.supported_versions,
    82	  };
    83	  const perItem = resp.items.flatMap((it) =>
    84	    rowClaims("ucp-catalog", it.id, [
    85	      ["existence", true],
    86	      ["title", it.title],
    87	      ["price.amount", it.price.amount],
    88	      ["price.currency", it.price.currency],
    89	      ["availability", it.availability === "available" ? "in_stock" : "out_of_stock"],
    90	      ["variant_dict", { group: it.group_id, dict: it.variant }],
    91	    ]),
    92	  );
    93	  return [meta, ...perItem];
    94	}
     1	/**
     2	 * ACP product-feed model + faithful-feed builder (W1).
     3	 *
     4	 * Field surface follows the PRIMARY-EXTRACTED ACP feed conformance surface
     5	 * (developers.openai.com/commerce product-feed spec, extracted 2026-07-02 —
     6	 * see docs/research/pivot-research-2026-07.md ADDENDUM): required fields
     7	 * (item_id ≤100 · title ≤150 · description ≤5000 · url · brand ≤70 · image_url ·
     8	 * price + ISO-4217 currency · availability enum · seller_name/seller_url ·
     9	 * is_eligible_search/is_eligible_checkout · target_countries/store_country),
    10	 * conditionals (availability_date iff pre_order; seller_privacy_policy +
    11	 * seller_tos iff checkout-eligible), cross-field invariants (sale_price ≤ price;
    12	 * is_eligible_checkout ⇒ is_eligible_search), variant grouping (group_id +
    13	 * variant_dict).
    14	 *
    15	 * HONESTY BOUNDS (C10, RULES §6):
    16	 * - The ACP spec is RETAIL-SHAPED (no menu/modifier model as of 2026-07-02); the
    17	 *   menu→feed mapping here is OUR INTERPRETATION, labeled, not spec text.
    18	 * - Price serialization as a decimal string + separate ISO-4217 currency field is
    19	 *   an INTERPRETATION (the extract pins "price + ISO-4217 currency", not an exact
    20	 *   wire format) — flagged UNVERIFIED-in-repo; re-extract before public claims.
    21	 * - Synthetic URLs use example.com and are NOT claimed to resolve (the spec's
    22	 *   url-must-resolve rule is a live-network check, out of the $0 offline wedge).
    23	 *
    24	 * Plain: this file turns the fake menu into the feed format AI agents read, the
    25	 * honest way — following the published field rules we extracted, and saying so
    26	 * where the menu world needed choices the retail spec doesn't make.
    27	 */
    28	import type { SyntheticCatalog } from "./types.ts";
    29	
    30	/** ACP availability enum — verbatim from the extracted spec surface. */
    31	export type AcpAvailability =
    32	  | "in_stock"
    33	  | "out_of_stock"
    34	  | "pre_order"
    35	  | "backorder"
    36	  | "unknown";
    37	
    38	/** One ACP feed row (extracted required fields + used conditionals/optionals). */
    39	export interface AcpFeedItem {
    40	  readonly item_id: string;
    41	  readonly title: string;
    42	  readonly description: string;
    43	  readonly url: string;
    44	  readonly brand: string;
    45	  readonly image_url: string;
    46	  /** Decimal-string amount (interpretation — see header) + ISO-4217 currency. */
    47	  readonly price: string;
    48	  readonly currency: string;
    49	  readonly availability: AcpAvailability;
    50	  readonly seller_name: string;
    51	  readonly seller_url: string;
    52	  readonly is_eligible_search: boolean;
    53	  readonly is_eligible_checkout: boolean;
    54	  readonly target_countries: readonly string[];
    55	  readonly store_country: string;
    56	  /** Variant grouping (extracted): all variations of one item share group_id. */
    57	  readonly group_id: string;
    58	  readonly variant_dict: Readonly<Record<string, string>>;
    59	  /** Conditional: required iff pre_order (extracted). */
    60	  readonly availability_date?: string;
    61	  /** Conditional: required iff checkout-eligible (extracted). */
    62	  readonly seller_privacy_policy?: string;
    63	  readonly seller_tos?: string;
    64	  /** Optional (extracted as present in spec): sale price and freshness stamps. */
    65	  readonly sale_price?: string;
    66	  readonly expiration_date?: string;
    67	  readonly updated_at?: string;
    68	}
    69	
    70	/** A full feed document — labeled simulated (C10). */
    71	export interface AcpFeed {
    72	  readonly simulated: true;
    73	  readonly spec: "acp-product-feed/extract-2026-07-02";
    74	  readonly items: readonly AcpFeedItem[];
    75	}
    76	
    77	/** Integer cents → decimal-string dollars ("1899" → "18.99"). */
    78	export function centsToDecimal(cents: number): string {
    79	  return (cents / 100).toFixed(2);
    80	}
    81	
    82	/**
    83	 * Build the FAITHFUL feed: one row per non-hidden SOR variation, sharing the SOR
    84	 * variation id as item_id (synthetic-controlled matching, C3). Mapping choices
    85	 * (documented interpretations): hidden variations are excluded from the feed;
    86	 * soldout_86 maps to out_of_stock; every row is search- and checkout-eligible
    87	 * with the conditional seller policy fields therefore present.
    88	 */
    89	export function buildFaithfulFeed(sor: SyntheticCatalog): AcpFeed {
    90	  const sellerUrl = "https://example.com/curbside-commons-simulated";
    91	  const items = sor.items.flatMap((item) =>
    92	    item.variations
    93	      .filter((v) => v.stock !== "hidden")
    94	      .map((v): AcpFeedItem => {
    95	        const variantDict: Record<string, string> = { variation: v.name };
    96	        return {
    97	          item_id: v.id,
    98	          title: item.variations.length > 1 ? `${item.name} (${v.name})` : item.name,
    99	          description: item.description,
   100	          url: `${sellerUrl}/items/${item.id}`,
   101	          brand: sor.merchantName,
   102	          image_url: `${sellerUrl}/img/${item.id}.png`,
   103	          price: centsToDecimal(v.priceCents),
   104	          currency: sor.currency,
   105	          availability: v.stock === "soldout_86" ? "out_of_stock" : "in_stock",
   106	          seller_name: sor.merchantName,
   107	          seller_url: sellerUrl,
   108	          is_eligible_search: true,
   109	          is_eligible_checkout: true,
   110	          target_countries: ["US"],
   111	          store_country: "US",
   112	          group_id: item.id,
   113	          variant_dict: variantDict,
   114	          seller_privacy_policy: `${sellerUrl}/privacy`,
   115	          seller_tos: `${sellerUrl}/tos`,
   116	          updated_at: sor.asOf,
   117	        };
   118	      }),
   119	  );
   120	  return { simulated: true, spec: "acp-product-feed/extract-2026-07-02", items };
   121	}

     3	/**
     4	 * The two-file audit workbench — the working tool (owner commission
     5	 * 2026-07-27: "I WANT WORKING WEBSITE WHERE I WILL UPLOAD TEST FILES").
     6	 *
     7	 * WHAT CHANGED AND WHY. The previous surface took ONE file (a feed) and
     8	 * checked it against a catalog compiled into the page. That is a demonstration
     9	 * of a rule, not an audit of your data: upload your own feed and nearly every
    10	 * row comes back "unknown or missing", because your items were never in our
    11	 * five illustrative records. A real audit takes BOTH sides — the claims and
    12	 * the records they are checked against — which is exactly what the CLI has
    13	 * always done (`runCheck(feedPath, catalogPath, surface)`, cli.ts:37).
    14	 *
    15	 * HONESTY OF THE REPORT HEADER. When the record side is the reader's own, the
    16	 * run is no longer ours and no longer matched by ids we minted, so the report
    17	 * must not say it was. That is slice 1: `verifyAcpFeed` passes the provenance
    18	 * describing the actual run (verify-in-browser.ts). Without it this component
    19	 * would stamp a reader's real records with the committed corpus's own labels —
    20	 * a claim broader than the thing backing it, the exact defect class this repo
    21	 * has caught six times across sessions 34-35.
    22	 *
    23	 * (Lab vocabulary is deliberately absent from this file, comments included:
    24	 * every byte here ships in the client bundle, and the C10 gate scans the
    25	 * source, not just the rendered text.)
    26	 *
    27	 * ZERO NETWORK, STILL. Every byte is read with FileReader and verified in this
    28	 * tab. Nothing is uploaded anywhere — "upload" here means "into the page", and
    29	 * the import-graph guard proves the closure cannot reach a network at all.
    30	 */

    45	interface SlotState {
    46	  readonly text: string;
    47	  readonly fileName: string | null;
    48	  readonly status: SlotStatus | null;
    49	}
    50	
    51	const EMPTY: SlotState = { text: "", fileName: null, status: null };
    52	
    53	interface RunState {
    54	  readonly report: VerifierReport;
    55	  /** True when the record side was the reader's own upload. */
    56	  readonly readerRecords: boolean;
    57	  readonly feedRows: number;
    58	  readonly recordRows: number;
    59	}
    60	
    61	function severityCounts(report: VerifierReport) {
    62	  const counts = { error: 0, warn: 0, info: 0 };
    63	  for (const f of report.findings) counts[f.severity] += 1;
    64	  return counts;
    65	}
    66	
    67	export function AuditWorkbench() {
    68	  const [feed, setFeed] = useState<SlotState>(EMPTY);
    69	  const [record, setRecord] = useState<SlotState>(EMPTY);
    70	  const [run, setRun] = useState<RunState | null>(null);
    71	  const [runError, setRunError] = useState<string | null>(null);
    72	
    73	  /** Any new input invalidates the verdict on screen — a stale verdict beside
    74	   *  changed inputs is a lie the reader has no way to detect. */
    75	  function invalidate() {
    76	    setRun(null);
    77	    setRunError(null);
    78	  }
    79	
    80	  function feedText(text: string, fileName: string | null) {
    81	    invalidate();
    82	    const parsed = parseAcpFeedText(text);
    83	    setFeed({
    84	      text,
    85	      fileName,
    86	      status: !text.trim()
    87	        ? null
    88	        : parsed.ok
    89	          ? { kind: "ok", summary: `${parsed.feed.items.length} rows read` }
    90	          : { kind: "error", message: parsed.error },
    91	    });
    92	  }
    93	
    94	  function recordText(text: string, fileName: string | null) {
    95	    invalidate();
    96	    const parsed = parseCatalogText(text);
    97	    setRecord({
    98	      text,
    99	      fileName,
   100	      status: !text.trim()
   101	        ? null
   102	        : parsed.ok
   103	          ? { kind: "ok", summary: `${parsed.catalog.items.length} items read` }
   104	          : { kind: "error", message: parsed.error },
   105	    });
   106	  }
   107	
   108	  function runAudit() {
   109	    invalidate();
   110	    const parsedFeed = parseAcpFeedText(feed.text);
   111	    if (!parsedFeed.ok) {
   112	      setRunError(`The feed could not be read. ${parsedFeed.error}`);
   113	      return;
   114	    }
   115	
   116	    // An empty record slot is not an error — it means "check against the
   117	    // records we ship", which is the honest default and stays labelled as such.
   118	    let catalog: SyntheticCatalog = SOR_CATALOG;
   119	    let readerRecords = false;
   120	    if (record.text.trim()) {
   121	      const parsedCatalog = parseCatalogText(record.text);
   122	      if (!parsedCatalog.ok) {
   123	        setRunError(`The record could not be read. ${parsedCatalog.error}`);
   124	        return;
   125	      }
   126	      catalog = parsedCatalog.catalog;
   127	      readerRecords = true;
   128	    }
   129	
   130	    try {
   131	      setRun({
   132	        report: verifyAcpFeed(parsedFeed.feed, catalog),
   133	        readerRecords,
   134	        feedRows: parsedFeed.feed.items.length,
   135	        recordRows: catalog.items.length,
   136	      });
   137	    } catch (e) {
   138	      setRunError(
   139	        `The verifier could not process this pair: ${e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e)}`,
   140	      );
   141	    }
   142	  }
   143	
   144	  function downloadReport() {
   145	    if (run === null) return;
   146	    const blob = new Blob([`${JSON.stringify(run.report, null, 2)}\n`], {
   147	      type: "application/json",
   148	    });
   149	    const url = URL.createObjectURL(blob);
   150	    const a = document.createElement("a");
   151	    a.href = url;
   152	    a.download = "curbside-commons-report.json";
   153	    a.click();
   154	    URL.revokeObjectURL(url);
   155	  }
   156	
   157	  const counts = run ? severityCounts(run.report) : null;
   158	  const canRun = feed.text.trim().length > 0;
   159	
   160	  return (
   161	    <div className="wb">
   162	      <div className="wb-slots">
   163	        <FileDrop
   164	          title="The feed"
   165	          hint="What is served to the agent — the claims being made."
   166	          textareaLabel="Feed JSON"
   167	          value={feed.text}
   168	          onText={feedText}
   169	          onReadError={(m) => {
   170	            invalidate();
   171	            setFeed({ text: "", fileName: null, status: { kind: "error", message: m } });
   172	          }}
   173	          fileName={feed.fileName}
   174	          status={feed.status}
   175	          onLoadSample={() => feedText(sampleFeedText(), "sample-feed.json")}
   176	          sampleLabel="Use the sample feed"
   177	        />
   178	        <FileDrop
   179	          title="The record"
   180	          hint="The merchant's own catalog — what the claims are checked against."
   181	          textareaLabel="Catalog JSON"
   182	          value={record.text}
   183	          onText={recordText}
   184	          onReadError={(m) => {
   185	            invalidate();
   186	            setRecord({ text: "", fileName: null, status: { kind: "error", message: m } });
   187	          }}
   188	          fileName={record.fileName}
   189	          status={record.status}
   190	          onLoadSample={() => recordText(catalogSampleText(), "sample-catalog.json")}
   191	          sampleLabel="Use the sample catalog"
   192	        />
   193	      </div>
   194	
   195	      <div className="wb-run">
   196	        <button type="button" className="lp-btn primary" onClick={runAudit} disabled={!canRun}>
   197	          Run the audit
   198	        </button>
   199	        {/* Three states, three different sentences. A note that reads the same
   200	            whether or not the button works tells the reader nothing about why
   201	            it is waiting (screenshot review, 2026-07-27). */}
   202	        <p className="wb-run-note">
   203	          {!canRun
   204	            ? "Add a feed to run the audit — the record side is optional."
   205	            : record.text.trim()
   206	              ? "Your feed will be checked against your own records."
   207	              : "No record supplied, so your feed is checked against the sample catalog shipped with this site."}
   208	        </p>
   209	      </div>
   210	
   211	      {/* No-JS: the whole workbench runs in the reader's browser. Without
   212	          scripting there is nothing to run, so dead controls are hidden and the
   213	          requirement is stated plainly (the v9 defect class, session 26). */}
   214	      <noscript>
   215	        <style dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: ".wb-slots,.wb-run{display:none}" }} />
   216	        <p className="wb-hint">
   217	          The audit runs entirely in your browser and needs scripting turned on. Nothing runs on a
   218	          server either way.
   219	        </p>
   220	      </noscript>
   221	
   222	      {runError !== null && (
   223	        <div className="wb-error" role="alert">
   224	          <strong>No verdict.</strong> {runError}
   225	        </div>
   226	      )}
   227	
   228	      {run !== null && counts !== null && (
   229	        <section className="wb-result" aria-label="Audit result">
   230	          <header className="wb-verdict-head">
   231	            <p className={run.report.ok ? "wb-verdict ok" : "wb-verdict fail"}>
   232	              {run.report.ok ? "PASS" : "FAIL"}
   233	            </p>
   234	            <p className="wb-tally">
   235	              {run.report.findings.length} finding{run.report.findings.length === 1 ? "" : "s"} —{" "}
   236	              {counts.error} error · {counts.warn} warn · {counts.info} info
   237	            </p>
   238	            <button type="button" className="lp-btn ghost wb-dl" onClick={downloadReport}>
   239	              Download the report
   240	            </button>
   241	          </header>
   242	
   243	          <p className="wb-prov">
   244	            {run.feedRows} feed rows checked against {run.recordRows}{" "}
   245	            {run.readerRecords ? "of your own records" : "sample records"}, computed in your browser
   246	            just now — no AI calls, no network requests, nothing left this page.
   247	            {!run.readerRecords &&
   248	              " Items outside the sample records honestly read as unknown or missing."}
   249	          </p>
   250	
   251	          <dl className="wb-meta">
   252	            <div>
   253	              <dt>spec version</dt>
   254	              <dd className="wb-mono">{run.report.specVersion}</dd>
   255	            </div>
   256	            <div>
   257	              <dt>matching</dt>
   258	              <dd className="wb-mono">{run.report.matchingMode}</dd>
   259	            </div>
   260	            <div>
   261	              <dt>record side</dt>
   262	              <dd className="wb-mono">{run.readerRecords ? "your upload" : "sample catalog"}</dd>
   263	            </div>
   264	          </dl>
   265	
   266	          {run.report.findings.length > 0 ? (
   267	            <ol className="wb-findings">
   268	              {run.report.findings.map((f) => (
   269	                <li key={`${f.claim.id}:${f.ruleId}:${f.claim.field}`} className="wb-finding">
   270	                  <p className="wb-plain">
   271	                    <span className={`wb-sev ${f.severity}`}>{f.severity}</span>{" "}
   272	                    {cleanFinding(f.plainLine ?? "")}
   273	                  </p>
   274	                  <dl className="wb-receipts">
   275	                    <div>
   276	                      <dt>claim</dt>
   277	                      <dd className="wb-mono">
   278	                        {f.claim.id} · {f.claim.field}
   279	                      </dd>
   280	                    </div>
   281	                    <div>
   282	                      <dt>asserted</dt>
   283	                      <dd className="wb-mono">{JSON.stringify(f.claim.value)}</dd>
   284	                    </div>
   285	                    <div>
   286	                      <dt>checked against</dt>
   287	                      <dd className="wb-mono">{f.referenceRowId}</dd>
   288	                    </div>
   289	                    <div>
   290	                      <dt>rule</dt>
   291	                      <dd className="wb-mono">{f.ruleId}</dd>
   292	                    </div>
   293	                  </dl>
   294	                </li>
   295	              ))}
   296	            </ol>
   297	          ) : (
   298	            <p className="wb-clean">
   299	              No drift detected — every claim in this feed agrees with the records it was checked
   300	              against.
   301	            </p>
   302	          )}
   303	        </section>
   304	      )}
   305	    </div>
   306	  );
   307	}
     1	"use client";
     2	
     3	/**
     4	 * One upload slot — drag-drop, click-to-browse, or paste (owner commission
     5	 * 2026-07-27, "a working website where I will upload test files").
     6	 *
     7	 * THE PROMISE THIS COMPONENT MUST NOT BREAK. /playground states on its face:
     8	 * "No network requests — nothing pasted leaves the page." A file input keeps
     9	 * that promise only if the file is read LOCALLY. `FileReader` is a browser API
    10	 * that never touches the network, and this module imports nothing that could:
    11	 * evals/packs/import-walk-guard.test.ts walks the import graph of the
    12	 * playground closure and asserts no node:fs and no fetch reaches it. The
    13	 * upload path is therefore zero-network BY CONSTRUCTION, not by promise.
    14	 *
    15	 * ACCESSIBILITY. A drop zone that is only a drop zone excludes every reader
    16	 * who cannot drag. The real control here is a native <input type="file">,
    17	 * kept in the accessibility tree and driven by its own <label> — so keyboard,
    18	 * screen reader, and pointer all reach the same function. The drag surface is
    19	 * an ENHANCEMENT layered on top; dragging is never the only way in. The paste
    20	 * textarea is the third route, and the one that needs no file at all.
    21	 *
    22	 * TWO CHANNELS, NEVER ONE. Content and failure travel separately: `onText`
    23	 * carries what the reader supplied, `onReadError` carries why nothing arrived.
    24	 * Encoding a failure as content (a sentinel string, a leading space) is the
    25	 * shape this product exists to catch — a value that means something other than
    26	 * what it says.
    27	 *
    28	 * WHY THE FILE NAME IS SHOWN. A two-slot tool whose slots look alike is a tool
    29	 * you can load backwards. Echoing the file name and its parsed row count is
    30	 * how a reader catches "I dropped the feed into the record slot" before the
    31	 * verdict confuses them.
    32	 */
    33	import { useId, useState } from "react";
    34	
    35	export type SlotStatus =
    36	  | { readonly kind: "ok"; readonly summary: string }
    37	  | { readonly kind: "error"; readonly message: string };
    38	
    39	export interface FileDropProps {
    40	  /** Slot heading — "The feed" / "The record". */
    41	  readonly title: string;
    42	  /** One line saying what this side IS, in the site's plain voice. */
    43	  readonly hint: string;
    44	  /** Accessible name for the paste textarea. */
    45	  readonly textareaLabel: string;
    46	  /** Current text held by the slot (lifted state: the parent owns the run). */
    47	  readonly value: string;
    48	  /** Reader supplied content — from a file, a paste, or the sample. */
    49	  readonly onText: (text: string, fileName: string | null) => void;
    50	  /** The file could not be read at all; no content is arriving. */
    51	  readonly onReadError: (message: string) => void;
    52	  /** Name of the file that filled this slot, if it came from one. */
    53	  readonly fileName: string | null;
    54	  /** Parse/read status, rendered inline so a failure sits beside its cause. */
    55	  readonly status: SlotStatus | null;
    56	  readonly onLoadSample: () => void;
    57	  readonly sampleLabel: string;
    58	}
    59	
    60	/** Refuse implausibly large files before reading them into memory. */
    61	const MAX_BYTES = 5 * 1024 * 1024;
    62	
    63	export function FileDrop({
    64	  title,
    65	  hint,
    66	  textareaLabel,
    67	  value,
    68	  onText,
    69	  onReadError,
    70	  fileName,
    71	  status,
    72	  onLoadSample,
    73	  sampleLabel,
    74	}: FileDropProps) {
    75	  const [dragging, setDragging] = useState(false);
    76	  const inputId = useId();
    77	  const areaId = useId();
    78	
    79	  function readFile(file: File) {
    80	    if (file.size > MAX_BYTES) {
    81	      onReadError(
    82	        `${file.name} is ${(file.size / 1024 / 1024).toFixed(1)} MB — larger than the 5 MB this tab will read.`,
    83	      );
    84	      return;
    85	    }
    86	    const reader = new FileReader();
    87	    reader.onload = () => onText(String(reader.result ?? ""), file.name);
    88	    reader.onerror = () => onReadError(`Could not read ${file.name}.`);
    89	    reader.readAsText(file);
    90	  }
    91	
    92	  return (
    93	    <div className="fd-slot">
    94	      <div className="fd-head">
    95	        <p className="fd-title">{title}</p>
    96	        <p className="fd-hint">{hint}</p>
    97	      </div>
    98	
    99	      {/* The drag surface decorates a real control; the <label> reaches the
   100	          native input, so a keyboard user takes the same path a mouse does. */}
   101	      <div
   102	        className={`fd-zone${dragging ? " is-drag" : ""}${fileName !== null ? " is-filled" : ""}`}
   103	        onDragOver={(e) => {
   104	          e.preventDefault();
   105	          setDragging(true);
   106	        }}
   107	        onDragLeave={() => setDragging(false)}
   108	        onDrop={(e) => {
   109	          e.preventDefault();
   110	          setDragging(false);
   111	          const file = e.dataTransfer.files?.[0];
   112	          if (file) readFile(file);
   113	        }}
   114	      >
   115	        <input
   116	          id={inputId}
   117	          className="fd-input"
   118	          type="file"
   119	          accept="application/json,.json,text/plain,.txt"
   120	          onChange={(e) => {
   121	            const file = e.target.files?.[0];
   122	            if (file) readFile(file);
   123	            // Reset so re-choosing the SAME file fires change again.
   124	            e.target.value = "";
   125	          }}
   126	        />
   127	        <label className="fd-label" htmlFor={inputId}>
   128	          <span className="fd-cta">Choose a file</span>
   129	          <span className="fd-or">or drag it here</span>
   130	        </label>
   131	        <button type="button" className="fd-sample" onClick={onLoadSample}>
   132	          {sampleLabel}
   133	        </button>
   134	      </div>
   135	
   136	      {fileName !== null && <p className="fd-file">{fileName}</p>}
   137	
   138	      {status !== null && (
   139	        <p
   140	          className={`fd-status ${status.kind}`}
   141	          role={status.kind === "error" ? "alert" : undefined}
   142	        >
   143	          {status.kind === "ok" ? status.summary : status.message}
   144	        </p>
   145	      )}
   146	
   147	      <details className="fd-paste">
   148	        <summary>or paste it as text</summary>
   149	        <label className="fd-paste-label" htmlFor={areaId}>
   150	          {textareaLabel}
   151	        </label>
   152	        <textarea
   153	          id={areaId}
   154	          className="fd-area"
   155	          spellCheck={false}
   156	          value={value}
   157	          onChange={(e) => onText(e.target.value, null)}
   158	        />
   159	      </details>
   160	    </div>
   161	  );
   162	}

evals/packs/playground-golden.test.ts:351:  it("an uploaded run cannot inherit the committed corpus's honesty label", () => {
evals/packs/playground-golden.test.ts:352:    // The C3/C10 labels describe the RUN. When the records are the reader's
evals/packs/playground-golden.test.ts:353:    // own, the report must not claim synthetic-controlled matching or a
evals/packs/playground-golden.test.ts:354:    // simulated corpus — see lib/packs/listings/run.ts (slice 1) and the
     1	/**
     2	 * Playground seam teeth (owner commission 2026-07-13) — the tests that make the
     3	 * /playground page's claims TRUE rather than asserted:
     4	 *
     5	 * 1. GOLDEN EQUALITY — the browser seam (components/playground/verify-in-browser.ts),
     6	 *    fed the committed drifted sample feed, reproduces the committed golden
     7	 *    fixtures/synthetic-restaurant/expected-report.acp.json BYTE-FOR-BYTE. This is
     8	 *    the exact equality the page claims on its face ("a committed test proves…").
     9	 * 2. THE GATE BITES — mutate one price in a clone of the sample feed and the
    10	 *    report changes (so #1 is not a vacuous comparison).
    11	 * 3. BROWSER SAFETY — walk the seam's transitive import graph (relative + "@/",
    12	 *    .ts/.tsx/.json) and assert no module reaches a node: builtin — the proof the
    13	 *    engine actually runs client-side rather than needing a server.
    14	 * 4. HONESTY LABELS — the page + client source carry the simulated framing, the
    15	 *    non-affiliation predicate, and the no-AI/no-network wording; the noscript
    16	 *    fallback exists and cites the committed golden's real tally.
    17	 */
    18	import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
    19	import { readFileSync, existsSync, statSync } from "node:fs";
    20	import { dirname, join, resolve } from "node:path";
    21	import {
    22	  SAMPLE_FEED,
    23	  parseAcpFeedText,
    24	  sampleFeedText,
    25	  verifyAcpFeed,
    26	} from "../../components/playground/verify-in-browser.ts";
    27	import { serializeReport } from "../../lib/verifier-core/verify.ts";
    28	import type { AcpFeed } from "../../lib/packs/listings/acp-feed.ts";
    29	
    30	const root = resolve(__dirname, "..", "..");
    31	const golden = readFileSync(
    32	  join(root, "fixtures", "synthetic-restaurant", "expected-report.acp.json"),
    33	  "utf8",
    34	);
    35	
    36	describe("playground golden equality (the page's central claim)", () => {
    37	  it("the browser seam reproduces the committed golden byte-for-byte for the sample feed", () => {
    38	    const report = verifyAcpFeed(SAMPLE_FEED);
    39	    expect(serializeReport(report)).toBe(golden);
    40	  });
    41	
    42	  it("the equality gate bites: a one-price mutation changes the report", () => {
    43	    const mutated = JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(SAMPLE_FEED)) as AcpFeed;
    44	    const first = mutated.items[0] as { price: unknown };
    45	    first.price = "999.99";
    46	    const report = verifyAcpFeed(mutated);
    47	    expect(serializeReport(report)).not.toBe(golden);
    48	  });
    49	
    50	  it("round-trips through the paste path: sampleFeedText → parse → verify == golden", () => {
    51	    const parsed = parseAcpFeedText(sampleFeedText());
    52	    expect(parsed.ok).toBe(true);
    53	    if (parsed.ok) {
    54	      expect(serializeReport(verifyAcpFeed(parsed.feed))).toBe(golden);
    55	    }
    56	  });
    57	
    58	  it("malformed input yields an honest error, never a verdict", () => {
    59	    expect(parseAcpFeedText("").ok).toBe(false);
    60	    expect(parseAcpFeedText("not json {").ok).toBe(false);
    61	    expect(parseAcpFeedText("[1,2,3]").ok).toBe(false);
    62	    expect(parseAcpFeedText('{"no_items": true}').ok).toBe(false);
    63	    const bad = parseAcpFeedText('{"no_items": true}');
    64	    if (!bad.ok) expect(bad.error).toMatch(/items/);
    65	  });
    66	
    67	  it("structurally broken rows are rejected with the row named (batch-F P2 fix)", () => {
    68	    // a null row would throw inside the adapter's property access
    69	    const nullRow = parseAcpFeedText('{"items":[null]}');
    70	    expect(nullRow.ok).toBe(false);
    71	    if (!nullRow.ok) expect(nullRow.error).toMatch(/items\[0\]/);
    72	    // an array row is not an object
    73	    expect(parseAcpFeedText('{"items":[[1]]}').ok).toBe(false);
    74	    // a row without a string item_id cannot name its findings
    75	    const noId = parseAcpFeedText('{"items":[{"title":"x"}]}');
    76	    expect(noId.ok).toBe(false);
    77	    if (!noId.ok) expect(noId.error).toMatch(/item_id/);
    78	    const numericId = parseAcpFeedText('{"items":[{"item_id":42}]}');
    79	    expect(numericId.ok).toBe(false);
    80	  });
    81	
    82	  it("wrong or missing FIELD VALUES still verify (findings, not errors)", () => {
    83	    // shape is readable (object + string item_id) — the engine's job is to
    84	    // report the drift, not to reject the row.
    85	    const sparse = parseAcpFeedText('{"items":[{"item_id":"sku-1","title":123}]}');
    86	    expect(sparse.ok).toBe(true);
    87	    if (sparse.ok) {
    88	      const report = verifyAcpFeed(sparse.feed);
    89	      expect(report.ok).toBe(false);
    90	      expect(report.findings.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
    91	    }
    92	  });
    93	});
    94	
    95	/**
    96	 * Browser-safety closure walk — FAIL-CLOSED (batch-F P2 fix: the first cut's
    97	 * single regex missed dynamic import(), require(), and import-equals, and
    98	 * silently skipped unresolved specifiers — a fail-open "proof". This version:
    99	 * matches every import form, checks the COMPLETE Node builtin list, fails on
   100	 * any unresolved or non-allowlisted specifier, and proves its own matcher
   101	 * bites on planted hidden forms.)
   102	 */
   103	import { builtinModules } from "node:module";
   104	
   105	const IMPORT_FORMS: readonly RegExp[] = [
   106	  // import ... from "x" / export ... from "x"
   107	  /(?:import|export)\s[^"'`]*?from\s*["']([^"']+)["']/g,
   108	  // side-effect import "x"
   109	  /import\s*["']([^"']+)["']/g,
   110	  // dynamic import("x")
   111	  /import\s*\(\s*["']([^"']+)["']\s*\)/g,
   112	  // require("x") — incl. TS import-equals `import x = require("x")`
   113	  /require\s*\(\s*["']([^"']+)["']\s*\)/g,
   114	];
   115	
   116	function extractSpecifiers(src: string): string[] {
   117	  const specs: string[] = [];
   118	  for (const re of IMPORT_FORMS) {
   119	    for (const m of src.matchAll(re)) specs.push(m[1]);
   120	  }
   121	  return specs;
   122	}
   123	
   124	/** Bare specifiers the browser bundle legitimately provides. Everything else fails. */
   125	// "@number-flow/react": vetted ADOPT-WITH-CONDITIONS 2026-07-16 (MIT, exact-pinned
   126	// 0.6.1; zero network/hooks verified in the repo AND the published tarballs incl.
   127	// the esm-env transitive; reduced-motion/ARIA floors verified) — first real use is
   128	// the /fees paste-leg tally. The covering batch re-probes the whole condition set.
   129	// "react-dom": the shipped FeesView.tsx imports { flushSync } from it (a client-only
   130	// React DOM API for the SSR-then-page tab flush) — browser-provided, same bundle as the
   131	// already-trusted "react-dom/" prefix; no node builtin is reachable through it.
   132	const BARE_ALLOWLIST = new Set(["react", "react-dom", "@number-flow/react"]);
   133	const BARE_PREFIX_ALLOWLIST = ["react/", "next/", "react-dom/"];
   134	const NODE_BUILTINS = new Set([...builtinModules, ...builtinModules.map((m) => `node:${m}`)]);
   135	
   136	describe("playground browser safety (fail-closed import-graph walk)", () => {
   137	  function resolveSpec(fromFile: string, spec: string): string | null {
   138	    let base: string;
   139	    if (spec.startsWith("@/")) base = join(root, spec.slice(2));
   140	    else if (spec.startsWith(".")) base = resolve(dirname(fromFile), spec);
   141	    else return null;
   142	    // FILE candidates only (fee-surface extension 2026-07-16): a bare `base`
   143	    // hit can be a DIRECTORY (a barrel import like "@/lib/packs/fees"), which
   144	    // the old order returned and then failed to read — resolve barrels to
   145	    // their index.ts and never return a directory.
   146	    for (const cand of [`${base}.ts`, `${base}.tsx`, `${base}.json`, join(base, "index.ts"), base]) {
   147	      if (existsSync(cand) && statSync(cand).isFile()) return cand;
   148	    }
   149	    return null;
   150	  }
   151	
   152	  function walkClosure(entries: string[]): { seen: Set<string>; offenders: string[] } {
   153	    const seen = new Set<string>();
   154	    const offenders: string[] = [];
   155	    function walk(file: string) {
   156	      if (seen.has(file)) return;
   157	      seen.add(file);
   158	      if (file.endsWith(".json")) return;
   159	      const src = readFileSync(file, "utf8");
   160	      // Batch P2 fix (2026-07-16): a dynamic import()/require() whose specifier
   161	      // is NOT a single quoted literal (template literal, variable, or a
   162	      // quoted-prefix CONCATENATION like require("node:" + x)) would slip the
   163	      // extraction regexes — count call sites vs FULL-literal calls and fail
   164	      // closed on any gap.
   165	      const dynCalls = (src.match(/\bimport\s*\(/g) ?? []).length;
   166	      const dynLiterals = (src.match(/\bimport\s*\(\s*["'][^"']*["']\s*\)/g) ?? []).length;
   167	      if (dynCalls > dynLiterals) {
   168	        offenders.push(`${file} contains a dynamic import with a non-literal specifier — fail closed`);
   169	      }
   170	      const reqCalls = (src.match(/\brequire\s*\(/g) ?? []).length;
   171	      const reqLiterals = (src.match(/\brequire\s*\(\s*["'][^"']*["']\s*\)/g) ?? []).length;
   172	      if (reqCalls > reqLiterals) {
   173	        offenders.push(`${file} contains a require() with a non-literal specifier — fail closed`);
   174	      }
   175	      for (const spec of extractSpecifiers(src)) {
   176	        if (NODE_BUILTINS.has(spec)) {
   177	          offenders.push(`${file} imports the Node builtin "${spec}"`);
   178	          continue;
   179	        }
   180	        if (spec.startsWith("@/") || spec.startsWith(".")) {
   181	          const resolved = resolveSpec(file, spec);
   182	          if (resolved === null) {
   183	            offenders.push(`${file} imports "${spec}" which did not resolve — fail closed`);
   184	            continue;
   185	          }
   186	          walk(resolved);
   187	          continue;
   188	        }
   189	        // bare specifier: allowlisted or a failure — never silently skipped
   190	        if (BARE_ALLOWLIST.has(spec) || BARE_PREFIX_ALLOWLIST.some((p) => spec.startsWith(p))) {
   191	          continue;
   192	        }
   193	        offenders.push(`${file} imports non-allowlisted bare specifier "${spec}" — fail closed`);
   194	      }
   195	    }
   196	    for (const e of entries) walk(e);
   197	    return { seen, offenders };
   198	  }
   199	
   200	  it("the seam's transitive closure reaches no Node builtin and nothing unresolved", () => {
   201	    const { seen, offenders } = walkClosure([
   202	      join(root, "components", "playground", "verify-in-browser.ts"),
   203	      // The upload surface (2026-07-27) joins the same closure: a file input
   204	      // is only zero-network if what reads it is. FileReader never touches a
   205	      // network, and this walk proves nothing in the workbench's import graph
   206	      // can either — the page's "nothing leaves this page" promise is
   207	      // structural, not a courtesy.
   208	      join(root, "components", "playground", "AuditWorkbench.tsx"),
   209	      join(root, "components", "playground", "FileDrop.tsx"),
   210	      // Fee surface (NYC showcase N1+N2, 2026-07-16): the fee seam + clients
   211	      // run in the same browser closure — same fail-closed proof.
   212	      join(root, "components", "fees", "audit-in-browser.ts"),
   213	      join(root, "components", "fees", "FeePlaygroundClient.tsx"),
   214	      join(root, "components", "fees", "FeesView.tsx"),
   215	      join(root, "components", "fees", "fee-report-data.ts"),
   216	    ]);
   217	    expect(offenders, offenders.join("\n")).toEqual([]);
   218	    // Sanity: the walk actually traversed the engine, not just the seam.
   219	    expect(seen.size).toBeGreaterThan(8);
   220	  });
   221	
   222	  it("the scanner itself bites: every hidden import form is detected", () => {
   223	    const hidden = [
   224	      'void import("node:fs")',
   225	      'const fs = require("node:fs")',
   226	      'import fs = require("node:fs")',
   227	      'export { x } from "node:crypto"',
   228	      'import "fs"',
   229	      'import { join } from "path"',
   230	    ];
   231	    for (const sample of hidden) {
   232	      const specs = extractSpecifiers(sample);
   233	      const caught = specs.some((s) => NODE_BUILTINS.has(s));
   234	      expect(caught, `scanner missed: ${sample} (extracted: ${JSON.stringify(specs)})`).toBe(true);
   235	    }
   236	    // and a non-allowlisted bare package would fail rather than be skipped
   237	    const bare = extractSpecifiers('import x from "left-pad"');
   238	    expect(bare).toContain("left-pad");
   239	    expect(BARE_ALLOWLIST.has("left-pad")).toBe(false);
   240	  });
   241	
   242	  it("the non-literal-specifier catch bites (template-literal / variable imports)", () => {
   243	    // Mirrors the walker's FULL decision: a planted non-literal form is caught
   244	    // either by the call-site counting gap, or by its extracted quoted PREFIX
   245	    // failing the bare-specifier allowlist (fail-closed both ways).
   246	    const caughtByWalkRules = (planted: string): boolean => {
   247	      const dynCalls = (planted.match(/\bimport\s*\(/g) ?? []).length;
   248	      const dynLiterals = (planted.match(/\bimport\s*\(\s*["'][^"']*["']\s*\)/g) ?? []).length;
   249	      const reqCalls = (planted.match(/\brequire\s*\(/g) ?? []).length;
   250	      const reqLiterals = (planted.match(/\brequire\s*\(\s*["'][^"']*["']\s*\)/g) ?? []).length;
   251	      if (dynCalls > dynLiterals || reqCalls > reqLiterals) return true;
   252	      return extractSpecifiers(planted).some(
   253	        (s) =>
   254	          !s.startsWith("@/") &&
   255	          !s.startsWith(".") &&
   256	          !NODE_BUILTINS.has(s) &&
   257	          !BARE_ALLOWLIST.has(s) &&
   258	          !BARE_PREFIX_ALLOWLIST.some((p) => s.startsWith(p)),
   259	      );
   260	    };
   261	    for (const planted of [
   262	      "void import(`./mods/${name}`)",
   263	      "const m = import(modPath)",
   264	      'const fs = require("node:" + name)',
   265	      "const r = require(pathVar)",
   266	    ]) {
   267	      expect(caughtByWalkRules(planted), `non-literal specifier slipped the catch: ${planted}`).toBe(
   268	        true,
   269	      );
   270	    }
   271	  });
   272	});
   273	
   274	describe("playground honesty labels (de-jargon Slice E — disclaimer-free + honest)", () => {
   275	  const pageSrc = readFileSync(join(root, "app", "playground", "page.tsx"), "utf8");
   276	  // REBOUND 2026-07-27 (the upload commission). These guards used to read
   277	  // components/playground/PlaygroundClient.tsx — the one-field paste leg. That
   278	  // component was DELETED when /playground became the two-file workbench, so
   279	  // the guards now read the surface that actually ships. Every invariant below
   280	  // is preserved verbatim; only the file they point at changed. A honesty
   281	  // guard aimed at a component the page no longer renders is worse than no
   282	  // guard: it reports green over an unexamined surface.
   283	  const clientSrc = readFileSync(
   284	    join(root, "components", "playground", "AuditWorkbench.tsx"),
   285	    "utf8",
   286	  );
   287	  const dropSrc = readFileSync(join(root, "components", "playground", "FileDrop.tsx"), "utf8");
   288	
   289	  // The public copy is disclaimer-free and jargon-free: the honest boundary is
   290	  // stated in plain product language ("illustrative"), NOT with lab-words or a
   291	  // non-affiliation disclaimer. These assertions are the red half — they FAIL
   292	  // against the pre-de-jargon copy.
   293	  it("the page states the honest boundary in plain, jargon-free language", () => {
   294	    // freeze-reversal 2026-07-20: the "illustrative" label requirement is retired; the
   295	    // rebuilt /playground states its honest boundary as the pinned-world copy — the
   296	    // merchant catalog of N records, out-of-catalog reads as unknown or missing.
   297	    // 2026-07-27: reworded from "reference world is …" to "sample catalog of N
   298	    // records", because the page now ALSO takes the reader's own records — a
   299	    // single fixed "reference world" stopped being true of every run on it.
   300	    expect(pageSrc).toMatch(/HONEST BOUNDARY/);
   301	    expect(pageSrc).toMatch(/checks against the sample catalog/);
   302	    expect(pageSrc).toMatch(/reads as unknown or missing/);
   303	    // No lab-words / removed disclaimer leak onto the public page.
   304	    expect(pageSrc).not.toMatch(/\bsimulated\b/i);
   305	    expect(pageSrc).not.toMatch(/\bsynthetic\b/i);
   306	    expect(pageSrc).not.toMatch(/\bgolden\b/i);
   307	    expect(pageSrc).not.toMatch(/Not\s+affiliated with/i);
   308	  });
   309	
   310	  it("the page states the no-AI / no-network boundary", () => {
   311	    expect(pageSrc).toMatch(/No AI calls/i);
   312	    expect(pageSrc).toMatch(/no network requests/i);
   313	  });
   314	
   315	  it("the noscript fallback exists and cites the real tally (jargon-free, no repo path)", () => {
   316	    // freeze-reversal 2026-07-20: the page no longer carries a single hardcoded noscript
   317	    // paragraph citing "16 findings (11 error / 5 warn)"; the bench SSRs the settled
   318	    // reference result so a no-JS reader gets the complete story, and PlaygroundClient
   319	    // keeps its own <noscript> for the paste leg. Rebound to those real surfaces — the
   320	    // byte-exact reference tally is proven by the golden-equality test above.
   321	    expect(pageSrc).toMatch(/<TryLiveBench \/>/);
   322	    expect(clientSrc).toMatch(/<noscript>/);
   323	    // The internal fixture path must NOT be cited on any public surface.
   324	    for (const src of [pageSrc, clientSrc, dropSrc]) {
   325	      expect(src).not.toMatch(/expected-report\.acp\.json/);
   326	      expect(src).not.toMatch(/fixtures\//);
   327	    }
   328	  });
   329	
   330	  it("the workbench names the record side of every run, in plain language", () => {
   331	    // 2026-07-27: the run's provenance is no longer one fixed sentence, because
   332	    // the record side is now a CHOICE. Whichever way the reader goes, the
   333	    // result must say which records the verdict was reached against — an
   334	    // unlabelled verdict is the one thing this surface may never produce.
   335	    expect(clientSrc).toMatch(/computed in your browser/i);
   336	    expect(clientSrc).toMatch(/of your own records/);
   337	    expect(clientSrc).toMatch(/sample records/);
   338	    expect(clientSrc).toMatch(/unknown or missing/);
   339	    // No lab-words on any public surface. The predecessor guard banned the
   340	    // WHOLE WORD `simulated`, and an earlier version of this rebound tooth
   341	    // narrowed it to `simulated: true` — which would have let the bare word
   342	    // back onto a shipping surface. Caught by diffing old assertions against
   343	    // new (claim-F attack, 2026-07-27); the original breadth is restored, and
   344	    // this comment exists so the next narrowing has to be deliberate.
   345	    for (const src of [clientSrc, dropSrc]) {
   346	      expect(src).not.toMatch(/\bsimulated\b/i);
   347	      expect(src).not.toMatch(/\bsynthetic\b/i);
   348	    }
   349	  });
   350	
   351	  it("an uploaded run cannot inherit the committed corpus's honesty label", () => {
   352	    // The C3/C10 labels describe the RUN. When the records are the reader's
   353	    // own, the report must not claim synthetic-controlled matching or a
   354	    // simulated corpus — see lib/packs/listings/run.ts (slice 1) and the
   355	    // provenance suite. This tooth pins that the workbench actually passes a
   356	    // catalog through rather than falling back to the compiled-in one.
   357	    expect(clientSrc).toMatch(/verifyAcpFeed\(parsedFeed\.feed, catalog\)/);
   358	    expect(clientSrc).toMatch(/readerRecords/);
   359	  });
   360	});
     1	/**
     2	 * Playground seam teeth (owner commission 2026-07-13) — the tests that make the
     3	 * /playground page's claims TRUE rather than asserted:
     4	 *
     5	 * 1. GOLDEN EQUALITY — the browser seam (components/playground/verify-in-browser.ts),
     6	 *    fed the committed drifted sample feed, reproduces the committed golden
     7	 *    fixtures/synthetic-restaurant/expected-report.acp.json BYTE-FOR-BYTE. This is
     8	 *    the exact equality the page claims on its face ("a committed test proves…").
     9	 * 2. THE GATE BITES — mutate one price in a clone of the sample feed and the
    10	 *    report changes (so #1 is not a vacuous comparison).
    11	 * 3. BROWSER SAFETY — walk the seam's transitive import graph (relative + "@/",
    12	 *    .ts/.tsx/.json) and assert no module reaches a node: builtin — the proof the
    13	 *    engine actually runs client-side rather than needing a server.
    14	 * 4. HONESTY LABELS — the page + client source carry the simulated framing, the
    15	 *    non-affiliation predicate, and the no-AI/no-network wording; the noscript
    16	 *    fallback exists and cites the committed golden's real tally.
    17	 */
    18	import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
    19	import { readFileSync, existsSync, statSync } from "node:fs";
    20	import { dirname, join, resolve } from "node:path";
    21	import {
    22	  SAMPLE_FEED,
    23	  parseAcpFeedText,
    24	  sampleFeedText,
    25	  verifyAcpFeed,
    26	} from "../../components/playground/verify-in-browser.ts";
    27	import { serializeReport } from "../../lib/verifier-core/verify.ts";
    28	import type { AcpFeed } from "../../lib/packs/listings/acp-feed.ts";
    29	
    30	const root = resolve(__dirname, "..", "..");
    31	const golden = readFileSync(
    32	  join(root, "fixtures", "synthetic-restaurant", "expected-report.acp.json"),
    33	  "utf8",
    34	);
    35	
    36	describe("playground golden equality (the page's central claim)", () => {
    37	  it("the browser seam reproduces the committed golden byte-for-byte for the sample feed", () => {
    38	    const report = verifyAcpFeed(SAMPLE_FEED);
    39	    expect(serializeReport(report)).toBe(golden);
    40	  });
    41	
    42	  it("the equality gate bites: a one-price mutation changes the report", () => {
    43	    const mutated = JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(SAMPLE_FEED)) as AcpFeed;
    44	    const first = mutated.items[0] as { price: unknown };
    45	    first.price = "999.99";
    46	    const report = verifyAcpFeed(mutated);
    47	    expect(serializeReport(report)).not.toBe(golden);
    48	  });
    49	
    50	  it("round-trips through the paste path: sampleFeedText → parse → verify == golden", () => {
    51	    const parsed = parseAcpFeedText(sampleFeedText());
    52	    expect(parsed.ok).toBe(true);
    53	    if (parsed.ok) {
    54	      expect(serializeReport(verifyAcpFeed(parsed.feed))).toBe(golden);
    55	    }
    56	  });
    57	
    58	  it("malformed input yields an honest error, never a verdict", () => {
    59	    expect(parseAcpFeedText("").ok).toBe(false);
    60	    expect(parseAcpFeedText("not json {").ok).toBe(false);
    61	    expect(parseAcpFeedText("[1,2,3]").ok).toBe(false);
    62	    expect(parseAcpFeedText('{"no_items": true}').ok).toBe(false);
    63	    const bad = parseAcpFeedText('{"no_items": true}');
    64	    if (!bad.ok) expect(bad.error).toMatch(/items/);
    65	  });
    66	
    67	  it("structurally broken rows are rejected with the row named (batch-F P2 fix)", () => {
    68	    // a null row would throw inside the adapter's property access
    69	    const nullRow = parseAcpFeedText('{"items":[null]}');
    70	    expect(nullRow.ok).toBe(false);
    71	    if (!nullRow.ok) expect(nullRow.error).toMatch(/items\[0\]/);
    72	    // an array row is not an object
    73	    expect(parseAcpFeedText('{"items":[[1]]}').ok).toBe(false);
    74	    // a row without a string item_id cannot name its findings
    75	    const noId = parseAcpFeedText('{"items":[{"title":"x"}]}');
    76	    expect(noId.ok).toBe(false);
    77	    if (!noId.ok) expect(noId.error).toMatch(/item_id/);
    78	    const numericId = parseAcpFeedText('{"items":[{"item_id":42}]}');
    79	    expect(numericId.ok).toBe(false);
    80	  });
    81	
    82	  it("wrong or missing FIELD VALUES still verify (findings, not errors)", () => {
    83	    // shape is readable (object + string item_id) — the engine's job is to
    84	    // report the drift, not to reject the row.
    85	    const sparse = parseAcpFeedText('{"items":[{"item_id":"sku-1","title":123}]}');
    86	    expect(sparse.ok).toBe(true);
    87	    if (sparse.ok) {
    88	      const report = verifyAcpFeed(sparse.feed);
    89	      expect(report.ok).toBe(false);
    90	      expect(report.findings.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
    91	    }
    92	  });
    93	});
    94	
    95	/**
    96	 * Browser-safety closure walk — FAIL-CLOSED (batch-F P2 fix: the first cut's
    97	 * single regex missed dynamic import(), require(), and import-equals, and
    98	 * silently skipped unresolved specifiers — a fail-open "proof". This version:
    99	 * matches every import form, checks the COMPLETE Node builtin list, fails on
   100	 * any unresolved or non-allowlisted specifier, and proves its own matcher
   101	 * bites on planted hidden forms.)
   102	 */
   103	import { builtinModules } from "node:module";
   104	
   105	const IMPORT_FORMS: readonly RegExp[] = [
   106	  // import ... from "x" / export ... from "x"
   107	  /(?:import|export)\s[^"'`]*?from\s*["']([^"']+)["']/g,
   108	  // side-effect import "x"
   109	  /import\s*["']([^"']+)["']/g,
   110	  // dynamic import("x")
   111	  /import\s*\(\s*["']([^"']+)["']\s*\)/g,
   112	  // require("x") — incl. TS import-equals `import x = require("x")`
   113	  /require\s*\(\s*["']([^"']+)["']\s*\)/g,
   114	];
   115	
   116	function extractSpecifiers(src: string): string[] {
   117	  const specs: string[] = [];
   118	  for (const re of IMPORT_FORMS) {
   119	    for (const m of src.matchAll(re)) specs.push(m[1]);
   120	  }
   121	  return specs;
   122	}
   123	
   124	/** Bare specifiers the browser bundle legitimately provides. Everything else fails. */
   125	// "@number-flow/react": vetted ADOPT-WITH-CONDITIONS 2026-07-16 (MIT, exact-pinned
   126	// 0.6.1; zero network/hooks verified in the repo AND the published tarballs incl.
   127	// the esm-env transitive; reduced-motion/ARIA floors verified) — first real use is
   128	// the /fees paste-leg tally. The covering batch re-probes the whole condition set.
   129	// "react-dom": the shipped FeesView.tsx imports { flushSync } from it (a client-only
   130	// React DOM API for the SSR-then-page tab flush) — browser-provided, same bundle as the
   131	// already-trusted "react-dom/" prefix; no node builtin is reachable through it.
   132	const BARE_ALLOWLIST = new Set(["react", "react-dom", "@number-flow/react"]);
   133	const BARE_PREFIX_ALLOWLIST = ["react/", "next/", "react-dom/"];
   134	const NODE_BUILTINS = new Set([...builtinModules, ...builtinModules.map((m) => `node:${m}`)]);
   135	
   136	describe("playground browser safety (fail-closed import-graph walk)", () => {
   137	  function resolveSpec(fromFile: string, spec: string): string | null {
   138	    let base: string;
   139	    if (spec.startsWith("@/")) base = join(root, spec.slice(2));
   140	    else if (spec.startsWith(".")) base = resolve(dirname(fromFile), spec);
   141	    else return null;
   142	    // FILE candidates only (fee-surface extension 2026-07-16): a bare `base`
   143	    // hit can be a DIRECTORY (a barrel import like "@/lib/packs/fees"), which
   144	    // the old order returned and then failed to read — resolve barrels to
   145	    // their index.ts and never return a directory.
   146	    for (const cand of [`${base}.ts`, `${base}.tsx`, `${base}.json`, join(base, "index.ts"), base]) {
   147	      if (existsSync(cand) && statSync(cand).isFile()) return cand;
   148	    }
   149	    return null;
   150	  }
   151	
   152	  function walkClosure(entries: string[]): { seen: Set<string>; offenders: string[] } {
   153	    const seen = new Set<string>();
   154	    const offenders: string[] = [];
   155	    function walk(file: string) {
   156	      if (seen.has(file)) return;
   157	      seen.add(file);
   158	      if (file.endsWith(".json")) return;
   159	      const src = readFileSync(file, "utf8");
   160	      // Batch P2 fix (2026-07-16): a dynamic import()/require() whose specifier
   161	      // is NOT a single quoted literal (template literal, variable, or a
   162	      // quoted-prefix CONCATENATION like require("node:" + x)) would slip the
   163	      // extraction regexes — count call sites vs FULL-literal calls and fail
   164	      // closed on any gap.
   165	      const dynCalls = (src.match(/\bimport\s*\(/g) ?? []).length;
   166	      const dynLiterals = (src.match(/\bimport\s*\(\s*["'][^"']*["']\s*\)/g) ?? []).length;
   167	      if (dynCalls > dynLiterals) {
   168	        offenders.push(`${file} contains a dynamic import with a non-literal specifier — fail closed`);
   169	      }
   170	      const reqCalls = (src.match(/\brequire\s*\(/g) ?? []).length;
   171	      const reqLiterals = (src.match(/\brequire\s*\(\s*["'][^"']*["']\s*\)/g) ?? []).length;
   172	      if (reqCalls > reqLiterals) {
   173	        offenders.push(`${file} contains a require() with a non-literal specifier — fail closed`);
   174	      }
   175	      for (const spec of extractSpecifiers(src)) {
   176	        if (NODE_BUILTINS.has(spec)) {
   177	          offenders.push(`${file} imports the Node builtin "${spec}"`);
   178	          continue;
   179	        }
   180	        if (spec.startsWith("@/") || spec.startsWith(".")) {
   181	          const resolved = resolveSpec(file, spec);
   182	          if (resolved === null) {
   183	            offenders.push(`${file} imports "${spec}" which did not resolve — fail closed`);
   184	            continue;
   185	          }
   186	          walk(resolved);
   187	          continue;
   188	        }
   189	        // bare specifier: allowlisted or a failure — never silently skipped
   190	        if (BARE_ALLOWLIST.has(spec) || BARE_PREFIX_ALLOWLIST.some((p) => spec.startsWith(p))) {
   191	          continue;
   192	        }
   193	        offenders.push(`${file} imports non-allowlisted bare specifier "${spec}" — fail closed`);
   194	      }
   195	    }
   196	    for (const e of entries) walk(e);
   197	    return { seen, offenders };
   198	  }
   199	
   200	  it("the seam's transitive closure reaches no Node builtin and nothing unresolved", () => {
   201	    const { seen, offenders } = walkClosure([
   202	      join(root, "components", "playground", "verify-in-browser.ts"),
   203	      join(root, "components", "playground", "PlaygroundClient.tsx"),
   204	      // Fee surface (NYC showcase N1+N2, 2026-07-16): the fee seam + clients
   205	      // run in the same browser closure — same fail-closed proof.
   206	      join(root, "components", "fees", "audit-in-browser.ts"),
   207	      join(root, "components", "fees", "FeePlaygroundClient.tsx"),
   208	      join(root, "components", "fees", "FeesView.tsx"),
   209	      join(root, "components", "fees", "fee-report-data.ts"),
   210	    ]);
   211	    expect(offenders, offenders.join("\n")).toEqual([]);
   212	    // Sanity: the walk actually traversed the engine, not just the seam.
   213	    expect(seen.size).toBeGreaterThan(8);
   214	  });
   215	
   216	  it("the scanner itself bites: every hidden import form is detected", () => {
   217	    const hidden = [
   218	      'void import("node:fs")',
   219	      'const fs = require("node:fs")',
   220	      'import fs = require("node:fs")',
   221	      'export { x } from "node:crypto"',
   222	      'import "fs"',
   223	      'import { join } from "path"',
   224	    ];
   225	    for (const sample of hidden) {
   226	      const specs = extractSpecifiers(sample);
   227	      const caught = specs.some((s) => NODE_BUILTINS.has(s));
   228	      expect(caught, `scanner missed: ${sample} (extracted: ${JSON.stringify(specs)})`).toBe(true);
   229	    }
   230	    // and a non-allowlisted bare package would fail rather than be skipped
   231	    const bare = extractSpecifiers('import x from "left-pad"');
   232	    expect(bare).toContain("left-pad");
   233	    expect(BARE_ALLOWLIST.has("left-pad")).toBe(false);
   234	  });
   235	
   236	  it("the non-literal-specifier catch bites (template-literal / variable imports)", () => {
   237	    // Mirrors the walker's FULL decision: a planted non-literal form is caught
   238	    // either by the call-site counting gap, or by its extracted quoted PREFIX
   239	    // failing the bare-specifier allowlist (fail-closed both ways).
   240	    const caughtByWalkRules = (planted: string): boolean => {
   241	      const dynCalls = (planted.match(/\bimport\s*\(/g) ?? []).length;
   242	      const dynLiterals = (planted.match(/\bimport\s*\(\s*["'][^"']*["']\s*\)/g) ?? []).length;
   243	      const reqCalls = (planted.match(/\brequire\s*\(/g) ?? []).length;
   244	      const reqLiterals = (planted.match(/\brequire\s*\(\s*["'][^"']*["']\s*\)/g) ?? []).length;
   245	      if (dynCalls > dynLiterals || reqCalls > reqLiterals) return true;
   246	      return extractSpecifiers(planted).some(
   247	        (s) =>
   248	          !s.startsWith("@/") &&
   249	          !s.startsWith(".") &&
   250	          !NODE_BUILTINS.has(s) &&
   251	          !BARE_ALLOWLIST.has(s) &&
   252	          !BARE_PREFIX_ALLOWLIST.some((p) => s.startsWith(p)),
   253	      );
   254	    };
   255	    for (const planted of [
   256	      "void import(`./mods/${name}`)",
   257	      "const m = import(modPath)",
   258	      'const fs = require("node:" + name)',
   259	      "const r = require(pathVar)",
   260	    ]) {
   261	      expect(caughtByWalkRules(planted), `non-literal specifier slipped the catch: ${planted}`).toBe(
   262	        true,
   263	      );
   264	    }
   265	  });
   266	});
   267

     1	import { readFileSync, readdirSync, existsSync, statSync } from "node:fs";
     2	import { join, relative } from "node:path";

     5	import {
     6	  CORPUS_AS_OF,
     7	  CORPUS_SEED,
     8	  UCP_PINNED_VERSION,
     9	  buildUcpSearchResponse,
    10	  generateCatalog,

    14	
    15	/**
    16	 * C10 honesty surface (plan §4 C10; P3-6 W1 gate advisory) — machine-checked:
    17	 *  (a) NO real-platform-access / "no-AI" claim appears in pack sources, the CLI,
    18	 *      or the fixture READMEs (an affirmative-overclaim grep-gate);
    19	 *  (b) the UCP-touching artifacts carry an explicit simulated/honesty label;
    20	 *  (c) every conformance report pins the spec version and the simulated flag.
    21	 *
    22	 * The banned patterns match ONLY affirmative overclaims — honest negations
    23	 * ("no real platform access", "not recorded from any real marketplace") are not
    24	 * caught, which is why the sources that make those honest disclaimers pass.
    25	 */
    26	
    27	const root = process.cwd();
    28	
    29	function packSources(): string[] {

   132	const BANNED_CLAIMS: readonly RegExp[] = [
   133	  /\bno AI was used\b/i,
   134	  /\bAI built this\b/i,
   135	  /\bbuilt (?:entirely )?by AI\b/i,
   136	  /\breal[- ](?:time )?(?:DoorDash|Square|Uber\s?Eats|Grubhub)\s+(?:data|access|feed|account|integration|API)/i,
   137	  /\b(?:connected|integrated) to (?:DoorDash|Square|Uber|Grubhub|the (?:live )?marketplace)\b/i,
   138	  /\bactual (?:DoorDash|Square|marketplace|merchant) (?:data|account|orders?)\b/i,
   139	  /\bproduction (?:merchant|platform|marketplace) data\b/i,
   140	  /\bwe have (?:real |live )?(?:platform |marketplace )?access\b/i,
   141	];
   142	
   143	// F-08: one ISOLATED planted claim per banned pattern (never combined under a
   144	// single `some()`), so a pattern that silently stopped matching is caught on its
   145	// own — a combined probe would stay green as long as ANY one pattern still bit.
   146	const BANNED_PROBES: ReadonlyArray<readonly [RegExp, string]> = [
   147	  [BANNED_CLAIMS[0], "no AI was used to build this"],
   148	  [BANNED_CLAIMS[1], "AI built this dashboard"],
   149	  [BANNED_CLAIMS[2], "this was built entirely by AI"],
   150	  [BANNED_CLAIMS[3], "the page shows real-time DoorDash data"],
   151	  [BANNED_CLAIMS[4], "the app is connected to DoorDash"],
   152	  [BANNED_CLAIMS[5], "displays actual DoorDash orders"],
   153	  [BANNED_CLAIMS[6], "renders production platform data"],
   154	  [BANNED_CLAIMS[7], "we have live platform access"],
   155	];
