   157	describe("C10 platform-claims grep-gate (no real-platform-access claims)", () => {
   158	  it.each(scannedFiles)("%s makes no real-platform-access / no-AI claim", (file) => {
   159	    const text = readFileSync(file, "utf8");
   160	    for (const pattern of BANNED_CLAIMS) {
   161	      const m = text.match(pattern);
   162	      expect(m === null, `banned claim ${pattern} in ${file}: ${m?.[0]}`).toBe(true);
   163	    }
   164	  });
   165	
   166	  it("every banned pattern has exactly one dedicated probe (kept in lockstep)", () => {
   167	    expect(BANNED_PROBES.length).toBe(BANNED_CLAIMS.length);
   168	    BANNED_PROBES.forEach(([p], i) => expect(p).toBe(BANNED_CLAIMS[i]));
   169	  });
   170	
   171	  it.each(BANNED_PROBES)(
   172	    "the gate bites in isolation: %s matches its own planted claim",
   173	    (pattern, planted) => {
   174	      expect(pattern.test(planted), `pattern ${pattern} failed to catch "${planted}"`).toBe(true);
   175	    },
   176	  );
   177	});

    94	
    95	/**
    96	 * Browser-safety closure walk — FAIL-CLOSED (batch-F P2 fix: the first cut's
    97	 * single regex missed dynamic import(), require(), and import-equals, and
    98	 * silently skipped unresolved specifiers — a fail-open "proof". This version:
    99	 * matches every import form, checks the COMPLETE Node builtin list, fails on
   100	 * any unresolved or non-allowlisted specifier, and proves its own matcher
   101	 * bites on planted hidden forms.)
   102	 */
   103	import { builtinModules } from "node:module";
   104	
   105	const IMPORT_FORMS: readonly RegExp[] = [
   106	  // import ... from "x" / export ... from "x"
   107	  /(?:import|export)\s[^"'`]*?from\s*["']([^"']+)["']/g,
   108	  // side-effect import "x"
   109	  /import\s*["']([^"']+)["']/g,
   110	  // dynamic import("x")
   111	  /import\s*\(\s*["']([^"']+)["']\s*\)/g,
   112	  // require("x") — incl. TS import-equals `import x = require("x")`
   113	  /require\s*\(\s*["']([^"']+)["']\s*\)/g,
   114	];
   115	
   116	function extractSpecifiers(src: string): string[] {
   117	  const specs: string[] = [];
   118	  for (const re of IMPORT_FORMS) {
   119	    for (const m of src.matchAll(re)) specs.push(m[1]);
   120	  }
   121	  return specs;
   122	}
   123	
   124	/** Bare specifiers the browser bundle legitimately provides. Everything else fails. */
   125	// "@number-flow/react": vetted ADOPT-WITH-CONDITIONS 2026-07-16 (MIT, exact-pinned
   126	// 0.6.1; zero network/hooks verified in the repo AND the published tarballs incl.
   127	// the esm-env transitive; reduced-motion/ARIA floors verified) — first real use is
   128	// the /fees paste-leg tally. The covering batch re-probes the whole condition set.
   129	// "react-dom": the shipped FeesView.tsx imports { flushSync } from it (a client-only
   130	// React DOM API for the SSR-then-page tab flush) — browser-provided, same bundle as the
   131	// already-trusted "react-dom/" prefix; no node builtin is reachable through it.
   132	const BARE_ALLOWLIST = new Set(["react", "react-dom", "@number-flow/react"]);
   133	const BARE_PREFIX_ALLOWLIST = ["react/", "next/", "react-dom/"];
   134	const NODE_BUILTINS = new Set([...builtinModules, ...builtinModules.map((m) => `node:${m}`)]);
   135	
   136	describe("playground browser safety (fail-closed import-graph walk)", () => {
   137	  function resolveSpec(fromFile: string, spec: string): string | null {
   138	    let base: string;
   139	    if (spec.startsWith("@/")) base = join(root, spec.slice(2));
   140	    else if (spec.startsWith(".")) base = resolve(dirname(fromFile), spec);
   141	    else return null;
   142	    // FILE candidates only (fee-surface extension 2026-07-16): a bare `base`
   143	    // hit can be a DIRECTORY (a barrel import like "@/lib/packs/fees"), which
   144	    // the old order returned and then failed to read — resolve barrels to
   145	    // their index.ts and never return a directory.
   146	    for (const cand of [`${base}.ts`, `${base}.tsx`, `${base}.json`, join(base, "index.ts"), base]) {
   147	      if (existsSync(cand) && statSync(cand).isFile()) return cand;
   148	    }
   149	    return null;
   150	  }
   151	
   152	  function walkClosure(entries: string[]): { seen: Set<string>; offenders: string[] } {
   153	    const seen = new Set<string>();
   154	    const offenders: string[] = [];
   155	    function walk(file: string) {
   156	      if (seen.has(file)) return;
   157	      seen.add(file);
   158	      if (file.endsWith(".json")) return;
   159	      const src = readFileSync(file, "utf8");
   160	      // Batch P2 fix (2026-07-16): a dynamic import()/require() whose specifier
   161	      // is NOT a single quoted literal (template literal, variable, or a
   162	      // quoted-prefix CONCATENATION like require("node:" + x)) would slip the
   163	      // extraction regexes — count call sites vs FULL-literal calls and fail
   164	      // closed on any gap.
   165	      const dynCalls = (src.match(/\bimport\s*\(/g) ?? []).length;
   166	      const dynLiterals = (src.match(/\bimport\s*\(\s*["'][^"']*["']\s*\)/g) ?? []).length;
   167	      if (dynCalls > dynLiterals) {
   168	        offenders.push(`${file} contains a dynamic import with a non-literal specifier — fail closed`);
   169	      }
   170	      const reqCalls = (src.match(/\brequire\s*\(/g) ?? []).length;
   171	      const reqLiterals = (src.match(/\brequire\s*\(\s*["'][^"']*["']\s*\)/g) ?? []).length;
   172	      if (reqCalls > reqLiterals) {
   173	        offenders.push(`${file} contains a require() with a non-literal specifier — fail closed`);
   174	      }
   175	      for (const spec of extractSpecifiers(src)) {
   176	        if (NODE_BUILTINS.has(spec)) {
   177	          offenders.push(`${file} imports the Node builtin "${spec}"`);
   178	          continue;
   179	        }
   180	        if (spec.startsWith("@/") || spec.startsWith(".")) {
   181	          const resolved = resolveSpec(file, spec);
   182	          if (resolved === null) {
   183	            offenders.push(`${file} imports "${spec}" which did not resolve — fail closed`);
   184	            continue;
   185	          }
   186	          walk(resolved);
   187	          continue;
   188	        }
   189	        // bare specifier: allowlisted or a failure — never silently skipped
   190	        if (BARE_ALLOWLIST.has(spec) || BARE_PREFIX_ALLOWLIST.some((p) => spec.startsWith(p))) {
   191	          continue;
   192	        }
   193	        offenders.push(`${file} imports non-allowlisted bare specifier "${spec}" — fail closed`);
   194	      }
   195	    }
   196	    for (const e of entries) walk(e);
   197	    return { seen, offenders };
   198	  }
   199	
   200	  it("the seam's transitive closure reaches no Node builtin and nothing unresolved", () => {
   201	    const { seen, offenders } = walkClosure([
   202	      join(root, "components", "playground", "verify-in-browser.ts"),
   203	      // The upload surface (2026-07-27) joins the same closure: a file input
   204	      // is only zero-network if what reads it is. FileReader never touches a
   205	      // network, and this walk proves nothing in the workbench's import graph
   206	      // can either — the page's "nothing leaves this page" promise is
   207	      // structural, not a courtesy.
   208	      join(root, "components", "playground", "AuditWorkbench.tsx"),
   209	      join(root, "components", "playground", "FileDrop.tsx"),
   210	      // Fee surface (NYC showcase N1+N2, 2026-07-16): the fee seam + clients
   211	      // run in the same browser closure — same fail-closed proof.
   212	      join(root, "components", "fees", "audit-in-browser.ts"),
   213	      join(root, "components", "fees", "FeePlaygroundClient.tsx"),
   214	      join(root, "components", "fees", "FeesView.tsx"),
   215	      join(root, "components", "fees", "fee-report-data.ts"),
   216	    ]);
   217	    expect(offenders, offenders.join("\n")).toEqual([]);
   218	    // Sanity: the walk actually traversed the engine, not just the seam.
   219	    expect(seen.size).toBeGreaterThan(8);
   220	  });
   221	
   222	  it("the scanner itself bites: every hidden import form is detected", () => {
   223	    const hidden = [
   224	      'void import("node:fs")',
   225	      'const fs = require("node:fs")',
   226	      'import fs = require("node:fs")',
   227	      'export { x } from "node:crypto"',
   228	      'import "fs"',
   229	      'import { join } from "path"',
   230	    ];
   231	    for (const sample of hidden) {
   232	      const specs = extractSpecifiers(sample);
   233	      const caught = specs.some((s) => NODE_BUILTINS.has(s));
   234	      expect(caught, `scanner missed: ${sample} (extracted: ${JSON.stringify(specs)})`).toBe(true);
   235	    }
   236	    // and a non-allowlisted bare package would fail rather than be skipped
   237	    const bare = extractSpecifiers('import x from "left-pad"');
   238	    expect(bare).toContain("left-pad");
   239	    expect(BARE_ALLOWLIST.has("left-pad")).toBe(false);
   240	  });
   241	
   242	  it("the non-literal-specifier catch bites (template-literal / variable imports)", () => {
   243	    // Mirrors the walker's FULL decision: a planted non-literal form is caught
   244	    // either by the call-site counting gap, or by its extracted quoted PREFIX
   245	    // failing the bare-specifier allowlist (fail-closed both ways).
   246	    const caughtByWalkRules = (planted: string): boolean => {
   247	      const dynCalls = (planted.match(/\bimport\s*\(/g) ?? []).length;
   248	      const dynLiterals = (planted.match(/\bimport\s*\(\s*["'][^"']*["']\s*\)/g) ?? []).length;
   249	      const reqCalls = (planted.match(/\brequire\s*\(/g) ?? []).length;
   250	      const reqLiterals = (planted.match(/\brequire\s*\(\s*["'][^"']*["']\s*\)/g) ?? []).length;
   251	      if (dynCalls > dynLiterals || reqCalls > reqLiterals) return true;
   252	      return extractSpecifiers(planted).some(
   253	        (s) =>
   254	          !s.startsWith("@/") &&
   255	          !s.startsWith(".") &&
   256	          !NODE_BUILTINS.has(s) &&
   257	          !BARE_ALLOWLIST.has(s) &&
   258	          !BARE_PREFIX_ALLOWLIST.some((p) => s.startsWith(p)),
   259	      );
   260	    };
   261	    for (const planted of [
   262	      "void import(`./mods/${name}`)",
   263	      "const m = import(modPath)",
   264	      'const fs = require("node:" + name)',
   265	      "const r = require(pathVar)",
   266	    ]) {
   267	      expect(caughtByWalkRules(planted), `non-literal specifier slipped the catch: ${planted}`).toBe(
   268	        true,
   269	      );
   270	    }
   271	  });
   272	});
   273	
   274	describe("playground honesty labels (de-jargon Slice E — disclaimer-free + honest)", () => {
   275	  const pageSrc = readFileSync(join(root, "app", "playground", "page.tsx"), "utf8");
   276	  // REBOUND 2026-07-27 (the upload commission). These guards used to read
   277	  // components/playground/PlaygroundClient.tsx — the one-field paste leg. That
   278	  // component was DELETED when /playground became the two-file workbench, so
   279	  // the guards now read the surface that actually ships. Every invariant below
   280	  // is preserved verbatim; only the file they point at changed. A honesty
   281	  // guard aimed at a component the page no longer renders is worse than no
   282	  // guard: it reports green over an unexamined surface.

   286	  );

   351	  it("an uploaded run cannot inherit the committed corpus's honesty label", () => {

   136	const BANNED_CLAIMS: readonly RegExp[] = [
   137	  /\bno AI was used\b/i,
   138	  /\bAI built this\b/i,
   139	  /\bbuilt (?:entirely )?by AI\b/i,
   140	  /\breal[- ](?:time )?(?:DoorDash|Square|Uber\s?Eats|Grubhub)\s+(?:data|access|feed|account|integration|API)/i,
   141	  /\b(?:connected|integrated) to (?:DoorDash|Square|Uber|Grubhub|the (?:live )?marketplace)\b/i,
   142	  /\bactual (?:DoorDash|Square|marketplace|merchant) (?:data|account|orders?)\b/i,
   143	  /\bproduction (?:merchant|platform|marketplace) data\b/i,
   144	  /\bwe have (?:real |live )?(?:platform |marketplace )?access\b/i,
   145	];
   146	
   147	// F-08: one ISOLATED planted claim per banned pattern (never combined under a
   148	// single `some()`), so a pattern that silently stopped matching is caught on its
   149	// own — a combined probe would stay green as long as ANY one pattern still bit.
   150	const BANNED_PROBES: ReadonlyArray<readonly [RegExp, string]> = [
   151	  [BANNED_CLAIMS[0], "no AI was used to build this"],
   152	  [BANNED_CLAIMS[1], "AI built this dashboard"],
   153	  [BANNED_CLAIMS[2], "this was built entirely by AI"],
   154	  [BANNED_CLAIMS[3], "the page shows real-time DoorDash data"],
   155	  [BANNED_CLAIMS[4], "the app is connected to DoorDash"],
   156	  [BANNED_CLAIMS[5], "displays actual DoorDash orders"],
   157	  [BANNED_CLAIMS[6], "renders production platform data"],
   158	  [BANNED_CLAIMS[7], "we have live platform access"],
   159	];
   160	
   161	describe("C10 platform-claims grep-gate (no real-platform-access claims)", () => {
   162	  it.each(scannedFiles)("%s makes no real-platform-access / no-AI claim", (file) => {
   163	    const text = readFileSync(file, "utf8");
   164	    for (const pattern of BANNED_CLAIMS) {
   165	      const m = text.match(pattern);
   166	      expect(m === null, `banned claim ${pattern} in ${file}: ${m?.[0]}`).toBe(true);
   167	    }
   168	  });
   169	
   170	  it("every banned pattern has exactly one dedicated probe (kept in lockstep)", () => {
   171	    expect(BANNED_PROBES.length).toBe(BANNED_CLAIMS.length);
   172	    BANNED_PROBES.forEach(([p], i) => expect(p).toBe(BANNED_CLAIMS[i]));
   173	  });
   174	
   175	  it.each(BANNED_PROBES)(
   176	    "the gate bites in isolation: %s matches its own planted claim",
   177	    (pattern, planted) => {
   178	      expect(pattern.test(planted), `pattern ${pattern} failed to catch "${planted}"`).toBe(true);
   179	    },
   180	  );
     1	import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
     2	import {
     3	  acpFeedToClaims,
     4	  buildFaithfulFeed,
     5	  generateCatalog,
     6	  runListingsVerification,
     7	} from "@/lib/packs/listings";
     8	
     9	/**
    10	 * SLICE 1 — the report's provenance labels must describe the RUN, not the
    11	 * engine's default (owner commission 2026-07-27, "I want a working website
    12	 * where I will upload test files").
    13	 *
    14	 * WHY THIS EXISTS. `runListingsVerification` used to hardcode
    15	 * `matchingMode: "synthetic-controlled"` and `simulated: true` into every
    16	 * report it built. That was TRUE for every caller that existed at the time —
    17	 * all of them fed the committed synthetic catalog — so nothing was wrong on
    18	 * the shipped surfaces. It becomes FALSE the moment a reader uploads their own
    19	 * catalog: the engine would stamp a reader's real records `simulated: true`
    20	 * and assert shared-synthetic-ID matching it never performed.
    21	 *
    22	 * That is a RULES §4 defect of exactly the class this project keeps catching —
    23	 * a claim broader than the thing backing it (state doc, session 34: four
    24	 * occurrences; session 35: two more). The label is not decoration: C10 pins
    25	 * `simulated` as an honesty surface, and C3 requires every report to say how
    26	 * matching was performed. A label that cannot vary cannot be honest about a
    27	 * run that varies.
    28	 *
    29	 * THE FIX SHAPE. The provenance becomes an optional third parameter that
    30	 * DEFAULTS to today's exact values, so every one of the ~40 existing call
    31	 * sites keeps its byte-identical report (proven by the untouched golden and
    32	 * drift-lock suites). Only a caller that KNOWS the data is the reader's own
    33	 * passes the honest alternative.
    34	 *
    35	 * Note on `simulated`: the engine reads only `sor.items` and `sor.asOf` from a
    36	 * catalog (reference.ts:38,64) — the `simulated` field on SyntheticCatalog is
    37	 * a pure honesty label and never a computation input, so varying it cannot
    38	 * change a single finding. This suite pins that too (T4): identical inputs
    39	 * produce identical FINDINGS under either label. The labels describe the run;
    40	 * they never steer it.
    41	 */
    42	
    43	const AS_OF = "2026-07-03T00:00:00Z";
    44	const SEED = 4242;
    45	
    46	describe("T1: the default is byte-identical to the pre-slice behaviour", () => {
    47	  it("a two-argument call still reports synthetic-controlled + simulated:true", () => {
    48	    const sor = generateCatalog(SEED, AS_OF);
    49	    const report = runListingsVerification(acpFeedToClaims(buildFaithfulFeed(sor)), sor);
    50	
    51	    expect(report.matchingMode).toBe("synthetic-controlled");
    52	    expect(report.simulated).toBe(true);
    53	  });
    54	});
    55	
    56	describe("T2: a reader-supplied run can label itself honestly", () => {
    57	  it("real-world matching + simulated:false reach the report header", () => {
    58	    const sor = generateCatalog(SEED, AS_OF);
    59	    const report = runListingsVerification(acpFeedToClaims(buildFaithfulFeed(sor)), sor, {
    60	      matchingMode: "real-world",
    61	      simulated: false,
    62	    });
    63	
    64	    expect(report.matchingMode).toBe("real-world");
    65	    expect(report.simulated).toBe(false);
    66	  });
    67	
    68	  it("each label moves independently (a partial provenance is still honest)", () => {
    69	    const sor = generateCatalog(SEED, AS_OF);
    70	
    71	    // Reader's own feed, but checked against OUR committed catalog: the data
    72	    // is still synthetic on the truth side, and matching is still by shared id.
    73	    const halfWay = runListingsVerification(acpFeedToClaims(buildFaithfulFeed(sor)), sor, {
    74	      simulated: false,
    75	    });
    76	    expect(halfWay.simulated).toBe(false);
    77	    expect(halfWay.matchingMode).toBe("synthetic-controlled");
    78	
    79	    const otherHalf = runListingsVerification(acpFeedToClaims(buildFaithfulFeed(sor)), sor, {
    80	      matchingMode: "real-world",
    81	    });
    82	    expect(otherHalf.simulated).toBe(true);
    83	    expect(otherHalf.matchingMode).toBe("real-world");
    84	  });
    85	});
    86	
    87	describe("T3: the spec version is NOT reader-overridable", () => {
    88	  it("the pinned rule-table version survives any provenance the caller passes", () => {
    89	    const sor = generateCatalog(SEED, AS_OF);
    90	    const base = runListingsVerification(acpFeedToClaims(buildFaithfulFeed(sor)), sor);
    91	    const labelled = runListingsVerification(acpFeedToClaims(buildFaithfulFeed(sor)), sor, {
    92	      matchingMode: "real-world",
    93	      simulated: false,
    94	    });
    95	
    96	    // C10 pins the rule-table version into every report header. A caller may
    97	    // describe WHOSE data it ran on; it may never restate WHICH RULES ran.
    98	    expect(labelled.specVersion).toBe(base.specVersion);
    99	  });
   100	});
   101	
   102	describe("T4: labels describe the run, they never steer it", () => {
   103	  it("findings are identical under either provenance (drifted input)", () => {
   104	    const sor = generateCatalog(SEED, AS_OF);
   105	    const faithful = buildFaithfulFeed(sor);
   106	    // Plant a lie so there is a non-empty finding set to compare.
   107	    // ACP serves prices as decimal STRINGS; the drift is a real disagreement
   108	    // with the record, not a type error.
   109	    const drifted = {
   110	      ...faithful,
   111	      items: faithful.items.map((r, i) => (i === 0 ? { ...r, price: "9999.99" } : r)),
   112	    };
   113	
   114	    const asSynthetic = runListingsVerification(acpFeedToClaims(drifted), sor);
   115	    const asReal = runListingsVerification(acpFeedToClaims(drifted), sor, {
   116	      matchingMode: "real-world",
   117	      simulated: false,
   118	    });
   119	
   120	    expect(asReal.findings.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
   121	    expect(JSON.stringify(asReal.findings)).toBe(JSON.stringify(asSynthetic.findings));
   122	    expect(asReal.ok).toBe(asSynthetic.ok);
   123	  });
   124	});

   173	    const asOf = match.value.catalog.asOf;
   174	    const actual = String(claim.value);
   175	    if (actual >= asOf) return []; // ISO-8601 strings compare lexicographically.
   176	    return [
   177	      {
   178	        claim,
   179	        referenceRowId: match.referenceRowId,
   180	        ruleId: "LST-STALE-EXPIRED",
   181	        severity: "warn",
   182	        category: "staleness",
   183	        plainLine: `This row expired ${actual} but is still served (catalog as-of ${asOf}).`,
   184	      },
   185	    ];
   186	  },
   187	
   188	  // staleness + conditional — pre_order rows must carry a future availability_date.
   189	  (claim, match) => {
   190	    if (claim.field !== "availability_date" || !match || !isSorTruth(match.value)) return [];
   191	    const asOf = match.value.catalog.asOf;
   192	    const base = { claim, referenceRowId: match.referenceRowId } as const;
   193	    if (claim.value === null) {
   194	      return [
   195	        {
   196	          ...base,
   197	          ruleId: "LST-XF-PREORDER-DATE-MISSING",
   198	          severity: "error",
   199	          category: "cross-field-invariant",
   200	          plainLine: "The row is pre_order but carries no availability_date (required-if conditional violated).",
   201	        },
   202	      ];
   203	    }
   204	    const actual = String(claim.value);
   205	    if (actual >= asOf) return [];
   206	    return [
   207	      {
   208	        ...base,
   209	        ruleId: "LST-STALE-AVAILDATE",
   210	        severity: "warn",
   211	        category: "staleness",
   212	        plainLine: `The pre-order availability date ${actual} already passed (catalog as-of ${asOf}) — the promise is stale.`,
   213	      },
   214	    ];
   215	  },
   216	
   217	  // identity — the variant label must identify THIS variation.
   218	  (claim, match) => {
   219	    if (claim.field !== "variant_dict" || !match || !isSorTruth(match.value)) return [];
   220	    const dictValue = claim.value as { readonly dict?: Readonly<Record<string, string>> };
   221	    const label = dictValue.dict?.["variation"];
   222	    const expected = match.value.variation.name;
   223	    if (label === expected) return [];
   224	    return [
   225	      {
   226	        claim,
   227	        referenceRowId: match.referenceRowId,
   228	        ruleId: "LST-IDENT-MODIFIER-AMBIG",
   229	        severity: "error",
   230	        category: "identity",
   231	        plainLine: `The variant label says "${label ?? "(none)"}" but this row is the "${expected}" variation — variants of this item can no longer be told apart.`,
   232	      },
   233	    ];
   234	  },
   235	
   236	  // cross-field — extracted ACP invariants (sale_price ≤ price is a PRICE-class drift per §7).
   237	  (claim, match) => {
   238	    if (claim.field !== "invariants" || !match) return [];
   239	    const v = claim.value as AcpInvariantsValue;
   240	    const out: FindingInput[] = [];
   241	    const base = { claim, referenceRowId: match.referenceRowId } as const;
   242	    if (v.sale_price !== undefined && Number(v.sale_price) > Number(v.price)) {
   243	      out.push({
   244	        ...base,
   245	        ruleId: "LST-PRICE-SALE-GT",
   246	        severity: "error",
   247	        category: "price",
   248	        plainLine: `The sale price ${v.sale_price} is higher than the regular price ${v.price} — a "sale" that costs more.`,
   249	      });
   250	    }
   251	    if (v.is_eligible_checkout && !v.is_eligible_search) {
   252	      out.push({
   253	        ...base,
   254	        ruleId: "LST-XF-CHECKOUT-SEARCH",
   255	        severity: "error",
   256	        category: "cross-field-invariant",
   257	        plainLine: "The row is checkout-eligible but not search-eligible — the extracted invariant requires checkout ⇒ search.",
   258	      });
   259	    }
   260	    return out;
   261	  },
   262	
   263	  // spec-version skew — the responder's supported versions must include the pin.
   264	  (claim, match) => {
   265	    if (claim.field !== "spec.supported_versions" || !match) return [];
   266	    const pinned = (match.value as { readonly pinnedSpecVersion: string }).pinnedSpecVersion;
   267	    const versions = claim.value as readonly string[];
   268	    if (versions.includes(pinned)) return [];
   269	    return [
   270	      {
     1	/**
     2	 * Surface adapters — W1 (C3: "one comparator, two adapters").
     3	 *
     4	 * Each adapter normalizes a serving surface into the SAME claim language
     5	 * (field paths + value forms), so the comparator and detectors are truly
     6	 * surface-agnostic: the ACP static feed and the constructed UCP catalog
     7	 * response produce comparable claims, and the C3 differential test can assert
     8	 * the same drift is caught from both.
     9	 *
    10	 * Claim id convention: `<servingRowId>#<fieldPath>`. Row-level context needed by
    11	 * cross-field rules travels on a dedicated `invariants` claim; group-level
    12	 * variant ambiguity travels on `variant_dict` claims.
    13	 *
    14	 * Plain: two translators turn "what the feed says" and "what the live answer
    15	 * says" into the same list of checkable statements, so one referee can judge
    16	 * both.
    17	 */
    18	import type { Claim } from "../../verifier-core/index.ts";
    19	import type { AcpFeed, AcpFeedItem } from "./acp-feed.ts";
    20	import type { UcpCatalogResponseFixture } from "./ucp.ts";
    21	
    22	/** Cross-field tuple carried by the per-row `invariants` claim (ACP surface). */
    23	export interface AcpInvariantsValue {
    24	  readonly price: string;
    25	  readonly sale_price?: string;
    26	  readonly is_eligible_search: boolean;
    27	  readonly is_eligible_checkout: boolean;
    28	}
    29	
    30	function rowClaims(
    31	  source: Claim["source"],
    32	  rowId: string,
    33	  fields: readonly (readonly [string, unknown])[],
    34	): Claim[] {
    35	  return fields.map(([field, value]) => ({
    36	    id: `${rowId}#${field}`,
    37	    source,
    38	    field,
    39	    value,
    40	  }));
    41	}
    42	
    43	/** Normalize an ACP feed into claims. */
    44	export function acpFeedToClaims(feed: AcpFeed): readonly Claim[] {
    45	  return feed.items.flatMap((r: AcpFeedItem) => {
    46	    const invariants: AcpInvariantsValue = {
    47	      price: r.price,
    48	      ...(r.sale_price !== undefined ? { sale_price: r.sale_price } : {}),
    49	      is_eligible_search: r.is_eligible_search,
    50	      is_eligible_checkout: r.is_eligible_checkout,
    51	    };
    52	    return rowClaims("acp-feed", r.item_id, [
    53	      ["existence", true],
    54	      ["title", r.title],
    55	      ["price.amount", r.price],
    56	      ["price.currency", r.currency],
    57	      ["availability", r.availability],
    58	      ["variant_dict", { group: r.group_id, dict: r.variant_dict }],
    59	      ...(r.expiration_date !== undefined
    60	        ? ([["expiration_date", r.expiration_date]] as const)
    61	        : []),
    62	      ...(r.availability === "pre_order"
    63	        ? ([["availability_date", r.availability_date ?? null]] as const)
    64	        : []),
    65	      ["invariants", invariants],
    66	    ]);
    67	  });
    68	}
    69	
    70	/**
    71	 * Normalize a (constructed) UCP catalog response into claims. Availability
    72	 * vocabulary is normalized to the shared enum (interpretation, documented:
    73	 * available → in_stock, unavailable → out_of_stock). The response-level
    74	 * `supported_versions` becomes a catalog-meta claim (§7 spec-version skew).
    75	 */
    76	export function ucpResponseToClaims(resp: UcpCatalogResponseFixture): readonly Claim[] {
    77	  const meta: Claim = {
    78	    id: "catalog#spec.supported_versions",
    79	    source: "ucp-catalog",
    80	    field: "spec.supported_versions",
    81	    value: resp.supported_versions,
    82	  };
    83	  const perItem = resp.items.flatMap((it) =>
    84	    rowClaims("ucp-catalog", it.id, [
    85	      ["existence", true],
    86	      ["title", it.title],
    87	      ["price.amount", it.price.amount],
    88	      ["price.currency", it.price.currency],
    89	      ["availability", it.availability === "available" ? "in_stock" : "out_of_stock"],
    90	      ["variant_dict", { group: it.group_id, dict: it.variant }],
    91	    ]),
    92	  );
    93	  return [meta, ...perItem];
    94	}

    42	  readonly description: string;
    43	  readonly url: string;
    44	  readonly brand: string;
    45	  readonly image_url: string;

    65	  readonly sale_price?: string;

    75	}

    77	/** Integer cents → decimal-string dollars ("1899" → "18.99"). */
    78	export function centsToDecimal(cents: number): string {
    79	  return (cents / 100).toFixed(2);
    80	}
    81	
    82	/**
    83	 * Build the FAITHFUL feed: one row per non-hidden SOR variation, sharing the SOR
    84	 * variation id as item_id (synthetic-controlled matching, C3). Mapping choices
    85	 * (documented interpretations): hidden variations are excluded from the feed;
    86	 * soldout_86 maps to out_of_stock; every row is search- and checkout-eligible
    87	 * with the conditional seller policy fields therefore present.
    88	 */
    89	export function buildFaithfulFeed(sor: SyntheticCatalog): AcpFeed {
    90	  const sellerUrl = "https://example.com/curbside-commons-simulated";
    91	  const items = sor.items.flatMap((item) =>
    92	    item.variations
    93	      .filter((v) => v.stock !== "hidden")
    94	      .map((v): AcpFeedItem => {
    95	        const variantDict: Record<string, string> = { variation: v.name };
    96	        return {
    97	          item_id: v.id,
    98	          title: item.variations.length > 1 ? `${item.name} (${v.name})` : item.name,
    99	          description: item.description,
   100	          url: `${sellerUrl}/items/${item.id}`,
   101	          brand: sor.merchantName,
   102	          image_url: `${sellerUrl}/img/${item.id}.png`,
   103	          price: centsToDecimal(v.priceCents),
   104	          currency: sor.currency,
   105	          availability: v.stock === "soldout_86" ? "out_of_stock" : "in_stock",
   106	          seller_name: sor.merchantName,
   107	          seller_url: sellerUrl,
   108	          is_eligible_search: true,
   109	          is_eligible_checkout: true,
   110	          target_countries: ["US"],
   111	          store_country: "US",
   112	          group_id: item.id,
   113	          variant_dict: variantDict,
   114	          seller_privacy_policy: `${sellerUrl}/privacy`,
   115	          seller_tos: `${sellerUrl}/tos`,
   116	          updated_at: sor.asOf,
   117	        };
   118	      }),
   119	  );
   120	  return { simulated: true, spec: "acp-product-feed/extract-2026-07-02", items };
   121	}

     2	 * Report model — W0 STUB (plan §3 "the report IS a document", S-9 / C4 / C10).
     3	 *
     4	 * The verifier's output: one page, evidence-cited, spec-version pinned. W0 fixes
     5	 * the machine-readable shape; rendering (web + machine JSON) lands in W3. No
     6	 * report-building logic here.

     8	 * Plain: the one-page result — every catch with its receipts, stamped with the
     9	 * spec version it was checked against, and labeled if anything is simulated.

    11	import type { Finding } from "./evidence.ts";
    12	import type { MatchingMode } from "./reference.ts";
    13	
    14	/** Machine-readable verifier report (CI-usable, C1). */
    15	export interface VerifierReport {
    16	  /** Spec / rule-table version pinned in the header of every report (C10). */
    17	  readonly specVersion: string;
    18	  /**
    19	   * C3: every report labels whether claim↔reference matching was
    20	   * synthetic-controlled (shared IDs) or real-world (entity resolution).
    21	   * Required by construction so an unlabeled report cannot exist.
    22	   */
    23	  readonly matchingMode: MatchingMode;
    24	  /** True if any synthetic artifact is involved — honesty surface (C10). */
    25	  readonly simulated: boolean;
    26	  /** All evidence-cited findings; an empty array means no drift detected. */
    27	  readonly findings: readonly Finding[];
    28	  /** Overall pass/fail; the CLI derives its non-zero exit from this (C1). */
    29	  readonly ok: boolean;
    30	}
     1	/**
     2	 * Verification engine — W1 (plan §3, §5 W1; criteria C1/C2/C3).
     3	 *
     4	 * The generic half of "one engine, two packs": walk claims, resolve each against
     5	 * the swappable Reference, run pack-provided detectors, enforce the C2 evidence
     6	 * guard on every finding, sort deterministically, and assemble the report.
     7	 * The engine knows NO domain taxonomy — packs own the detectors and any
     8	 * completeness/unmatched logic; the engine owns evidence discipline, determinism,
     9	 * and report assembly. Zero LLM, zero network, zero clock reads (any "now" must
    10	 * arrive as data so identical inputs give byte-identical reports).
    11	 *
    12	 * Plain: the referee's rulebook-independent machinery — take each claim, look up
    13	 * the truth, ask the domain rules what's wrong, demand receipts for every catch,
    14	 * and write the report the same way every time.
    15	 */
    16	import type { Claim } from "./claim.ts";
    17	import type { Reference, ReferenceMatch, MatchingMode } from "./reference.ts";
    18	import type { Finding } from "./evidence.ts";
    19	import { makeFinding, type FindingInput } from "./guard.ts";
    20	import type { VerifierReport } from "./report.ts";
    21	
    22	/**
    23	 * A pack-provided detection rule. Runs on every (claim, match) pair; returns
    24	 * finding inputs (usually 0 or 1). `match` is null when the reference could not
    25	 * resolve the claim — unmatched handling is also a pack decision.
    26	 */
    27	export type Detector = (claim: Claim, match: ReferenceMatch | null) => readonly FindingInput[];
    28	
    29	/** Options for a verification run — all report-level facts arrive as data. */
    30	export interface VerifyOptions {
    31	  /** Spec / rule-table / taxonomy version pinned into the report header (C10). */
    32	  readonly specVersion: string;
    33	  /** How claim↔reference matching was performed (C3 label). */
    34	  readonly matchingMode: MatchingMode;
    35	  /** True when any synthetic artifact is involved (C10 honesty surface). */
    36	  readonly simulated: boolean;
    37	}
    38	
    39	/**
    40	 * Deterministic finding order: category, then ruleId, then claim id. Stable
    41	 * output ordering is what makes golden-report byte-comparison possible.
    42	 */
    43	export function sortFindings(findings: readonly Finding[]): readonly Finding[] {
    44	  return [...findings].sort(
    45	    (a, b) =>
    46	      (a.category ?? "").localeCompare(b.category ?? "") ||
    47	      a.ruleId.localeCompare(b.ruleId) ||
    48	      a.claim.id.localeCompare(b.claim.id),
    49	  );
    50	}
    51	
    52	/**
    53	 * Run detectors over claims against a reference. Every emitted finding passes
    54	 * through {@link makeFinding} (C2: no finding without all four receipts).
    55	 */
    56	export function verifyClaims(
    57	  claims: readonly Claim[],
    58	  reference: Reference,
    59	  detectors: readonly Detector[],
    60	): readonly Finding[] {
    61	  const findings: Finding[] = [];
    62	  for (const claim of claims) {
    63	    const match = reference.resolve(claim);
    64	    for (const detector of detectors) {
    65	      for (const input of detector(claim, match)) {
    66	        findings.push(makeFinding(input));
    67	      }
    68	    }
    69	  }
    70	  return findings;
    71	}
    72	
    73	/**
    74	 * Assemble the machine-readable report (C1-consumable). Re-asserts C2 on every
    75	 * finding (belt-and-suspenders for findings built outside verifyClaims, e.g. a
    76	 * pack's completeness sweep), sorts deterministically, and derives `ok`.
    77	 */
    78	export function buildReport(
    79	  findings: readonly Finding[],
    80	  opts: VerifyOptions,
    81	): VerifierReport {
    82	  const validated = findings.map((f) => makeFinding(f));
    83	  const sorted = sortFindings(validated);
    84	  return Object.freeze({
    85	    specVersion: opts.specVersion,
    86	    matchingMode: opts.matchingMode,
    87	    simulated: opts.simulated,
    88	    findings: sorted,
    89	    ok: sorted.length === 0,
    90	  });
    91	}
    92	
    93	/**
    94	 * Canonical report serialization — the single stringifier every consumer (CLI,
    95	 * golden fixtures, tests) uses, so byte-identity is meaningful.
    96	 */
    97	export function serializeReport(report: VerifierReport): string {
    98	  return `${JSON.stringify(report, null, 2)}\n`;
    99	}

    16	 *     `@ai-sdk`, `groq|gemini`. Verified bypasses: `node:net`, `node:tls`,
    17	 *     `node:dgram`, `node:worker_threads`, `node:child_process` — none of which
    18	 *     require a new dependency. A denylist of egress-capable modules can never
    19	 *     be complete; every new Node release can add another.
    20	 *  2. **The source scan matched one spelling of `fetch`.** `(^|[^.\w])fetch\s*\(`
    21	 *     rejects `fetch(`, but passes `globalThis.fetch(`, `(0,fetch)(`, and
    22	 *     `const f = globalThis.fetch; f(`.
    23	 *  3. **The specifier extractor only saw STRING LITERALS.** `import(mod)` and
    24	 *     `import("node:" + "net")` are invisible to it — so a dynamic import is
    25	 *     both unbanned and unwalked, hiding whole subtrees.
    26	 *
    27	 * THE FIX IS A SHAPE CHANGE, not more patterns: **the module check is now an
    28	 * ALLOWLIST.** The CLI path legitimately uses exactly five Node builtins
    29	 * (`crypto`, `fs`, `os`, `path`, `url`, plus their `/promises` variants).
    30	 * Anything else is a failure *by default*, so a module nobody has thought of yet
    31	 * — including one that ships in a future Node — is caught the day it appears.
    32	 * That is the property a denylist structurally cannot have.
    33	 *
    34	 * Non-literal dynamic imports are now a HARD FAILURE rather than a silent skip:
    35	 * an import the walker cannot resolve is an import it cannot police, and on a
    36	 * surface whose entire claim is "structurally enforced", unanalyzable is not
    37	 * acceptable. There is no legitimate need for one on these paths.
    38	 *
    39	 * Extracted here so all ten import-walk suites share ONE implementation. Ten
    40	 * copies of a subtly-wrong walker is how a hole survives in nine places after
    41	 * being fixed in one.
    42	 */
    43	import { existsSync, readFileSync } from "node:fs";
    44	import { join } from "node:path";
    45	
    46	/**
    47	 * Node builtins permitted on a `$0`, offline path. Deliberately minimal: these
    48	 * are the five the engine actually uses. ADDING ONE IS A REVIEWED DECISION —
    49	 * `node:net`, `node:tls`, `node:dgram`, `node:http*`, `node:worker_threads`, and
    50	 * `node:child_process` are all egress- or execution-capable and must never
    51	 * appear here without an accompanying record.
    52	 */
    53	export const ALLOWED_NODE_BUILTINS: readonly string[] = [
    54	  "node:crypto",
    55	  "node:fs",
    56	  "node:fs/promises",
    57	  "node:os",
    58	  "node:path",
    59	  "node:url",
    60	  "node:util",
    61	  "node:buffer",
    62	  "node:assert",
    63	  "node:stream",
    64	  "node:string_decoder",
    65	  "node:events",
    66	  "node:timers",
    67	  "node:process",
    68	];
    69	
    70	/**
    71	 * Third-party packages permitted on the offline paths — deterministic,
    72	 * non-networking libraries the engine genuinely depends on.
    73	 */
    74	export const ALLOWED_BARE_PACKAGES: readonly string[] = ["ajv", "ajv-formats"];
    75	
    76	/** Every spelling of a network call the source scan must reject. */
    77	const NETWORK_CALL_PATTERNS: ReadonlyArray<readonly [RegExp, string]> = [
    78	  [/(^|[^.\w])fetch\s*\(/, "bare fetch("],
    79	  [/\bglobalThis\s*\.\s*fetch\b/, "globalThis.fetch"],
    80	  [/\bglobal\s*\.\s*fetch\b/, "global.fetch"],
    81	  [/\bwindow\s*\.\s*fetch\b/, "window.fetch"],
    82	  [/\(\s*0\s*,\s*fetch\s*\)/, "(0,fetch) indirect call"],
    83	  [/\bnew\s+XMLHttpRequest\b/, "XMLHttpRequest"],
    84	  [/\bnew\s+WebSocket\b/, "WebSocket"],
    85	  [/\bnavigator\s*\.\s*sendBeacon\b/, "navigator.sendBeacon"],
    86	  [/\bnew\s+EventSource\b/, "EventSource"],
    87	];
    88	
    89	export interface Violation {
    90	  readonly file: string;
    91	  readonly kind: "module" | "network-call" | "unanalyzable-import";
    92	  readonly detail: string;
    93	}
    94	
    95	export interface WalkOptions {
    96	  /** Repo root, for resolving `@/...` aliases. */
    97	  readonly root: string;
    98	  /** Extra bare packages this surface may legitimately import. */
    99	  readonly allowPackages?: readonly string[];
   100	  /** Extra node builtins this surface may legitimately import. */
   101	  readonly allowBuiltins?: readonly string[];
   102	}
   103	
   104	/**
   105	 * Extract import specifiers AND flag non-literal dynamic imports.
   106	 *
   107	 * The old extractor matched only quoted specifiers, so `import(mod)` produced no
   108	 * match and was silently skipped — unbanned and unwalked. Here a dynamic import
   109	 * whose argument is not a string literal is reported as a violation.
   110	 */
   111	export function extractImports(text: string): { specs: string[]; unanalyzable: string[] } {
   112	  const specs: string[] = [];
   113	  const unanalyzable: string[] = [];
   114	  const literal = /(?:from\s+|import\s*\(\s*|import\s+|require\s*\(\s*)["']([^"']+)["']/g;
   115	  for (let m = literal.exec(text); m; m = literal.exec(text)) specs.push(m[1]);
   116	  // A dynamic import/require whose first non-space argument is not a quote.
   117	  const dynamic = /\b(?:import|require)\s*\(\s*(?!["'\s)])([^)]{0,60})/g;
   118	  for (let m = dynamic.exec(text); m; m = dynamic.exec(text)) {
   119	    // `import.meta` and type-only `import(` in TS type positions are not calls.
   120	    if (/^meta\b/.test(m[1])) continue;
   121	    unanalyzable.push(m[1].trim().slice(0, 40));
   122	  }
   123	  return { specs, unanalyzable };
   124	}
   125	
   126	/** Resolve `@/...` and relative specifiers to a repo file, or null if bare. */
   127	export function resolveSpec(fromFile: string, spec: string, root: string): string | null {
   128	  let base: string | null = null;
   129	  if (spec.startsWith("@/")) base = join(root, spec.slice(2));
   130	  else if (spec.startsWith(".")) base = join(fromFile, "..", spec);
   131	  if (base === null) return null;
   132	  const candidates = [base, `${base}.ts`, `${base}.tsx`, `${base}.mjs`, `${base}.json`, join(base, "index.ts")];
   133	  return candidates.find((c) => existsSync(c) && /\.(ts|tsx|mjs|json)$/.test(c)) ?? null;
   134	}
   135	
   136	/**
   137	 * Walk every module reachable from `entry` and return all violations.
   138	 *
   139	 * ALLOWLIST semantics: a bare specifier (one that does not resolve to a repo
   140	 * file) must appear in the permitted builtins or packages, or it is a violation.
   141	 * Unknown-by-default is the whole point.
   142	 */
   143	export function walkImports(entry: string, opts: WalkOptions): { violations: Violation[]; seen: Set<string> } {
   144	  const builtins = new Set([...ALLOWED_NODE_BUILTINS, ...(opts.allowBuiltins ?? [])]);
   145	  const packages = new Set([...ALLOWED_BARE_PACKAGES, ...(opts.allowPackages ?? [])]);
   146	  const violations: Violation[] = [];
   147	  const seen = new Set<string>();
   148	  const queue = [entry];
   149	
   150	  while (queue.length > 0) {
   151	    const file = queue.pop() as string;
   152	    if (seen.has(file)) continue;
   153	    seen.add(file);
   154	    if (file.endsWith(".json")) continue; // data leaf
   155	
   156	    let text: string;
   157	    try {
   158	      text = readFileSync(file, "utf8");
   159	    } catch {
   160	      continue;
   161	    }
   162	
   163	    for (const [pattern, label] of NETWORK_CALL_PATTERNS) {
   164	      if (pattern.test(text)) violations.push({ file, kind: "network-call", detail: label });
   165	    }
   166	
   167	    const { specs, unanalyzable } = extractImports(text);
   168	    for (const u of unanalyzable) {
   169	      violations.push({
   170	        file,
   171	        kind: "unanalyzable-import",
   172	        detail: `non-literal dynamic import \`${u}\` — the walker cannot police what it cannot resolve`,
   173	      });
   174	    }
   175	    for (const spec of specs) {
   176	      const resolved = resolveSpec(file, spec, opts.root);
   177	      if (resolved !== null) {
   178	        queue.push(resolved);
   179	        continue;
   180	      }
   181	      // Bare specifier: must be explicitly permitted.
   182	      const normalized = spec.startsWith("node:") ? spec : `node:${spec}`;
   183	      const isBuiltin = builtins.has(spec) || builtins.has(normalized);
   184	      const isPackage = packages.has(spec) || [...packages].some((p) => spec === p || spec.startsWith(`${p}/`));
   185	      if (!isBuiltin && !isPackage) {
   186	        violations.push({
   187	          file,
   188	          kind: "module",
   189	          detail: `"${spec}" is not on the offline allowlist (adding one is a reviewed decision)`,
   190	        });
   191	      }
   192	    }
   193	  }
   194	  return { violations, seen };
   195	}

    71	/**
    72	 * Open a slot's paste box and fill it — the keyboard/no-drag route.
    73	 * Idempotent: clicking the <summary> TOGGLES, so a second call on the same
    74	 * slot would close what the first opened. Open only when it is shut.
    75	 */
    76	async function fillSlot(page: import("@playwright/test").Page, label: string, text: string) {
    77	  const slot = page.locator(".fd-slot", { hasText: label });
    78	  const details = slot.locator("details.fd-paste");
    79	  if (!(await details.evaluate((d: HTMLDetailsElement) => d.open))) {
    80	    await slot.getByText("or paste it as text").click();
    81	  }
    82	  await slot.locator("textarea").fill(text);
    83	}

codex
The cross-file attack has landed several independent honesty and correctness failures, including one deeper than the three already fixed: real-world title resolution currently emits only an ID-mismatch and never reruns price/availability/hidden-item checks against the resolved record. I’m doing the final deduplication now so the output contains only distinct, reproducible findings and precise fixes.
2026-07-27T18:58:56.845455Z ERROR codex_models_manager::manager: failed to renew cache TTL: missing field `supports_reasoning_summaries` at line 86 column 5
codex
1. **P1 — `components/playground/verify-in-browser.ts:155-162`** — Load `SAMPLE_FEED` and a reader catalog. The report sets `simulated:false` solely because the catalog differs, although the feed is explicitly synthetic (`:54-57`). C10 says true when *any* synthetic artifact participated. Minimal fix: derive `simulated` from both feed and catalog provenance; add sample-feed + reader-catalog coverage.

2. **P1 — `components/playground/verify-in-browser.ts:160`; `lib/packs/listings/reference.ts:67-72`** — A reader feed and catalog sharing `sku-1` are matched by direct ID lookup, but the report says `real-world`; `lib/packs/listings/report-view.ts:76-79` decodes that as “identifiers do not line up” and “resolution” occurred. Minimal fix: derive C3 from the matching actually performed, with a mixed mode if both direct-ID and resolution paths occur; ownership must be separate.

3. **P1 — `components/playground/verify-in-browser.ts:179-190`** — Change only the sample catalog’s engine-read `asOf`; unchanged `items` still classify it as the committed corpus. Conversely, reorder identical sample items and `JSON.stringify` classifies the synthetic corpus as reader data. An unrelated catalog structurally identical to the fixture is also called synthetic. Minimal fix: carry explicit source provenance from the input action; use a canonical full engine-input fingerprint only for recognizing the exact sample, not for inferring ownership.

4. **P1 — `components/playground/AuditWorkbench.tsx:119-128,243-263`** — Clicking “Use the sample catalog” sets `readerRecords=true` merely because the record slot is nonempty. The UI then says “your own records” and “your upload” beside a report correctly labelled `synthetic-controlled`. Minimal fix: track `sample` versus `reader` as slot state and render from that single provenance source; add the missing sample-pair E2E assertion.

5. **P1 — `components/playground/verify-in-browser.ts:340-352`; `lib/packs/listings/detectors.ts:121-145`** — The parser silently invents `currency:"USD"`. Missing currency can therefore pass without reference backing, while an uploaded EUR catalog is rewritten to USD and produces a false mismatch against an EUR feed. Minimal fix: require and preserve currency; if only USD is supported, explicitly reject other values.

6. **P1 — `components/playground/verify-in-browser.ts:232-237,351`; `lib/packs/listings/detectors.ts:170-205`** — Any nonempty `asOf`, including `"not-a-date"` or noncanonical offset timestamps, is accepted and compared lexicographically. This can reverse chronological ordering and emit false or missed staleness findings. Minimal fix: validate timestamps as ISO-8601 instants and compare parsed epoch values for catalog and feed dates.

7. **P1 — `components/playground/verify-in-browser.ts:287-318`; `lib/packs/listings/adapters.ts:30-40`** — Variation IDs may contain the engine’s `#` delimiter or equal reserved ID `"catalog"`. Exact catalog/feed ID `sku#1` is truncated to `sku`, producing a false mismatch; `"catalog"` is treated as metadata and later reported missing. Minimal fix: carry row identity separately from claim IDs. Immediate containment: reject `#` and `"catalog"` in both catalog variation IDs and feed `item_id`.

8. **P1 — `lib/packs/listings/run.ts:43-70`; `lib/packs/listings/reference.ts:58-73`** — A legacy ID resolved by unique exact title gets only `LST-IDENT-ID-MISMATCH`; its price, availability, title and hidden-item claims are never rerun against the resolved truth. A hidden item served under a legacy ID can therefore escape the hidden-item detector. Minimal fix: after unique resolution, verify every grouped claim against the resolved `SorTruth`, then add the ID-mismatch finding.

9. **P1 — `lib/packs/listings/run.ts:49-82`** — Two catalog variations with the same expected title make a legacy-ID row fall into the same branch as zero matches. The report says “no such item exists” although two candidates exist, then reports both missing. Minimal fix: distinguish zero from multiple matches and emit an ambiguity finding naming candidate IDs.

10. **P2 — `components/playground/verify-in-browser.ts:83-85`; `components/playground/AuditWorkbench.tsx:266-273`** — User evidence is rewritten for display: a genuine item named “Simulated Burger” becomes “example Burger,” and “Synthetic Salad” becomes “illustrative Salad.” The downloaded report and visible verdict then disagree. Minimal fix: apply copy cleanup only to the committed demonstration, never reader-supplied runs or receipt text.

11. **P2 — `components/playground/FileDrop.tsx:79-89,120-125`; `components/playground/AuditWorkbench.tsx:73-78`** — Selecting a new file leaves the old verdict runnable and downloadable until asynchronous `FileReader` completion. Rapid selections can complete out of order, letting an older file overwrite the latest choice. Minimal fix: invalidate immediately on read start, disable Run while reading, and abort or generation-token obsolete reads; test delayed and reversed completions.

12. **P2 — `evals/packs/playground-golden.test.ts:152-219`** — The zero-network guard checks imports but not direct browser egress. Adding `fetch`, `sendBeacon`, `XMLHttpRequest`, `WebSocket`, `EventSource`, or a content-derived DOM URL requires no import and leaves it green. Minimal fix: use the existing scanner in `evals/lib/import-walk.ts:76-87`, extend it to relevant DOM sinks, and add a runtime no-request test or restrictive CSP.

13. **P2 — `components/playground/FileDrop.tsx:93-130`** — Both file inputs have the same accessible name, “Choose a file or drag it here.” “The feed” and “The record” are unassociated sibling paragraphs, so screen-reader control lists cannot distinguish the inputs. Minimal fix: use fieldsets/legends or unique `aria-labelledby` values and associate hints/statuses with `aria-describedby`.

14. **P2 — `components/playground/verify-in-browser.ts:293-297`** — `Number.isInteger` accepts unsafe integers. JSON `priceCents:9007199254740993` is silently rounded to `9007199254740992` before verification. Minimal fix: require `Number.isSafeInteger` and a documented nonnegative upper bound.

15. **P3 — `components/playground/verify-in-browser.ts:267-268`; `lib/packs/listings/reference.ts:27-46`** — An item without top-level `id` is refused even though indexing, expected-title construction, detectors and completeness do not read that field. This refutes “refuses only what the engine cannot index.” Minimal fix: remove the requirement or describe and test it as an additional catalog-schema policy.

16. **P3 — `evals/packs/playground-golden.test.ts:330-358`** — The deleted client’s guard distinguished the committed sample feed from a pasted feed. The rebound guard checks only record origin, and `AuditWorkbench` no longer retains feed origin. Minimal fix: explicitly retire that invariant or restore feed-origin state plus a rendered E2E assertion.

Claims A–F:

- **A survives substantively.** HEAD had 25 direct runner invocations plus eight downstream `verifyAcpFeed` invocations; existing callers use the old arity, and `lib/packs/listings/run.ts:148,155-156` reproduces the former defaults.
- **B does not survive.** Findings 1–4.
- **C survives.** `ListingsRunProvenance` excludes `specVersion`, and `lib/packs/listings/run.ts:153-156` always supplies `LISTINGS_SPEC_VERSION`; runtime extra properties are ignored.
- **D does not survive.** Findings 5–9 and 14–15. Duplicate variation IDs, empty catalogs, direct-ID hidden rows, and exact whitespace/Unicode identity now behave as claimed.
- **E does not survive as “by construction.”** Finding 12. The current components contain no actual exfiltration path, and user content is rendered as escaped React text; no file-content-to-HTML injection path was found.
- **F does not survive.** Finding 16, plus the untested contradictory sample-catalog UI in finding 4. The whole-word lab-term bans and C10 file rebinding themselves were preserved.
tokens used
2,27,892
1. **P1 — `components/playground/verify-in-browser.ts:155-162`** — Load `SAMPLE_FEED` and a reader catalog. The report sets `simulated:false` solely because the catalog differs, although the feed is explicitly synthetic (`:54-57`). C10 says true when *any* synthetic artifact participated. Minimal fix: derive `simulated` from both feed and catalog provenance; add sample-feed + reader-catalog coverage.

2. **P1 — `components/playground/verify-in-browser.ts:160`; `lib/packs/listings/reference.ts:67-72`** — A reader feed and catalog sharing `sku-1` are matched by direct ID lookup, but the report says `real-world`; `lib/packs/listings/report-view.ts:76-79` decodes that as “identifiers do not line up” and “resolution” occurred. Minimal fix: derive C3 from the matching actually performed, with a mixed mode if both direct-ID and resolution paths occur; ownership must be separate.

3. **P1 — `components/playground/verify-in-browser.ts:179-190`** — Change only the sample catalog’s engine-read `asOf`; unchanged `items` still classify it as the committed corpus. Conversely, reorder identical sample items and `JSON.stringify` classifies the synthetic corpus as reader data. An unrelated catalog structurally identical to the fixture is also called synthetic. Minimal fix: carry explicit source provenance from the input action; use a canonical full engine-input fingerprint only for recognizing the exact sample, not for inferring ownership.

4. **P1 — `components/playground/AuditWorkbench.tsx:119-128,243-263`** — Clicking “Use the sample catalog” sets `readerRecords=true` merely because the record slot is nonempty. Minimal fix: track `sample` versus `reader` as slot state and render from that single provenance source; add the missing sample-pair E2E assertion.
